# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## What this is

**Night City Messenger (NCM)** — a Foundry VTT v12 module for the Cyberpunk RED tabletop RPG. Module ID `night-city-messenger`. Provides in-character messaging, contact management, data shard mechanics, network simulation, and GM tooling. The local working directory IS the live Foundry module install; there is no separate build artifact.

Repo: https://github.com/christinophrappuccino/night-city-messenger

**Module id renamed 2026-09-10: `cyberpunkred-messenger` → `night-city-messenger` (folder must match).** v1.0 shipped under the old id, and a module id is the flags namespace on every document, the settings namespace, the socket name and the compendium pack ids — so the rename is a breaking change carried by a one-time migration, `scripts/core/NamespaceMigration.js`: `migrateClientSettings` (preInit/1, every client, localStorage copy) + `runNamespaceMigration` (ready/1, GM only, MUST stay the first ready task because later services cache world state): scans for legacy world settings + document flags across `MIGRATION_SCOPES` (actors+items, items, journal+pages, scenes+notes+tokens, users, macros, folders, messages), prompts the GM (raw `Dialog.confirm` — it runs before the admin-dialog hook exists), copies world settings (registered keys only, never clobbers), then per document copies the flag block → **verifies the read-back** → deletes the legacy block only when verified (mismatches keep their legacy flags and are listed), stamps the hidden setting `namespaceMigrationDone`, and calls `SettingsConfig.reloadConfirm({world:true})`. Declining doesn't stamp (asked again next load; module runs empty meanwhile). Legacy Setting documents are left in place (inert, rollback-safe). **`LEGACY_MODULE_ID` in constants.js is the ONLY place the old id may appear in module code** — a stray literal is a bug (the 2026-09-10 sweep rewrote 253 files; the stylesheet entry is now `styles/night-city-messenger.css`). The macro packs embed the id (`flags.<id>.managed`, pack keys) — regenerated by `node dev/tools/gen-macros.cjs`. For USERS the rename means Foundry sees a different module: uninstall the old one, install the new, load the world, accept the prompt (backup first). Sim: `dev/tools/sims/test-namespace-migration.mjs`.

## Workflow

- No `package.json`, no test runner, no build step. Foundry loads `scripts/main.js` directly via `module.json`'s `esmodules` field.
- **Repo layout (2026-10-23, for the public GitHub + Foundry listing):** everything a visitor doesn't need lives under **`dev/`** — `dev/tools/` (the Node scripts + `dev/tools/sims/` + the `*.macro.js` test seeds), `dev/mockups/` (design mocks + probes), `dev/design/` (the design documents; the gitignored rulebook PDFs sit there too), `dev/guide-export/` (the HTML/PDF export of the guide). `docs/` holds only `GETTING-STARTED.md`. Every dev tool resolves the repo root from its own location (`__dirname, '..', '..'` / `new URL('../../../', import.meta.url)` from a sim) — **never a hardcoded machine path**; `git grep cphan` must stay empty. Mockups keep `../../styles/…` links (same depth as before) and reach the CPR system stylesheet as `../../../../systems/cyberpunk-red-core/main.css` (relative to Data/), which works over `file://` AND through `dev/tools/serve-mockups.cjs`. `WORKFLOW.local.md` (root, gitignored via `*.local.md`) is the developer's own branch/release cheat-sheet.
- **Player Guide (2026-10-31): `docs/PLAYER-GUIDE.md` is its own source, spoiler-free (no traps, no GM windows; the sim greps), built by the same `build-guide.cjs` into `docs/NCM-Player-Guide.pdf`.** It opens through `scripts/utils/guides.js` `openGuide('player')` from the ? button in the player's inbox header (not the GM's: the GM header is already at its measured 860px budget) and the Identity drawer's footer. Player instructions live there; the GM guide's Part 2 only points to it.
- **The Getting Started guide has ONE source: `docs/GETTING-STARTED.md`.** `node dev/tools/build-guide.cjs` turns it into the styled print HTML (`dev/guide-export/NCM-Getting-Started.html`, GENERATED — never edit it; styles in `dev/guide-export/src/guide.css`) and prints `docs/NCM-Getting-Started.pdf` (ships in the zip; the Overview's Guide button opens it) plus a dev copy, with headless Chrome. Pages FLOW (every Part on a new page, figures / tips / table rows never split) — the old hand-laid fixed-height A4 pages clipped silently and drifted from the markdown. Figures are an invisible markdown comment `<!-- fig: file.png | caption | width% -->` (file in `dev/guide-export/img/`; a missing file stops the build); a paragraph starting `Tip:` becomes a tip box; "Three rules to remember" becomes rule cards. Rewritten 2026-10-29 for the admin redesign, access points, network nodes, sounds; new shots rendered from the real templates via the admin previews. Re-run it after editing the guide or bumping the version (the cover prints module.json's version).
- `dev/tools/` holds dev-only Node scripts (e.g. `gen-macros.cjs`) that Foundry never runs and that produce committed artifacts. These are authoring conveniences, **not** a build step — running them is never required to load the module.
- **Packaging: `node dev/tools/build-zip.cjs`** (zero deps; `--dry-run` lists, `--out <path>` redirects, default `dist/<id>-v<version>.zip`, `dist/` is gitignored) writes the distributable from the SAME allowlist as `.github/workflows/release.yml` and **refuses to run if the two lists drift** (it parses the workflow's `zip -r` block). Ships: module.json, LICENSE, README.md, **docs/** (the Getting Started guide — back in the zip since 2026-10-23 for the public release; it was Discord-only between 2026-10-21 and then), scripts/, styles/, templates/, assets/, languages/, the two `.db` packs. Never ships: **ALL of `dev/`** (tools, sims, mockups, design notes, the guide export, the gitignored rulebook PDFs), `.github/`, CLAUDE.md, INTEGRATION.md, `*.local.md`, the LevelDB pack folders — a FORBIDDEN list asserts none leak. **`scripts/tests/` must ship**: main.js + five `register*` files import the Phase suites statically (they only RUN under `debugMode`). On Windows it drives the built-in bsdtar (`C:\Windows\System32\tar.exe -a`); elsewhere `zip -r`. **Release model (2026-10-23):** `dev` is the working branch, `main` only moves at a release, and **a pushed `v*` tag on `main` IS a release** — release.yml stamps `version` + `manifest` (the stable `releases/latest/download/module.json` URL Foundry polls) + `download` (this tag's zip) into module.json, refuses if the tag and module.json disagree, zips, and publishes. The committed module.json carries the same three fields so installs from the manifest URL work and update. Never tag `dev`; never push a tag casually. The developer's cheat-sheet is `WORKFLOW.local.md` (gitignored).
- The developer pushes to GitHub then tests in live Foundry. **Do not commit unless explicitly asked.**
- All testing is manual in Foundry. There are no automated tests; "verify" means syntax-check + smoke-test in the browser.

## Verification

Before presenting changes, syntax-check JS and balance-check Handlebars:

```bash
node dev/tools/check-syntax.mjs            # every .js/.mjs in scripts/ + dev/tools/, in MODULE context
node dev/tools/check-syntax.mjs scripts    # or just one tree
```

> **`node --check path/to/file.js` IS NOT ENOUGH — it passes files Foundry cannot load.**
> With no `package.json` type, node parses a `.js` file as CommonJS/sloppy script, and
> constructs that are fatal in module (strict) context survive that parse. This bit on
> 2026-09-22: a template literal containing `'…ELSE\\'S…'` (a double-escaped apostrophe)
> passed `node --check` on every run, then failed at import with *"missing ) after
> argument list"* — the Player Authoring window simply refused to open, with a full-file
> sweep reporting "all parse". The same bytes as `.mjs` are caught immediately, which is
> all `check-syntax.mjs` does: copy to a temp `.mjs`, then `--check`. It is parse-only on
> purpose — an `import()`-based check would EXECUTE the several `dev/tools/` files that run a
> CLI on load. The isolated one-liner repro does NOT reproduce it; it needs the
> surrounding code that lets the script-mode parse succeed, so trust the tool, not a
> hand-built snippet.

For `.hbs` templates, count block opens vs closes:

```bash
opens=$(grep -oP '\{\{~?#(if|unless|each|with)' path/to/file.hbs | wc -l)
closes=$(grep -oP '\{\{~?/(if|unless|each|with)' path/to/file.hbs | wc -l)
[ "$opens" = "$closes" ] && echo BALANCED || echo "MISMATCH ($opens/$closes)"
```

If the grep flags a mismatch, scan for block tags inside `{{!-- --}}` doc comments first — they're false positives.

To verify the macro packs after editing `dev/tools/gen-macros.cjs`, regenerate — the generator self-validates (every line round-trips as JSON; every `command` parses as the async function Foundry runs it as; ids unique/well-formed):

```bash
node dev/tools/gen-macros.cjs
```

When diffing a regenerated pack against a prior copy, key by `_id`, **never** by line order — Foundry's NeDB compaction reorders the on-disk `.db` on world load (see Compendium packs).

**In-Foundry verification suites** live in `scripts/tests/` — `game.nightcity.verify()` plus `verifyPhase2()`…`verifyPhase6()` (all auto-run under `debugMode`). **Phase 6** (`Phase6Verification.js`, registered in `registerTerminalSystem` postReady/33) smoke-covers the terminal era: TerminalService/TraceService/resident method contracts, the security-era settings, the 9 player→machine socket relays, the workshop/deck launchers + terminal macro API, `PROGRAM_EFFECTS` registry shape + adjudicate fallback, the forge ladder's three rungs, operator helpers, and template loads. When adding a Phase-6-era surface, extend it — coverage previously stopped at Phase 5 while half the module was newer.

**Durable logic sims** live in `dev/tools/sims/*.mjs` (plain `node dev/tools/sims/test-X.mjs`, drive REAL module methods via `prototype.call` with mocked globals). Historically these sims were written in the session scratchpad and lost — the 100+ asserted checks cited in the design docs are unreproducible for that reason. **New sims go in `dev/tools/sims/`**, not the scratchpad.

## Architecture: how startup works

**Single entry point**: `scripts/main.js` instantiates a `ModuleInitializer` (`scripts/core/ModuleInitializer.js`), then nine `register*` files in `scripts/init/` each push tasks into one of four phases: `preInit`, `init`, `ready`, `postReady`. Tasks have a numeric priority (lower runs first; conventionally increments of 10). Foundry hooks (`Hooks.once('init')`, `Hooks.once('ready')`) drain the phases.

This means: **to add a new service or hook, add a `register(phase, priority, name, fn)` call in the appropriate `init/register*.js` file.** Don't bolt new `Hooks.once` calls into `main.js`.

## Architecture: services

Long-lived stateful objects in `scripts/services/` (MessageService, NetworkService, DataShardService, MasterContactService, SpamService, etc.) are constructed during the `init` phase and exposed on the `game.nightcity` namespace (e.g. `game.nightcity.messageService`). UI code accesses them via `BaseApplication` getters (`this.messageService`, `this.networkService`, etc.). Cross-service communication goes through `EventBus` (`scripts/core/EventBus.js`) using event names from the frozen `EVENTS` enum in `scripts/utils/constants.js`.

## Architecture: player send feedback — the relay ACK (2026-10-20)

Tester report: "sometimes my player failed to send a message, but we don't know if it's the signal, bad email or other thing." Root cause: the player path was **fire-and-forget** — `MessageService._relayMessage` emitted `MESSAGE_RELAY` and returned `success:true` immediately, so the composer played "Delivered" and closed; a GM-side failure (orphaned inbox, unresolvable anon reply) came back as a stray `ui.notifications.warn` with repository text, broadcast to **every** client. Rules now:
- **`_relayMessage` awaits the GM's `MESSAGE_DELIVERED`** for its `messageId` (`_awaitDeliveryAck`, `_pendingRelays` map, `RELAY_ACK_TIMEOUT_MS` 8s → `errorCode:'RELAY_TIMEOUT'`). The composer therefore shows the REAL outcome in its existing failure overlay (Retry / Queue / Save Draft) and stays open. The GM handler (`handleMessageRelay(data, senderUserId)`) stamps **`forUserId`** on the ack (SocketHandlers passes the socket `sender`), and `handleDeliveryConfirmation` ignores acks for other users; a late/unsolicited ack for ME falls back to a toast.
- **Errors are translated for the player in ONE place — `MessageService.playerFacingError(raw)`** ("Could not access inbox journal" → "…ask your GM to check Admin → Tools → Orphan Audit"); the raw text rides `rawError` + the console. **The GM is toasted too** (permanent warn naming sender → recipient + raw reason) — they are the only one who can fix it.
- **Queued ≠ Delivered.** `_relayMessage` returns `{success:true, queued:true, reason:'gm_offline'|'dead_zone'}`; the composer shows an amber **Message Queued** panel with the reason (`_playSendAnimation('success', {queued, reason})`) instead of "Delivered to X". **Dead zones now actually queue** (the Send button already read "Queue" but nothing enforced it, so the message relayed at once and arrived garbled at signal 0); the existing `NETWORK_CONNECTED` listener flushes the queue when signal returns. GM sends are unaffected (the GM composes from anywhere, incl. "send via").
- **`errors.general` is rendered** (block line under SUBJ, `.ncm-composer__field-error--general`) — the composer had set it for years for "needs a subject or body" and the template only printed `errors.to`, so an empty Send looked like a dead button.
- **Every `{ success:false }` return in MessageService / MessageRepository carries an `error`** — the sim asserts it, and asserts every `_errors.<key>` the composer sets has a `{{errors.<key>}}` in the template. Sim: `dev/tools/sims/test-send-feedback.mjs` (35 checks, real methods via prototype + a scripted two-client socket). **Not yet live-tested two-client.**

## Architecture: multi-recipient mail — fan-out, participants, Reply All (contact groups phase 1, 2026-10-20)

Groundwork for contact groups ("send to the whole crew"). **Before this the module had never delivered one message to two people**: the composer packed 2+ chips into an ARRAY `toActorId`, the service resolved a single recipient, and the send died with "Recipient not found".
- **Members are keyed by ADDRESS, ids are hints — `scripts/utils/participants.js`** (pure): `makeMember({email, name, kind, hint:{actorId, contactId}, role:'from'|'to'})`, `dedupeMembers`, `othersFor`, `selfKeys`, `memberKeys`, and the 4-step `resolveMember(member, world)`: **hint by id** (precise inside one world; reports `stale` when the document's address moved) → **address** (`verifyContact`-shaped: directory entry + linked actor, or an actor's email flag) → **name** (space/punctuation-insensitive) → unresolved (still sendable as a raw address). Why address-first: master-contact export is a bare JSON array re-imported with FRESH ids and de-duped by email, so an id-keyed member breaks the moment a group crosses worlds. `worldAdapter()` builds the adapter from Foundry globals; the sim drives it with two mocked worlds and proves an exported member re-resolves by address when every id differs.
- **`MessageService.sendMessage` is now: validate → sender → `_normalizeRecipients` → one or many.** `data.recipients` (array of `{actorId, contactId, email, name}`) wins; the legacy single fields and the legacy array `toActorId` still normalize. ONE → `_resolveRecipient` (the lifted recipient-resolution + send-only + verification block, async because of the correspondent rule) → `_stampOutgoing` (addresses, NPC auto-register, names, ids, thread, GM overrides, network/signal/timestamp) → deliver/relay, unchanged behaviour. MANY → **`_sendToMany`**: each recipient resolved + gated on its own (a refusal is REPORTED in `results`/`failed`, never a silent drop; all refused → `ALL_RECIPIENTS_FAILED` and nothing is written), the base stamped once (`to`/`toName` = the whole party), **`participants`** = sender (role `from`) + every recipient that passed, then N copies `{...base, toActorId, to, toName, _recipientInboxId}`. GM → `_deliverMany` (each copy through the normal `_deliverMessage(copy, {sentCopy:false})`, so spam filter / anon mask / terminal mirror / NPC toast / MESSAGE_NOTIFY all run **per copy**; then **ONE** sent copy via `_createSentCopy(base)`). Player → ONE relay carrying `_fanout: copies`; `handleMessageRelay` fans out and acks once with `delivered`/`failed`/`results`; the composer reports "Not delivered to 2 of 4 — who: why" and refunds the failed share of attached eddies (**eddies are debited × recipients** — every copy carries the amount). **Never add a second sent-copy writer.**
- **`participants` persists through all three flag builders** (`createMessage`, `_pageToMessage`, `_buildMessageFlags`) — the field-by-field rule. `_maskAnonForRecipient` strips the `role:'from'` member (truly hidden). `_recipientEmailFor` orders registered-flag → **directory address** → synthesized `name@nightcity.net` (the directory used to lose to the synthesized address when the linked actor had never run Email Setup).
- **Threading needed no redesign.** `counterparties(msg, viewingActorId, viewingKeys)` adds every participant's keys minus the viewer's own (actor id + address + directory id — `MessageViewerApp._threadOpts` passes `viewingKeys` via `selfKeys`), so two group threads with the same subject merge only when they share someone OTHER than me; a sent copy's joined `to` is split on commas. Pre-fan-out mail (no participants) threads exactly as before.
- **Reply All**: `buildReply(orig, fromActorId, body, {all:true})` → `reply.recipients` = `replyAllRecipients` (participants minus self by id/address/directory id/name; **empty on anonymous mail — the mask would leak — and on legacy mail**). Viewer: `reply-all-message` action + button beside Reply (both branches), gated by `canReplyAll` (≥2 others, not masked, not send-only); the compose launcher forwards `replyAll` (it whitelists options — add new ones there). Composer adds the recipients as removable chips. A `.ncm-viewer__detail-party` chip beside the To line shows the party size (hover = names).
- Sim: `dev/tools/sims/test-group-send.mjs` (56 checks — member record, the 4 steps + cross-world round trip, normalization, GM fan-out shape, partial failure, all-refused, the relay/ack round trip, anon strip, Reply All, threading over the party, validator, and the persistence/launcher/template drift guards). **Not live-tested.** Phases 2–3 (groups as data, GM push, admin + import/export) are designed in the `contact-groups` memory note.

## Architecture: contact groups (phase 2, 2026-10-20) — "send to the whole crew"

**A group is a named list of address-keyed members** (`{ id, name, color, members:[member], note, createdAt, updatedAt, sharedFrom }`, `sanitizeGroup` normalizes everything). Two homes, one service — **`scripts/services/ContactGroupService.js`** (`game.nightcity.contactGroupService`, BaseApplication getter):
- **Player groups** live on the character — actor flag **`contactGroups`** (the player owns the actor, so `saveActorGroup`/`deleteActorGroup` are their own writes; `isOwner` is checked). **GM groups** live in the world setting **`masterContactGroups`** (`saveMasterGroup`/`deleteMasterGroup`, GM-only). `groupsFor({actorId, isGM})` is what the composer searches: the from-character's own groups + the GM's when the GM composes. Factory Reset / the id migration need nothing (flag block + registered setting).
- **Members are built by `memberFromPlayerContact` / `memberFromMasterContact` / `memberFromActor`** and resolved live by `recipientsFor(group)` → `{recipients, unresolved}` (participants.js `resolveMember`): a dead id hint with no address is REPORTED by name, a bare address still goes as a raw recipient, duplicates collapse. `groupFromTag(actorId, tag)` is the "make a group from this tag" shortcut (unsaved).
- **Push (GM → player) — `pushGroupToPlayer(groupId, targetActorId)`:** every member the player lacks is shared first (directory entries via `pushToPlayer`, bare actors via `pushActorToPlayer`, both duplicate-safe; the target never receives itself), then the group lands on the character **as a COPY with its own id, tagged `sharedFrom:{groupId, at}`**; re-pushing REPLACES that copy. `sharedWith(groupId)` reads who holds one (via `getPlayerCharacters()` — rows are `{actorId, actorName, playerName, actor}`, NOT `{id, name}`; that shape bit this build once).
- **Export/import:** Admin → Contacts export now writes **`{ ncmMasterContacts: 1, exportedAt, contacts, groups }`**; both importers (Admin tab + GM Contact Manager) accept that envelope OR the old bare array. `importMaster` de-dupes groups by name, strips `sharedFrom`, and lists members that resolve to nothing in THIS world (reported, never dropped). The sim round-trips a group into a mocked world where every id differs and asserts every member comes back by address.
- **Composer:** group hits lead the To autocomplete (`_groupResult`, `.ncm-autocomplete-item--group`); picking one adds ONE chip (`isGroup`, `×N`, hover = member names, `.ncm-composer__chip--group`, colour as `--g`) whose members were resolved at pick time (unresolved named once, up front). `_onSend` flattens group chips into `sendList` (de-duped) and sends `recipients` whenever a group chip is present, even for one member. `recipientKey` includes `groupId` so the chip removes as one; drafts persist `groupId` and re-add via `_addGroupById`. `composeMessage({ groupId })` opens the composer with the group (launcher forwards it). `MessageService._normalizeRecipients` also expands `data.groupId` (macro API / scripts).
- **Player UI:** the Contact Manager's **Groups panel** (`templates/contact-manager/partials/contact-groups-panel.hbs` = `TEMPLATES.CONTACT_GROUPS_PANEL`, hidden while a form is open). **Rebuilt 2026-10-21 as a RAIL + TRAY (`.ncm-cgr-*`, dev-approved mock `dev/mockups/contact-groups-rail-mock.html`; the row list with hover-only names is gone):** a group is a **chip** (colour bar · name · a stack of up to 3 member avatars · count · GM-shared shield) on a wrapping rail that scrolls past three rows; clicking a chip (`cgOpen`) opens ONE **tray** under the rail — every member as a card (portrait/initials, name, role · address; `cgMember` opens that contact in the detail pane; a member matching none of this character's contacts is a dashed "address only" card) with Message all / Edit / Delete / close (`cgMessage`/`cgEdit`/`cgDelete`/`cgClose`) in the tray header once. **Recently messaged groups sort first**: `sanitizeGroup` keeps `lastUsedAt`, `ContactGroupService.touchActorGroup(actorId, groupId)` stamps it silently (never throws, no event) from the composer's success path for every actor-scope group chip; never-used groups follow A→Z. Past **`ContactManagerApp.GROUP_RAIL_LIMIT`** (6, ≈ one row) the rest fold behind a dashed **"+N more"** chip (`cgMore` toggles `_groupsExpanded`); the open group is always shown. The view-model is `_contactGroupsContext()` → `{ total, expanded, hiddenCount, list, open }` and members resolve to the character's OWN contacts through `_findGroupMemberContact` (master id → address → linked actor → name). New / `cgNew` is the dashed chip at the rail's end. **GM UI:** Admin → Contacts **Groups card** (`.ncm-cg-*`, above the search): New / Push / Edit / Delete (`cgAdmin*` → `ContactsTab.onGroup*`); Push opens the multi picker over player characters. **The card shipped permanently EMPTY (testers 2026-10-22: "saved, can be messaged, never appears in the admin UI; players never get it")** — `AdminPanelApp._prepareContext` lifted two NAMED keys (`contactSummary`, `pushLog`) out of the Contacts tab context instead of spreading it, so `masterGroups` never reached the template and Push was unreachable (the composer reads the setting directly, which is why sending still worked). Now `...contactContext` is spread like every other tab, and `test-contact-groups` asserts the spread AND that every key the tab returns is read by the template. **A tab's context must be SPREAD into the panel context, never cherry-picked.** **Both use `scripts/ui/ContactGroups/GroupEditorDialog.js`** (`editGroupDialog(group, {memberSource})` → name, colour swatches (`GROUP_COLORS`), member list, "Add members" = **`showNamePicker({ multi: true })`** — the shared picker gained a multi-select mode: check rows, Done button, resolves an ARRAY; members already in the group are greyed `in group`). The dialog returns the edited group UNSAVED; the caller owns the write. All CSS in `styles/components/contact-groups.css`, every colour a token (player panel/composer follow the player theme; dialog/card the admin preset).
- **Group pictures (2026-10-29, dev: "group image uploads — it'll appear in groups and when you send them messages").** `group.img` (kept by `sanitizeGroup` through `sanitizeGroupImg`: a path, an http(s) URL or a `data:image/` URI; `javascript:` / other `data:` / anything with a quote or `<>` is dropped). The group editor (`GroupEditorDialog`) has a Picture row — a live preview tile in the group colour, a path box, Browse (FilePicker; a player without file-browser rights is told to paste a path), Clear. Drawn on: the player Contact Manager chip (replaces the colour bar, keeps the colour as an edge) + tray head, the composer's group chip AND its autocomplete row, Admin → Contacts → Groups (list + detail head; a picture gets its own 84px / 44px column), Players → Groups they hold (rack tiles + tray), and every GROUP PICKER: Players → **Push a group to …** (the name picker — it now draws an `icon` in the entry's `color` for a row with no picture instead of the item bag), the map's Group highlight and Messages' Who / Send-to scope pickers. It is part of `groupSig`, so changing it marks pushed copies out of date; pushes, export and import carry it. Not on received mail (a fan-out copy does not know which group it came from). Sim `dev/tools/sims/test-group-images.mjs` (32, also covers the Players → Edit redesign). Not live-tested.
- **Macro API:** `getContactGroups(actorIdOrName?)`, `addContactGroup(group)`, `pushGroupToPlayer(groupId, actorIdOrName)`; `sendMessage({ groupId })`.
- **Phase 3 (same day) — GM tooling.** (1) **Mass Broadcast** (Admin → Messages) has a **Send to** select — every player character, or one of the GM's groups — and sends ONE fan-out (`sendMessage({ from, subject, body, isBroadcast:true, recipients })`) instead of a per-player loop, so refused recipients are NAMED; a bare sender name writes no sent copy (no inbox for it). (2) **Activity feed collapses a fan-out**: `MessagesTab._collapseFanout` (after the filters, before pagination) merges received copies sharing a base messageId into ONE row (`isGroupRow`, id `fanout:<base>`, "N recipients", "x / N unread") that expands (same `toggleMsgExpand`) to a per-recipient list with Open/Delete per copy; sent copies and lone messages pass through; the Unread filter narrows the group to its unread copies. (3) **Orphan Audit** lists **group members that resolve to nothing** (no address AND dead id hints — a raw address is never an orphan) across GM and player groups, and prunes them (`_pruneGroupMembers`; a group left empty is deleted). Also: the **NPC Inbox** panel gained an **Include GM-sent** chip (`_npcInboxIncludeGm`, default off — the panel stays the player→NPC triage view). Sim `dev/tools/sims/test-group-admin.mjs` (21, real MessageService + MessagesTab + ToolsTab over mocked journals).
- **Follow-ups 4–6 (same day).** (4) `MessageRepository.getLastMessagesByCounterparty` indexes every OTHER participant of a group copy (the sent copy's joined "a, b" address is never a key; the viewer's own actor id + address are excluded), so the Contact Manager's Last Interaction / Recent sort see group mail. (5) **Duplicate addresses WARN at assignment** — `EmailService.findActorsByEmail` / `warnIfAddressTaken(email, actor)` (permanent toast naming the other holder, never blocks), called from `registerEmail`, `forceSetEmail` and `ContactRepository.setActorEmail`; a shared address makes the address-keyed member resolver guess. (6) **Group events**: `EVENTS.GROUP_CREATED/UPDATED/DELETED/PUSHED` (`groups:*`) emitted by `ContactGroupService` (`_emit`, never throws), mirrored by IntegrationBridge as Foundry hooks and documented in INTEGRATION.md; the api object exposes `groups` (the service). Sim `dev/tools/sims/test-group-leftovers.mjs` (25).
- Sim: `dev/tools/sims/test-contact-groups.mjs` (58 — sanitize/builders, actor + master CRUD + ownership, search, resolution + unresolved, push/re-push/sharedWith, export → import into a different world, groupId expansion, and wiring guards for templates/css/settings/init/actions/launcher/picker/macro API). **Not live-tested.** Still open: Orphan Audit for dead members, Mass Broadcast → group, Activity-feed collapse by base id, IntegrationBridge group events, last-interaction index over participants.

## Architecture: chat cards — the Esper readout (2026-10-20)

Every NCM chat card is ONE family, **`ncm-cc`** in `styles/chat/chat-cards.css`, rebuilt from the dev-approved mock `dev/mockups/chat-cards-esper-mock.html` (the rejected directions stay as records: `chat-cards-redesign-mock.html` = the panel family, "too AI"; `chat-cards-receipt-mock.html` = thermal printouts, "love it, differs too much from the module"). Tester report that started it: Admin → Share to Chat posted a card headed "GM Intercept — Not visible to players" to a PUBLIC message, cut the body at 200 chars and escaped its HTML into visible tags.
- **Anatomy** (built like the enhance screen, NOT a boxed panel — no card border, no left accent bar, no scanline film; the chat message's own frame is the only frame): a tracked **record line** (`__rec`: `__kind` + `__time` on line 1, `__sub` who/where on line 2 — two deliberate lines, never a wrap), an optional `__whisper` "// GM only" strip, the **hero** inside the zoom brackets (`__hero` + `__tr`/`__bl`: a `__subject`, or a `__numrow` = 44px `__num` + `__verdict`), a **readout strip** (`__ro`: name OVER value), `__flag` lines (">> urgent", ">> critical, exploding 10"), prose in `__text` at 13px body font. **Tone = meaning**: `--ok` green, `--fail`/`--ice` red, `--gm` amber, `--mal` violet, default cyan; nothing else is coloured. Every value is `var(--ncm-*, #fallback)` — the chat log is body-mounted but the PLAYER theme writes `:root`, so a player's palette reaches their cards.
- **Five templates** (message-card / hack-result / ice-retaliation / malware-analysis / network-broadcast) + the two JS builders (`whisperCardHtml` in helpers, `postNetActionCard` in NetResolve) all emit the family; **`network-event.hbs` and `payload-executed.hbs` are DELETED** (no caller, preloaded for nothing). The old `.ncm-ice-card` chat class is gone — it collided with the Contact Manager's ICE overlay partial.
- **Shared message**: one path, `MessageService.shareToChat(message, actorId, {sharedBy})` — the player inbox AND Admin → Messages (`sharedBy:'GM'`) both use it. The WHOLE body ships as HTML (`chatBodyHtml`: HTML passes through, plain text is escaped with line breaks kept); **long bodies fold** (`splitChatBody`: >700 chars or >8 blocks → first paragraph open + a native `<details open>` for the rest, ">> N more lines" / "collapse", no JS, survives re-render). From/To are **people rows** (`__p`: portrait + name + address, directory-first via `resolveDisplayName`/`resolveDisplayPortrait`) that carry `data-ncm-email/actor/contact/name/portrait` — **never for a masked anonymous sender**. `when` = `chatCardTime(message.timestamp)` (the message's own game-time stamp in the GM's date format; every card's record line uses `chatCardTime()`).
- **Add contact is decided per CLIENT** — `scripts/integrations/ChatCards.js` (`registerChatCardHooks`, ready/114): the `renderChatMessage` hook fills each row's `__slot` with an **Add contact** button when the person is not in the viewer's (`resolveOperator()`) contacts, "in contacts" when they are, nothing for yourself / a send-only address / a group copy's joined address (`rowAffordance`, pure). A document-capture click adds through `ContactRepository.addContact` and flips the slot. Chat HTML is one document for everyone, which is why none of this can live in the template.
- Sim `dev/tools/sims/test-chat-cards.mjs` (real templates through Foundry's Handlebars + real `shareToChat`/`splitChatBody`/`rowAffordance`); probe `dev/mockups/chat-cards-probe.html` renders the REAL templates against the real cascade (needs `--allow-file-access-from-files` or the mockup server — it fetches the .hbs and loads Handlebars from the Foundry install).

## Architecture: incoming-message notifications

The recipient's "New Message" toast is driven by a **single** path: `EVENTS.MESSAGE_RECEIVED` → the listener in `registerMessagingSystem.js` → `NotificationService.showMessageNotification`. That method owns the toast **and** the receive sound, and **dedups by `messageId`** (a `_recentMsgToasts` map, ~4s window) because the same delivery emits `MESSAGE_RECEIVED` from two sources: the socket path (`MessageService.handleMessageNotification`, recipient online) and the document-sync path (`DocumentSyncBridge._onJournalPageCreated`, when the journal page appears).

**Scene-control unread badge sits INSIDE the button** (`.ncm-unread-badge`, notifications.css, top/right 1px + z-index) — it used to hang outside at −4px and Foundry's controls toolbar clips overflow, so the circle/count rendered cut off. Don't move it back outside the button bounds. **Mark all as read (2026-08-14):** `MessageRepository.markAllAsRead(actorId)` flips every unread non-sent non-deleted message (spam included — the exact set `getUnreadCount` counts) in ONE `updateEmbeddedDocuments` batch; `MessageService.markAllAsRead` wraps it with a single `MESSAGE_READ` emit + `MESSAGE_STATUS_UPDATE` socket (`messageId: null, all: true` — every listener only reads `actorId`). UI = the `.ncm-pill--action` "Mark all read" pill at the right edge of the viewer's category-pills track (`data-action="mark-all-read"`, renders only when `counts.unread`, dashed border so it doesn't read as a filter).

Rules so the toast doesn't duplicate or render "Unknown — (no subject)":
- **Never** call `showMessageNotification` directly from a delivery handler, and never add a second toast/sound trigger on `MESSAGE_RECEIVED` — emit the event and let the one listener handle it.
- **Every** `MESSAGE_RECEIVED` emitter must carry the display payload: `from`, `fromName`, `fromActorId`, `fromContactId`, `subject`, `preview`, `priority`. The thin `{messageId, toActorId, fromActorId, priority}` shape was the original duplicate-toast bug. `showMessageNotification` resolves the sender as `fromName → linked actor name → master-contact name → from(email) → "Unknown"`.
- Stored message flags have **no** `fromName`/`senderName` field — only `from` (email) + `fromActorId`/`fromContactId`. Resolve names at read time.
- The document-sync emitter (`DocumentSyncBridge._onJournalPageCreated`) detects inboxes via `MessageRepository.identifyInbox` (flag-based — it previously keyed on a stale `isInbox`/`[NCM]` check and never fired). It **skips `-sent` copies and `status.spam`** and gates the toast on the recipient `actor.isOwner`, mirroring the socket path so a player never gets toasts for NPC or other-player mail. All four `DocumentSyncBridge` journal/page handlers use `identifyInbox`.
- Player→NPC mail never hits a player toast path; the **GM** gets its own toast via `MessageService._notifyGmOfNpcMail` (in `_deliverMessage`, gated by setting `notifyGmNpcMail`, default on). Spam-filtered mail (see spam filter section) is delivered **silently** — the socket notify is skipped and the doc-sync path bails on `status.spam`.

## Architecture: eddies (money) transfer

Eddies attached to a message transfer in **one** place — the recipient clicks **Claim** in the viewer (`MessageViewerApp._claimEddies`), which credits `actor.system.wealth` (CPR `value` + `transactions[]` ledger) and sets `status.eddiesClaimed`. The sender is debited at send (`MessageComposerApp._deductEddies`); the amount rides the message via the `eddies` flag until claimed. **Never auto-credit the recipient in the composer** — doing so caused a double transfer / duplicate ledger entry. `_creditEddies` survives only as the **sender refund** on send failure. `_claimEddies` guards re-claim (sent copy, already-claimed, a per-`messageId` re-entrancy lock) and resolves the target message by id from `_cachedMessages` (so thread-transcript entries claim correctly).

## Architecture: self-destruct messages

Composed via `selfDestruct = { mode, expiresAt, duration }` (modes `after_read` | `1h` | `6h` | `24h`; `_computeExpiry` returns null for `after_read`), persisted in flags. Triggered in `MessageViewerApp`:
- **`after_read`** — erased when the reader *leaves* the opened message: `_destroyAfterReadIfNeeded()` from `_selectMessage` (opening another) and `close()`.
- **Timed** — `_setupDestructFill` runs the countdown bar and fires `_playDestructExpiration` at 0, **and** fires immediately if a message is opened after it already expired. A GM-side **reaper** (`MessageService.reapSelfDestruct`, 60s interval registered in `registerMessagingSystem` postReady) erases timed messages whose expiry passed even if never opened (skips `after_read` + sent copies).

Both paths **hard-delete** (`permanentDelete`, not soft/trash) — self-destruct must be non-recoverable. **GMs never trigger self-destruct** (all three viewer paths early-return when `game.user.isGM`) so they can audit inboxes; timed cleanup still happens via the reaper.

## Architecture: unknown-sender spam filter

Opt-in (`spamUnknownSenderEnabled`, default **off**). At delivery, `MessageService._shouldSpamFilter(data)` may route mail to a **player's Spam folder** (sets `status.spam`) when the sender isn't in the recipient's contacts (`_isSenderInRecipientContacts` checks email / linkedActorId / masterContactId). Player-recipient only; skips sent/broadcast/already-spam. Tuners (Module Settings): `spamUnknownSenderChance` (%), `spamUnknownSenderScope` (`known` = sender is a real contact — a master-directory entry **by `fromContactId` or email**, or a registered character — but the player hasn't added them | `all` = also unknown addresses), `spamUnknownSenderExcludePlayers` (keep party comms out). Delivery is silent (see notifications rules). The viewer's `inbox` filter excludes `status.spam`; the `spam` filter shows it.

**`status.spam` must survive persistence.** `MessageRepository.createMessage` builds the stored status field-by-field; it must read `spam: messageData.status?.spam || false` (it was once hardcoded `false`, which silently dropped **every** spam route — the unknown-sender filter AND the Spam tab blast/templates/auto-spam — into the normal inbox). If spam "isn't landing" and the route decision logged true, check that block first. (`saved`/`deleted`/`scheduled` stay hardcoded `false` for new mail — only `spam` honors the caller.) Also note `_shouldSpamFilter`'s recipient gate is `toActor?.hasPlayerOwner` only — narrower than `SpamService.getPlayerActors` (`hasPlayerOwner` **or** an NCM `email` flag) — so it no-ops when the recipient isn't owned by a logged-in non-GM player (the solo-GM-testing trap). Left intentionally narrow: the only extra actors it'd catch are email-only NPCs, which don't check a spam folder.

**Scope `'known'` is the most common "100% chance but still in inbox" gotcha:** a completely unknown sender (a typed-in address not in the directory) is *intentionally skipped* under `'known'` — switch the scope setting to `'all'` to catch those. `_shouldSpamFilter` emits a `log.debug` at **every** early-return with the reason (recipient-not-player, excludePlayers, already-a-contact, scope-skip, chance-0, etc.) plus the final route/pass decision — turn on `debugMode` to see exactly why any message did or didn't land in Spam. The `'known'` check qualifies a sender via `fromActor` **or** a truthy `data.fromContactId` **or** `verifyContact(data.from).verified`, so a message from a real master contact filters even when its email is blank/synthesized (previously it only checked the email path).

## Architecture: Spam tab (generator, auto-spam, import/export)

The Admin → Spam tab (`SpamTab.js` + `templates/admin-panel/tab-spam.hbs`) is backed by `scripts/services/SpamService.js`. Spam templates are pure content (`fromName`/`fromEmail`/`category`/`subject`/`body`/`networkFilter`) — **recipients are chosen at send-time** via the chip UI, never stored on a template. Both the Quick Blast bar and each template's "Send to →" row render the same `spamPlayerActors` list.

- **Recipient list = `SpamService.getPlayerActors()`.** It returns every actor that is `actor.hasPlayerOwner` **or** carries an NCM `email` flag — the same "messageable character" definition used elsewhere (e.g. `OverviewTab`). **Do not** narrow this back to an explicit per-user `OWNER` entry: that silently returns empty for actors owned via *default* ownership or registered NPCs, which is the bug that made the chip lists show only the "All" chip and no players. The per-user owner lookup survives only to pick a chip color.
- **Auto-Spam by Network** config lives in the `spamAutoConfig` world setting (`{ [networkId]: { enabled, volume, categories } }`; volumes low/med/high/flood → count ranges via `getVolumeRange`). It is **wired but easy to mistake for broken**: `registerNetworkSystem.js` (ready, priority 109) listens for `EVENTS.NETWORK_CHANGED` and calls `SpamService.onNetworkConnect(networkId, actorId)` **only when** the event is `source: 'socket'`, **not** `isGMAction`, and carries an `actorId`. That means it fires when a **player with an assigned character** switches networks (their client broadcasts `NETWORK_STATE_CHANGED` with `game.user.character.id`) — **not** on the GM's own switches and **not** for a player without an assigned character. Delivered spam is pre-flagged `status.spam` (goes to the Spam folder, silent — no toast). Both the listener and `onNetworkConnect` emit `log.debug` with the skip/deliver reason (`debugMode`).
- **New / Edit template renders IN the preview pane** (2026-10-28, dev: "put that in the template preview section on the right") — the first branch of `.ncm-spam-lib__detail` (`spamShowCreator` → the form, else the selected template, else the empty state); the old §3 section under the library is gone. **Top row (2026-10-28):** Quick Blast · a **Category** dropdown (`<select data-spam-category>`, change listener in `SpamTab.onRender`; it replaced the row of category pills — the blast still draws from the picked category) · a **Templates** card (New + icon-only Import / Export) share the top (`.ncm-spam-top`, a container query: one line from 900px, else Quick Blast on its own slim line and Category + Templates on the next). **Recipients are ONE searchable multi ScopePicker** (`spamToPicker` in Quick Blast, `spamToPicker2` in the template Send bar, both over the same `_recipients`; a pinned "Every player" row selects all) — the chip-per-character lists are gone (dev: "I have a lot of characters"). `onToggleAll`/`onToggleRecipient` stay for other callers. Sim `test-spam-layout.mjs` (29, a real Handlebars render).
- **Import / Export templates** (`spamImportTemplates`/`spamExportTemplates` actions → `SpamTab.onImport/ExportTemplates` → `SpamService.importTemplates`/`exportTemplates`). Export downloads **only custom** templates (built-ins ship with the module) as `{ ncmSpamTemplates: 1, exportedAt, templates[] }`. Import accepts that envelope **or** a bare array, assigns fresh ids, validates the category, and de-dupes against existing customs by `fromName + subject` (case-insensitive) so re-importing is safe. Follows the ContactsTab blob-download / hidden-`<input type=file>` pattern.

## Architecture: GM awareness of player→NPC mail

`MessageService._notifyGmOfNpcMail` (called in `_deliverMessage`, GM client) toasts the GM when a **player-owned sender → non-player recipient** (gated by `notifyGmNpcMail`). The **NPC Inbox** triage panel in Admin → Messages (`MessagesTab._gatherNpcInbox`, actions `toggleNpcInbox`/`npcInboxFilter`/`npcInboxReply`) groups player→NPC mail by NPC.

## Architecture: anonymous send (GM)

A GM-only composer option (`anonymousEnabled`) that masks the sender: the recipient sees a masked avatar + alias and **cannot resolve the real identity** (it's stored GM-side only — *truly hidden*, not cosmetic). Gated by `game.user.isGM && anonSendEnabled`. Six world settings under "Anonymous Send": `anonSendEnabled`, `anonDefaultStyle` (default **scrambled**), `anonAllowRepliesDefault`, `anonTraceEnabled`, `anonTraceDV` (15), `anonTraceSkill` (a CPR-skill dropdown from `SKILL_MAP`). The message carries an `anonymous` block `{ enabled, role?, style, alias, maskedEmail, allowReplies, traceable, traceDV, traced }`.

**Truly-hidden data model — the masking policy lives in `MessageService`, not the repository.** `MessageRepository` only persists/reads the `anonymous` block (createMessage flags + `_pageToMessage`) and uses the alias for the page name (`_resolveSenderName`). In `_deliverMessage`, `_maskAnonForRecipient(data)` builds a clone with the real identity stripped (`from`=masked email, `fromActorId`/`fromContactId`=''); that clone is used for **both** the recipient inbox copy **and** the recipient `MESSAGE_NOTIFY`/toast. The **real sender survives only on the GM-owned `-sent` copy** (the original `data`). So the recipient's player-readable page never contains the real id. Anonymous mail is **spam-filter exempt** (`_shouldSpamFilter` bails — an "unknown" sender would otherwise always match).

**Masked display is one shared resolver + one CSS component.** `resolveAnonSender(message,{isGM})` (helpers.js) returns the masked view for non-GM (and for a GM reading the scrubbed copy — a known limitation; GMs audit via the `-sent` copy, which reveals + tags **SENT ANON**). All read paths call it: `MessageViewerApp._enrichMessageDisplay` (detail/list/thread — exposes `isAnonMasked`/`isAnonRevealed`/`anonStyle`), `NotificationService.showMessageNotification` (alias), `DocumentSyncBridge._onJournalPageCreated` (carries `anonymous` + alias in the MESSAGE_RECEIVED payload — the doc-sync toast path reads the already-scrubbed page). Avatars render via the `ncm-anon-avatar` Handlebars helper (registerTemplates.js) → pure-CSS layers in `styles/components/anon-mask.css` keyed by `data-anon-style`. **The mask never uses a real photo** — Filtered/Scrambled obscure a neutral silhouette, so nothing leaks. Four styles: `filtered` / `scrambled` (default; heavy static + jitter + tear + RGB) / `ghost` (blobby silhouette) / `monolith` (SEELE "SOUND ONLY" slab). Add-to-Contacts is hidden on masked mail; the `.ncm-anon-badge` shows ANON / SENT ANON / TRACED.

**Reply routing (masked both ways).** `buildReply` detects `originalMessage.anonymous` (role≠recipient, not traced) and returns an `anonReply` descriptor `{ parentMessageId, alias, style, maskedEmail }` with **no** real target — the player can't know it. `sendMessage` and `DataValidator.validateMessage` both skip the recipient-required + verification gates when `data.anonReply`. GM-side `_deliverMessage` calls `_resolveAnonReplyTarget(parentMessageId)` (scans journals for the `<id>-sent` page → real sender), sets the true `toActorId`, and delivers; the replier's **own** sent copy is re-masked via `_maskAnonReplyForSender` → an `anonymous` block with **`role: 'recipient'`** so their outbox can't reveal who they answered (`resolveAnonRecipient` masks the TO; `resolveAnonSender` skips role `'recipient'`). The composer shows a locked masked recipient chip (`anonReplyTo`). One-way mode (`allowReplies` false) → `anonRepliesBlocked` hides Reply/Quick-Reply and shows a "No Reply" chip.

**GM reply within an anon thread pre-ticks Anonymous Send (toggleable).** When a **GM** opens a reply, the composer calls `MessageService.getThreadAnonConfig(originalMessage)` (scans journals for the thread's original GM-owned anon copy — `anonymous.enabled && role !== 'recipient'`, matched by base `messageId`/`inReplyTo`/`references`/`threadId`, preferring the thread-root) and, if found, **pre-enables** `anonymousEnabled` + seeds `anonStyle`/`anonAlias`/`anonAllowReplies`/`anonTraceDV` from that block (`_anonConfigFromBlock`). It's only pre-filled — the GM can untick it per reply (deliberately not forced). Gated `game.user.isGM && _isAnonFeatureEnabled()`, so players never get it (all anon **sending** UI — toggle, options strip, masked identity — is behind `isGM`; a player only ever sees the locked `anonReplyTo` chip).

**Composer Send button must be re-enabled on every `_onSend` exit path.** `MessageComposerApp._onSend` disables the send button (`disabled=true` + `--disabled`, which is `pointer-events:none`) at the top; it must be restored in the `finally` block. It previously only restored on the eddies-deduction early-return, so a single failed/exception send (the anon-reply path is prone — the GM resolves the hidden target) left the button dead to mouse clicks while Ctrl+Enter still worked (the keydown handler calls `_onSend` directly, bypassing the dead button). That's the "keyboard sends but the button won't click" signature.

**Trace-to-unmask (GM-mediated).** A "Trace" button (`canTraceAnon`) runs `MessageViewerApp._traceAnonSender` → skill dialog + `skillService.performCheck(actor, anonTraceSkill, {dc: anonymous.traceDV})`. Result is reported to the GM via `SOCKET_OPS.ANON_TRACE_REQUEST` (player→GM; the client reports success — trust model matches the existing relay). `MessageService.handleAnonTraceRequest` → `revealAnonSender(messageId, inboxActorId)` resolves the real sender (`_resolveAnonReplyTarget`) and **patches the player's page** with real `fromActorId`/`from`/`fromContactId` + `anonymous.traced=true`; the update syncs back (`DocumentSyncBridge._onJournalPageUpdated` → `MESSAGE_STATUS_CHANGED` → viewer re-render) and the mask resolves to the real identity. `resolveAnonSender` and `buildReply` both bail when `traced` (so a traced sender shows normally and replies directly). GM clicking Trace force-reveals (no roll). Sounds: `trace-fire` (attempt), `shard-decrypt` (reveal), `hack-fail` (miss).
- **Two trace bugs that bit and how they were fixed (don't reintroduce):** (1) `revealAnonSender` must rewrite the **whole** `anonymous` block (`{...current, traced:true}`), NOT a nested `anonymous.traced` dot-path via `updateMessage` — the nested-flag update is unreliable (the standing CLAUDE.md gotcha) and the **player-side unmask hinges entirely on `traced` landing** (the player masks whenever `enabled && !traced`, regardless of `fromActorId`; only the GM-reveal path keys off `fromActorId`, so the GM saw it "work" while players stayed masked). (2) `handleAnonTraceRequest` must **check `revealAnonSender`'s result** before toasting "identity revealed" — it used to toast success unconditionally, so a failed reveal (e.g. missing `-sent` copy) looked like it worked while nothing changed. On failure it now warns the GM with the reason.

**CSS gotchas this hit:** new composer buttons (`ncm-composer__anon-pill`/`anon-reply`) MUST be added to the composer button-reset lists in `message-composer.css` or they render as Foundry's full-width native buttons; viewer avatar hosts (`.ncm-viewer__avatar--anon`) need `overflow:hidden` because they set `border-radius` but rely on a rounded `<img>` (the absolutely-positioned mask isn't otherwise clipped); the `.ncm-anon-badge` needs `vertical-align:middle`+`align-self:center`+`line-height:1` or it rides high on the text baseline.

## Architecture: inbox window title + theme extras

The inbox window title is **personalized and diegetic**: `MessageViewerApp.get title()` → `{handle} // {agentName}` (handle = NET email local-part, else actor name; `agentName` from `playerTheme.agentName`, default "Agent"). The singleton viewer switches characters, so `_onRender` re-syncs the `.window-title` element (AppV2 builds the frame once). Boot splash + Character Select keep the "Night City Messenger" brand. The Theme Customizer **Text panel** (tab labeled "Text" — formerly "Fonts"; the internal `data-tc-tab`/`activeTab` key is still `fonts`) holds the **Agent Name** field, the font selectors, text size, and a **contrast** slider (70–140%, step 5, default 100). The Agent Name row is wrapped in `.ncm-tc__font-section` so it shares the panel's 16px indent (it's the only `.ncm-tc__srow` outside that section otherwise, which left it flush-left). Contrast — `ThemeService.applyTheme` tone-maps the bg/text/border palette by spreading their **OKLCH lightness** around a pivot of **0.42** (`CONTRAST_SPREAD` in constants.js; `adjustLightness` in helpers.js), **not** a CSS `filter: contrast()`. The filter approach distorted images/wallpaper and drifted saturation; the palette spread leaves images untouched and preserves hue/chroma (neon stays neon), and accents (`primary/secondary/accent`) are deliberately excluded. It always recomputes from base prefs/preset, so dragging can't compound. `textMuted`/`border` aren't user-overridable or in any preset, so `CONTRAST_SPREAD` carries their variables.css defaults as the `base` fallback. Range is deliberate: `text-primary` whites out ~110% and `bg-deep` blacks out ~142%, so 140 is a clean high-contrast ceiling without crushing the bg depth layers (don't exceed ~150); the 70 floor is a soft/dim mode for glare sensitivity. No JS clamps the value — the slider `min`/`max` in `theme-customizer.hbs` are the only gates. The old `--ncm-contrast` var + reset.css filter rule are gone. The customizer's Save button enables via `_markDirty()` (toggles the button's `disabled` in the DOM) because live inputs (sliders/selects/text) don't re-render. **Player wallpaper (2026-07-11):** the Effects panel has a **Wallpaper** section (path + FilePicker browse + a 5–100 **Strength** slider, `prefs.wallpaper`/`prefs.wallpaperStrength`, live via `_applyLive`). `ThemeService.applyTheme` sets `--ncm-wallpaper[-strength]` on `:root`; message-viewer.css renders it behind BOTH viewer panes (`.ncm-viewer__list-panel/__detail-panel` — the terminal pattern: `isolation: isolate` + a `z-index:-1` ::before, since the panels' own backgrounds are opaque). **Terminals stay isolated**: an element-scoped `applyTheme` with no wallpaper writes an explicit `none`/`0` instead of removing the vars — removal would let the terminal INHERIT the player's `:root` wallpaper.

## Architecture: composer header pills — network + date (2026-10-17)

Tester ask: "is there a way to change the network it's sending from?" — there wasn't: the pill was display-only and `sendMessage` always stamped `currentNetworkId` + now. Mockup `dev/mockups/composer-network-picker-mock.html`; real-template probe `dev/mockups/composer-netpicker-probe.html` (`?as=gm|player&open=1&via=1&dated=1&q=&f=`); sim `dev/tools/sims/test-composer-overrides.mjs` (25 checks).
- **The network pill is a button (`toggleNetPicker`) with TWO behaviours.** PLAYER: the inbox selector's list (reachable only; locked → `NetworkAuthDialog`) and a pick is a REAL `switchNetwork` — the inbox follows because it already re-renders on `NETWORK_CHANGED`. GM: every network for **this message only** (`sendNetworkId`), own connection pinned first as Default, search + per-type filter chips (live DOM filter in `_setupNetPicker`, never a re-render); an override shows a dashed amber pill + VIA tag. The dropdown reuses `network-selector.css`'s `ncm-net-selector`/`ncm-net-option` rows (`_buildNetPicker`).
- **The composer now re-renders on `NETWORK_CHANGED`** (`_netChangedHandle`, unsubscribed in `close()`) — but skips `source:'socket'` events, which are OTHER users' switches echoed in and don't move this client.
- **GM date chip (`pickSendDate`) → CyberTimePicker.** Past = backdate (`sendTimestamp`: delivered now, stamped then — still unread, still toasts). Future = it's a schedule, so it switches on the EXISTING Schedule (with `scheduleGameTime`) rather than inventing a second one. Enabling Schedule clears a backdate.
- **Service side is opt-in by flag and GM-only — `MessageService._resolveSendOverrides`.** `networkOverride` + `network` → stamp that network, its `getMessageAccessControl(networkId)` and its configured signal (garbling); `backdated` + a PAST `timestamp` → keep it. Every other caller (players, the dead-zone queue flush, scheduled delivery without the flags) keeps the old rule. Both flags are deleted before persistence. Timed self-destruct is unaffected — its `expiresAt` is computed from now in the composer, not from the stamp.
- **Theme follow-up (same day, dev: "the CitiNet pill on all-white is hard to read; the date picker's formatted output and arrows are off").** The pill carries the network colour as **`--net-c`** on the button (never an inline `color:`), so `[data-ncm-admin-theme="clean-light"]` can pull it toward `--ncm-text-primary` (`color-mix` in oklab, 45%) — a network colour is chosen for a dark screen and #19f3f7 on white is ~1.3:1. **CyberTimePicker's inline styles are now `var(--ncm-*, #brandhex)`** (it was the last JS-built dialog still painting literal hex, so the Formatted Output panel and spin arrows stayed dark under every admin preset), hover/focus/AM-PM state moved from JS handlers to classes in `cyber-time-picker.css`, and its digit boxes are sized at **(0,3,1)** because `tab-tools.css`'s `.ncm-time-config-dialog input[type=text]` (0,2,1, `!important`) had been forcing every picker box to 40px/12px beside 30px arrows — in all five places the picker is used. Probe: `dev/mockups/time-picker-probe.html?t=clean-light|clean-dark|high-contrast&h12=1&month=short`; the netpicker probe gained `?t=`. (Headless renders sometimes snapshot before Font Awesome loads — a blank arrow/Set icon in a screenshot is that, not a bug; a 12s virtual-time budget usually gets the glyphs.)

## Architecture: the clean composer (2026-10-28)

Dev: "a new revised version that's more clean with all the same functionality" — rebuilt from the approved `dev/mockups/composer-clean-mock.html` (same 640×560 window, same data-actions where this section does not say otherwise). Files: `templates/message-composer/message-composer.hbs`, `styles/components/message-composer.css` (tokens only — `var(--ncm-X, var(--ncm-fallback))` so the same rules wear the admin preset in GM view and any player theme; every font-size and control height a `calc(Npx * var(--ncm-text-scale, 1))`; the Foundry/CPR button reset at 0,2,1 with `!important` on structural props covers every new control — the mock used spans, the build does not), `scripts/ui/MessageComposer/MessageComposerApp.js`, the pure `scripts/utils/drafts.js` and `scripts/ui/MessageComposer/previewModel.js`, `scripts/services/MessageTemplateService.js`. `composer-atmosphere.css` was never imported by `night-city-messenger.css` — dead file, left alone.
- **Layout:** ONE header line (identity with the Send-as caret / ANON look / GM · VIA tags · the network pill, unchanged behaviour: player = real switch, GM = this-message VIA override, `_setupNetPicker` DOM filter kept, the dropdown now clamps to the window's right edge · the date chip). **The mode badge is gone** — the quote card says "Replying to" / "Forwarding this message" and the Send button says it. One slim alert line (dead zone red with Queue on the line / darknet violet). The **address block** is hairline rows with one label column (To with the chips — person / group ×N / locked / unreachable / anon-reply — and the autocomplete; `Cc` as a quiet link at the row's end; Subject hidden on a reply; `errors.to` / `errors.general` both render). Plain-language labels in sentence case — no SUBJ:/PRI/ENC.
- **Delivery = chips + ONE drawer.** Each option is a chip stating its value (`Normal` · `Open` / `ICE DV 15` / `Black ICE DV 18` · `Self-destruct after reading` · `Anonymous Scrambled` · `Schedule 15 Aug 08:00`); a chip click (`toggleDrawer` + `data-drawer`, state `_drawer`) opens that option's panel under the row and **only one drawer is ever open**; switching a feature ON (`toggleSelfDestruct` / `toggleAnonymous` / `toggleSchedule`, now taking an optional `data-on="1|0"` from the drawer's Off|On segment, else toggling) opens its drawer; picking a self-destruct mode switches it on. Off chips are quiet outlines, on chips fill in their meaning colour (`data-tone`: act cyan · attn amber · bad red · secret violet · money); priority by level. The lock drawer holds Open|ICE (`setEncryption`), the DV box (`encryption-dv`), Standard|Red ICE|Black ICE (**`cycleEncryptionType` with `data-type`** — without one it still cycles), and for lethal ICE the "On a miss" (`setFailureMode`) and "The ICE" rows (`setIceSource` + the actor cards `setIceActor`). Players see Priority + Schedule only. The editor toolbar is two groups, format (B I code link list ⋯) | insert (shard · file · media · eddies | timestamp · location · GM templates); the ICE-protected tag + char count sit on the editor's foot line; attachments are chips above the footer.
- **Drafts are a LIST per character — `composerDrafts: { [id]: draft }` (cap `DRAFT_CAP` 20, oldest pruned with `-=id` deletion keys), lifted once from the legacy single `composerDraft` at read time (`readDrafts` unsets it and writes the list).** Tester Cobain: "when I go to the same character that already has a draft and try to create another draft it just opens the last saved one" — the old `_restoreDraft()` ran in the constructor. Now **a fresh composer NEVER restores a draft by itself**; a draft is opened on purpose: `composeMessage({ draftId })` (whitelisted in the launcher; `_loadPendingDraft` runs at the top of `_prepareContext`, before the first paint) from the Agent's **Drafts tab** (`_buildDraftItems` lists every draft with to / subject / age / kind; rows carry `data-draft-id`), or from the composer's own footer **"N drafts"** link (`openDrafts` → the shared picker, `admin:false`; picking one opens it in THIS window — what was being written is saved first as its own draft, so nothing is asked and nothing is lost). Each window mints its own `_draftId` on the first autosave (every 30 s; an empty window writes nothing); the footer **Draft** button saves and CLOSES; a successful send deletes only that id; closing with content autosaves it, closing an empty window deletes it (`_draftDone` keeps a sent/saved window from writing again). A reply/forward draft stores `originalMessageId` + `originalActorId` and comes back WITH its quote card (`_applyDraft` reloads the original through `messageService.getMessage` and re-runs `_initReply` / `_initForward`; a gone original opens as a plain draft and says so). The constructor's reply / forward / compose setup lives in `_initReply(options)` / `_initForward()` / `_initCompose(options)` for that reason. A contact-only sender (no actor) keeps its draft in memory only.
- **Preview as recipient (GM): footer `togglePreview` (`_preview`) swaps the body for a read-only card of what the player will open** — pure `buildPreview(state)` in previewModel.js: the masked avatar (`ncm-anon-avatar`) + alias + masked address when anonymous (the real identity is nowhere in the view-model — the sim asserts it), else name / address / portrait; network + date; the priority flag; the To line as the recipient sees it (a group = the party); the body cleaned with richText `mail`, OR the ICE lock block (DV · type · what a miss does) when encrypted — an encrypted preview carries no body at all; attachments; foot notes (self-destruct mode, replies-go-back-to-the-mask / one-way, the scheduled time). "Back to editing" is a re-render; the form state survives because `_syncFormData` runs first.
- **Message templates (GM): world setting `messageTemplates`** (`[{ id, name, subject, body, priority, encryption:{enabled,dv,type,failureMode}, selfDestruct:{enabled,mode}, createdAt }]`), `game.nightcity.messageTemplateService` (`MessageTemplateService`: `getAll/get/findByName/save` (a known name overwrites, keeping the id) `/remove/exportTemplates/importTemplates`, `sanitize`, `mergeImport` (pure, de-dupes by name case-insensitively, fresh ids), `summary` = "subject + body · Red ICE DV 14 · self-destruct"; writes GM-only). The toolbar's templates button opens a menu (`toggleTemplates`, `tpl-menu`): **Insert** (`tplInsert` → the shared picker, sub = what it carries; fills an empty subject, sets priority / lock / self-destruct, and asks Replace / Append for the body when the editor has text), **Save this message as a template** (`tplSave` → `adminPrompt` for the name, `adminConfirm` on Overwrite), **Import / Export** (`tplImport` / `tplExport`: `{ ncmMessageTemplates: 1, exportedAt, templates }`, a bare array accepted — the SpamService hidden-`<input type=file>` / blob-download pattern).
- **Removed:** `insertSignature` (action, handler, menu item — it only ever pasted the header's own name + address; the `.ncm-signature` editor rule stays for old bodies), the mode badge and its `modeKey/modeLabel*` context, the two ICE strips (folded into the lock drawer), the free-standing Cc input's "CC" button text, "Message Transmitted" (now "Message sent" / "Message queued"). Unchanged in behaviour: the paste listener, group chips, the anon-reply locked chip, `terminalSend` (VIA machine tag, locked sender, no eddies when impersonating), the dead-zone Queue path, the relay-ACK success/queued/failure overlay, `_onSend`'s `finally` re-enabling Send, Ctrl+Enter / Esc, the GM-view hooks mirrored by hand from BaseApplication.
- Sim `dev/tools/sims/test-composer-clean.mjs` (153: the drafts rules, the preview model, the template envelope round-trip + de-dupe, actions ↔ template both ways, every data-id the JS reads, no insertSignature, tokens-only + text-scaled CSS, every `--ncm-*` read is defined, the viewer Drafts tab, the launcher whitelist, the setting + service, Send-in-finally, and a real Handlebars render of GM drawer / preview / player dead-zone). Probe `dev/mockups/composer-netpicker-probe.html` renders the REAL hbs (`?as=gm|player&t=…&p=<player preset>&mode=reply&all=1&drawer=enc|anon|sd|sched|pri&preview=1&drafts=3&att=1&err=1&open=1&sendas=1&tpl=1&contrast=1`); the contrast walker paints every computed colour through a canvas because `color-mix(in oklab …)` computes to `oklab(…)`, which a channel regex misreads — measured 0 text nodes under 4.5:1 on clean-dark, clean-light, high-contrast and the player theme (incl. the Arasaka preset). **Not live-tested.**

## Player send-gate verification

`MessageService.sendMessage`'s player-only verification gate must accept a recipient that matches the master directory **or** a registered actor email — go through `ContactRepository.verifyContact(email)` (which checks both), not a master-contacts-only lookup. This must stay in sync with the `RequireContactVerificationHint` setting text in `languages/en.json`, which promises both.

**Correspondent rule (2026-09-10, tester report):** the gate ALSO passes when the recipient has **already written to this character** — `MessageService._isKnownCorrespondent(fromActorId, {actorId, contactId, email})` scans the replier's mailbox for a non-spam, non-sent, non-masked-anon message from that actor / master contact / email. Without it, a GM sending from an NPC actor that isn't in the directory and has no registered email produced mail the player could read but never answer ("Contact Not Found" on Reply). The setting hint states the rule. Sim: `dev/tools/sims/test-sender-name.mjs`.

**Auto-register NPC senders (2026-09-10, dev-picked "autobuilder but notify").** `MessageService._autoRegisterNpcSender(data, fromActor)` runs in `sendMessage` right after `data.from` is resolved (GM client only): sending from an NPC actor (`!hasPlayerOwner`) with no directory entry **by actor or by email** creates a master contact — name = actor name, `email` = the resolved `from`, `actorId`, portrait (never mystery-man), CPR role item → `role`, provenance note, **not** send-only (it's a person, unlike the machine-created contacts) — back-fills `data.fromContactId`, and toasts the GM pointing at Admin → Contacts to edit the name. An existing entry by actor just back-fills `fromContactId`; an unlinked entry with that email is left alone. Gated by world setting **`autoRegisterNpcSenders`** (default on). Never blocks the send (failures log + return null). Same sim.

**Tester round 2 (2026-09-11) — five silent no-ops, one enumeration bug behind four reports.** Sim: `dev/tools/sims/test-contact-sharing.mjs` (21 checks).
- **`getPlayerCharacters()` (helpers.js) is the ONE enumeration of "the players' characters"** for every GM tool: each non-GM user's ASSIGNED character first, then any other actor a player OWNS (`hasPlayerOwner`), non-person types (blackIce/demon/container) skipped. Four surfaces (Admin → Contacts share dialog + "Known by" pips + the Player Characters group, Messages → Mass Broadcast, Messages → View Inbox) each had their own `for (user of game.users) if (!user.character) continue` loop, so a PC that was owned but never assigned on the user sheet was invisible to ALL of them ("can't open the player's inbox from the panel", "broadcast doesn't work"). Don't write another `user.character` loop.
- **"Known by" never worked**: `ContactsTab._buildKnownBy` called `contactRepo.getAll(actorId)` — a method `ContactRepository` never had (its reader is the async `getContacts`) — inside a try/catch, so every pip read "does not have this contact". It now reads the `contacts` flag synchronously and matches by master id, email, OR the linked actor.
- **Sharing a Player Character did nothing**: the Contacts-tab PC rows carry `pc-<actorId>` ids and `_showShareDialog` skipped them silently while toasting "Shared 0 contacts". `MasterContactService.pushActorToPlayer(actorId, targetActorId)` builds the contact from the actor (name / NCM email flag / portrait / CPR role / `actorId`, `type:'player'`); a character never receives itself. `pushToPlayer` is now **duplicate-safe** (master id / email / linked actor) so re-sharing is a no-op instead of a second copy. The dialog reports partial failures.
- **"Contact Not Found" on a shared imported-NPC contact** (Import Actor → Share → player sends): players don't receive actors they have no permission on, so on the PLAYER client `game.actors.get(toActorId)` is null for a GM-owned NPC and the old resolver died with "Recipient not found" before the gate ran. `sendMessage` now resolves such a recipient through the master directory (`getByActorId`, a world setting every client reads) and otherwise trusts the player's own actor link when an address is present (`actorLinkOnly` — the verification gate still applies, so an unvouched address is an honest CONTACT NOT FOUND). The composer carries a sole actor recipient's address in `to` for the same reason, and its To-search now surfaces **contact-only entries** (no linked actor) as `_addContactRecipient` chips instead of only actor-linked ones; chips are removed by `recipientKey` (actor id → master id → email), because contact-only chips keyed on a null actor id could never be removed.
- **Admin hard-delete toasted "deleted" unconditionally**: `MessagesTab.onHardDelete` ignored `hardDeleteMessage`'s `{success:false, error}` (an orphaned inbox — owner actor/contact gone — or a missing page returns that, never throws). It now checks the result, falls back to a direct journal scan by page flag, clears the repo's inbox cache, and reports the reason. **Opening an orphaned inbox** from the feed / View Inbox warns "owner no longer exists → Tools → Orphan Audit" (`_warnIfOrphanInbox`) instead of the generic "Actor or contact not found". Mass Broadcast likewise counted a send as success without reading the result and stored the character NAME as `to` — it now leaves `to` for the service to resolve and reports failures.
- **Players had no Remove for their own contacts**: the card's delete icon only rendered inside the `isEncrypted` branch and the detail panel's Delete lived in the GM Controls card. Both now offer it (`deleteContact` — the handler already accepted owner deletes).
- **Factory Reset (Tools → Danger Zone, built 2026-09-11 at the dev's request) — `scripts/core/FactoryReset.js`.** `gatherResetPlan()` is a read-only census the dialog shows; `runFactoryReset({resetSettings, reload})` does it: deletes every NCM journal (flagged OR anywhere inside the "Night City Messenger" folder tree — `MessageRepository.ROOT_FOLDER_NAME`) and the tree itself (`root.delete({deleteSubfolders, deleteContents})`), terminal pin Notes, module-managed macros (`managed:true`) + their two folders, then strips `flags.<MODULE_ID>` from every other document across `MIGRATION_SCOPES` (primary + embedded — actors/items/scenes/tokens/users/folders/messages; GM-authored macros only lose the flag block), and, when the checkbox is ticked (default), resets every registered WORLD setting to its default via `game.settings.settings`. Two stamps are KEPT: `namespaceMigrationDone` stays true (legacy Setting documents remain on disk and un-stamping would re-prompt the id migration) and `dataSchemaVersion` is set to `DATA_SCHEMA_VERSION`. Client settings and compendia are never touched. Finishes with `SettingsConfig.reloadConfirm({world:true})` because every service caches world state. The dialog requires typing RESET (button disabled until then). The old dead "Reset Module" must not come back as a second entry. Sim: `dev/tools/sims/test-factory-reset.mjs` (29 checks).
**Sender/recipient display names — the MASTER DIRECTORY beats the actor label (2026-09-10).** `resolveDisplayName({contactId, actorId, email, storedName}, fallback)` (helpers.js; portrait twin `resolveDisplayPortrait`) is the ONE precedence: master contact by id → linked actor → email → stored `fromName`/`toName` → actor name → email local-part. Every read path uses it: `MessageViewerApp._enrichMessageDisplay` (the player's OWN contact label still wins first), `_addContactFromMessage`, `MessageRepository._resolveSenderName` (page title), `DocumentSyncBridge` toast name, `MessageService` `fromName`/`toName` at send, and the composer's From line + `_addRecipient`. Rationale: the GM labels actors for bookkeeping ("Rogue-Fixer") and edits the directory entry to the in-world name ("Rogue"); players must see the edited name. The old order (actor name first, directory never consulted) is the bug the tester hit. `fromName` is still not persisted in flags — resolve at read time.

**Auto send-only:** master contacts **auto-created by a machine** — a terminal-account save (`TerminalConfigApp`) or the composer's From→master-list link-or-create (`DataShardComposer._syncFromToMasterList`) — default to **`sendOnly: true`** (a machine login / file sender isn't a person's Agent). Untick Send-only in the Master List to open one up. Contacts the GM creates by hand are unaffected.

**Send-only master contacts (system addresses, 2026-07-13).** A master contact can be flagged **`sendOnly`** — a one-way broadcast/system address (`system@localhost`, `alerts@…`): the GM sends FROM it; players can never message it, reply to it, or add it to contacts. Edited via the GM Master List form's **Message Routing** select (`_val('sendOnly') === 'true'` in the GMCM save; `'sendOnly'` is in `updateContact`'s whitelist); surfaced as an amber **SEND-ONLY** chip in the GM Contact Manager detail AND an Admin → Contacts row badge (`.ncm-ct-badge--sendonly`). Enforcement (helper `MasterContactService.isSendOnly(email)`):
- **`sendMessage` hard-block** — a player send to a sendOnly recipient (matched by email **or** `getByActorId`) returns `errorCode: 'SEND_ONLY'` ("TRANSMISSION REFUSED"), **independent of `requireContactVerification`** — the gate sits BEFORE the verification gate and always runs.
- **Every add/reply affordance hides**: MessageViewer (`senderSendOnly` in `_enrichMessageDisplay` — hides the Add button, folds into `anonRepliesBlocked` so Reply/Quick-Reply vanish and the No-Reply chip shows system-address copy), shard entry-header chips (`resolveEntrySender` exposes `sendOnly` → `canAdd`/`canReply` false), terminal detail chips (`canAddContact`/`canReply` check `isSendOnly`), and the composer's recipient autocomplete filters flagged emails for players.
- GM flows are untouched (the GM composes from/to anything).

## Architecture: the boot splash (the Monogram Etch)

**`scripts/ui/BootSplash.js` — `playBootSplash()`.** The mark that plays on the FIRST inbox open of a page load (`openNCM` guards with `_hasBootedThisSession`, so a reload is the only way to see it twice). A ~520px **panel** opens from a scanline sliver, the three letters are drawn inside it as open SVG strokes, a scan sweeps down and leaves them solid, a rule draws outward from the centre, and NIGHT CITY MESSENGER unfolds beneath it. **The accent is the SECONDARY slot (brand cyan), not primary** (dev: "make the red accents the blue") — primary is the machine-identity red and a boot flourish in alert red reads as a warning at a glance. The scan stays legible on top of it by being the same hue pushed toward white (`color-mix` with a literal fallback declared first), rather than a second colour competing with the accent. **It is a window, not a takeover** (dev, second round): a boot flourish that blacks out the screen reads as an error state, so there is **no scrim at all** (dev, third round) — the panel carries its own ground and its own glow, the scanline film sits on the PANEL rather than the desktop, and the table keeps looking at Foundry while it plays. Darkening a screen the splash deliberately does not block would be a lie about what is usable. A header strip carries the identity, a segmented meter and the state chip (Etching → Set) so the mark itself never has to. The header's bottom edge IS the progress line — one hairline doing two jobs. Corner brackets are inset 5px and the header and footer are padded to 22px to clear them; at 14px the text ran under the marks and read as a mistake. It replaced the icon + title + percentage card; the dev picked it from five concepts in `dev/mockups/boot-splash-concepts.html`, which stays as the record of the options.

- **The animation is ONE deterministic `paint(p)`, p = 0..1, fed by rAF** — not a pile of CSS delays. That is what makes reduced motion "paint(1) and stop" rather than a second look that can drift, lets any frame be frozen for review, and puts the pace in ONE constant (`DURATION_MS`) with the beats as fractions of it in `STAGE`. The sim asserts the stage windows stay inside 0..1, never invert, and never run backwards.
- **A wall clock finishes it, not just the frame loop.** `requestAnimationFrame` stops dead in a hidden tab and can be throttled to nothing, and **`openNCM` awaits this promise before opening the inbox** — so a stalled loop would leave a splash on screen with no Agent behind it. `capAfter()` arms a `setTimeout` for the whole run; whichever lands first wins, because `finish()` is idempotent. Headless found this: under `--virtual-time-budget` the rAF loop never completed and the overlay was still in the DOM at 45s.
