- Sub-tabs: **This scene** (scene scope picker with the viewed scene pinned, dead zone, "Players start on", Broadcast with a scope-picker target, only global + this scene's networks each with a signal slider → `updateNetwork({signalStrength})`, players as avatars from `game.nightcity.playerNetworks`, on/off; "Change which networks reach this scene" multi picker) · **All networks** (search, Group by, chips, list + detail, ⋯ reset logins/duplicate/log/delete) · **Scenes** (THE ATLAS — see the bullet below) · **Activity log** (multi network picker, kind chips, Add a note row, Locked out now, ⋯ import/export/reset/clear).
- **The six-step editor IN the detail pane** — `NetworksEditor.js` (pure: `NET2_STEPS`, `NET2_PRESETS` "Start from", `readEditorForm`, `wherePatches`, `editorContext`). All steps stay in the DOM (strip/Back/Next only toggle `.is-on`). `readEditorForm` builds the networkData shape `createNetwork`/`updateNetwork` always took + `where` → `setSceneNetworkAvailability` for changed scenes only. `NetworksTab._readForm` adds the **countermeasure override** (the shared partial in the Access step, `readCmvOverride`). The draft survives re-renders (`prepareContext` reads `.ncm-net2-form[data-edit-session=N]` back into `_draft`; bump `_editSession` when the mounted form stops describing the draft). Signal/reliability/delay of 0 now survive. Context `net2`; `networks`/`sceneStrip`/`netStats` stay top-level (Overview + footer). CSS `ncm-net2-*`.
- **Scenes = THE ATLAS (2026-10-29, dev-picked from `dev/mockups/admin-networks-scenes-atlas-mock.html`: "A as the home, C's transmitter strip as the Paint picker, B one switch away as Grid"; built by hand, no agent).** For worlds with many scenes and scenes still in compendiums. **Shelves are the GM's own Foundry scene folders** (tree order, nested sub-folders indented as their own shelf, scenes in a deleted folder → Not in a folder) with the **navigation bar** pinned as the first shelf; each shelf head shows its networks as dots, players, dead / not-wired counts; a shelf shows `SHELF_LIMIT` (36) cards then "+N more" (`scMore`). Default folds = the standing rule: the nav shelf + the viewed scene's folder and its parents open, else the first shelf; a search / Show / Folder pick opens all; the GM's fold wins for the session (DOM-only `[data-sc-fold]` → `_scShut`/`_scOpen`). A **card** = thumbnail (`scene.thumb`, else a gradient in the name hue), start network flag, faces of players looking at it, nav pin, hatched red dead zone, coverage dots in each network's own colour, amber "not wired". Toolbar is ONE line (2026-10-29: Show / Sort are icon-labelled selects, Atlas | Grid icon-only, the "N scenes · K in compendiums" count moved to the page head) = search (name or folder path) · **Folder** ScopePicker (a folder and its subtree; callback ignores the open fire) · **Show** (All / Not wired / Dead zones / Players here / On the nav bar / Imported, counts on the options) · **Sort** inside a shelf (Folder order / A–Z / Recently changed / Most wired; the viewed scene leads) · the hide-unused-imports eye (OFF by default since 2026-10-29 — every scene shows, like Foundry's Scenes tab; on, it hid most of a world built from compendium maps) · **Atlas | Grid** · "Showing N of M · K in compendiums". **Paint = ONE brush bar (2026-10-29, dev: the tile strip got squished; it replaced the transmitter strip + dock below):** `.ncm-net2-brush` = the searchable `paint` ScopePicker ("Paint with — Pick a network…", "Change" while painting via `fixedLabel`), the network being painted as a chip in its colour with its coverage, up to 4 recently painted networks as one-click swaps (`paintRecent`, `scPaint`), and Stop; one line however many networks. The tile strip, the dock-fitting code and the tile Find box are gone (`paintDock` stays in scenesAtlas.js, unused by the template). Older note: **Paint = the transmitter strip** (past `PAINT_DOCK_AT` = 8 networks, 2026-10-29: a DOCK of the useful few — the one being painted, then recently painted, then the most widely placed, `paintDock` in scenesAtlas.js — shown only as many as FIT at full width (the painted one always; `onRender` hides the rest and re-fits on resize / once fonts land), plus an **All networks (N)** ScopePicker grouped by type with each network's coverage; picking one starts painting, the open-fire is ignored): every network as a tile (colour, coverage n/total); click one (`scPaint`; a global network is refused with a reason) and every card becomes a one-click toggle for it (`scCard`), wired cards carry a check in the network colour, each shelf head offers **Wire all / Unwire all** (`scShelfPaint`, `shelfPaint`: dead zones untouched), Esc or the lit tile stops; hovering a tile previews its reach DOM-only. Not painting, a card click opens the **inspector** under the shelves (networks + Add picker, Players start on, Dead zone, View scene, Open in This scene) — everything the old row had. **Grid** (`_scMode='grid'`): scenes × networks under folder bands, globals as dashed not-editable columns, band tallies n/m that wire / unwire the folder (`scBandNet`), cells (`scCell`) with **shift-click = the clicked cell's new state over the range in display order** (`rangeIds`, `_scGridOrder`), Start select + Dead switch per row. **In compendiums**: one folded shelf per Scene pack (index loaded once, async, then re-render); a pack scene can be **pre-wired** (paint it, or pick it → networks / start / dead zone + **Import into the world**) — kept in the hidden world setting **`scenePrewire`** `{ [packUuid]: { nets, start, dead } }` and applied by **`NetworkService.applyScenePrewire`** from a `createScene` hook (importing client only) keyed by `_stats.compendiumSource`; a pack card already imported says "In the world". Pure rules **`scripts/utils/scenesAtlas.js`** (utils, because the service reads it too): `SCENE_SHOW`, `SCENE_SORTS`, `scenePasses`, `sceneMatches`, `sortScenes`, `showCounts`, `shelfShut`, `shelfSummary`, `buildShelves`, `navShelf`, `ancestorKeys`, `subtreeKeys`, `shelfPaint`, `coverage`, `rangeIds`, `sanitizePrewire`, `togglePrewire`, `prewireFlags`, `sourceUuidOf`. CSS `ncm-net2-at*` / `ncm-net2-cg*`; a network NAME drawn in its colour is mixed `--net-ink` toward the text (70%, **36% on clean-light** — a network colour is picked for a dark screen). Contrast measured with `admin-networks-preview.html?view=scenes&pop=1&contrast=1` (+ `&paint=<net>`, `&sc=grid`, `&scsel=<id|pack uuid>`, `&open=all`; its walker parses rgb / color(srgb) / **oklab()** and waits for the CSS): 0 text nodes under 4.5:1 in clean-dark, clean-light and high-contrast across those states. Sims `test-scenes-atlas.mjs` (77: rules, the real `applyScenePrewire`, wiring) + `test-admin-networks` (480, the real tab incl. paint / Wire all / inspector / grid range). **Not live-tested** — in Foundry check the pack index loading, Import + its pre-wire, and the Esc listener.
- **Network Broadcast** (`_sendBroadcast`): Everyone online / on this scene / on <network>; every online GM and the sender are always whispered — `whisper: []` is PUBLIC in Foundry. Not built: moving a player by clicking their avatar. Sim `test-admin-networks.mjs` (333).
- **This scene = the RADIO DIAL (2026-10-28, dev-approved Version A of `dev/mockups/admin-networks-scene-modules-mock.html`)** — replaced the slider rows. Each network that reaches the scene is a 20-segment meter in its OWN configured colour (`--nc` inline; `--ncv` = the stroke/text version, pulled toward the text on clean-light); drag up/down or arrow keys (Shift ±10, Home/End) set its signal — **DOM only while dragging** (`_wireDialMeters` paints segments/thumb/number/quality/faces/aria the way the template does), ONE `updateNetwork({signalStrength})` on release, keys debounce to one write. The signal is the network's own, so it changes on every scene (the meter tooltip says so). Players on it ride the meter as faces; the flag = where players start (`dialStart` → `setSceneDefaultNetwork`, clicking the lit flag clears it — this replaced the "Players start on" select); on/off is the existing `sceneNet`; the lever is the existing `sceneDead` (dead zone hatches every meter and locks it out of the tab order, levels kept); a column header picks the readout (`dialPick`: type, reach, access in words, signal, who; Edit / Broadcast-to (`dialBcast` aims the broadcast picker + focuses the box) / Log). A foot line lists every online player and where they are (on here / on a network this scene can't reach / no signal / not reported) — it replaced the "elsewhere" card (the `elsewhere` / `defaultOptions` / `rows` keys stay for the sims). **Each meter's % is also a number box with ▲▼** (2026-10-28, dev: "an up and down arrow with a spot to enter a number"): `[data-dial-num]` (Enter / change commits, Esc reverts, clamped 0–100) and `[data-dial-step]` (±1, Shift ±10) go through the SAME `paint` + debounced `save` as a drag, and a drag never overwrites the box while it has focus; in a dead zone the box is plain text. **The dead-zone switch is emphasised** — a red "Dead zone" heading, a red edge + faint hazard stripes while live, a glowing striped red panel when on. Pure rules `tabs/networksDial.js` (`buildDial`, `dialSegments`, `signalQuality`, `crowdBottom`, `pctAtPointer`, `stepSignal`, `accessWords`); context `net2.scene.dial` + `net2.scene.foot`; CSS `ncm-net2-dial*`. Sim `test-networks-dial.mjs` (112); **Round 2026-10-28:** Scene + Broadcast share one row (`ncm-net2-scenerow`, stacks under ~610px); the picked readout is a band in the network colour with the signal in its quality tone (`qualityTone`) and access/reach as meaning pills (`accessTone`, `reachTone`), Edit filled, Broadcast/Log quiet. `admin-networks-preview.html` gained `?h=` (taller frame) and `?sel=` (dial pick).

## Architecture: Admin → Messages

Mock `dev/mockups/admin-messages-redesign-mock.html`; context `msg2`; pure rules `tabs/messagesModel.js`; CSS `ncm-msg2-*`.
- **Needs a reply** (replaces the NPC Inbox): one conversation per NPC inbox × the alias written to (`convKey`, shared with the Overview's NEEDS YOU item, whose primary button opens it via `openTo('npc:<key>')`). **Answered** = the GM's ✓ (world setting `npcMailAnswered`, `{baseId: iso}`, pruned) or a later sent copy to that player. Inline reply = `buildReply` + `sendMessage` as the "Reply as" pick; several waiting players → one fan-out; drafts per conversation.
- **Needs a reply is THE DESK** (2026-10-28, Version B of `dev/mockups/admin-needs-reply-rethink-mock.html`, dev-picked; it replaced the switchboard the same day — the switchboard's model/tab/template/CSS/sim are gone). Three columns: the **queue** (one row per NPC × the alias written to — NPC face, name + violet alias tag, the newest waiting subject, who is waiting as faces + names, a count, how long the oldest has waited and a **wait meter** that fills across a game day and turns red past it; answered rows below under their own line, dim; the chips All lines / Waiting / Answered + Include mail I sent; NPC inboxes nobody wrote to are a COUNT, not rows), the **conversation** (head = faces · subject · "Alias of X · with V & Rache · N messages" · a waiting flag · the inbox button; the thread as chat bubbles oldest-first with a day line whenever the day changes, player mail left, the NPC's replies right, the unanswered ones marked; under it the **reply box**: "Reply as <identity> <address> → to V / all N who wrote (one fan-out)", the draft (`data-msg2-draft data-key=@root…`, Ctrl+Enter, kept per conversation), Reply as X / Mark answered / anonymous / **Open in the full composer** (carries the draft + identity + reply-all), and a green "Sent as X to Y." line after an inline reply), and the **persona** of the identity being answered as (portrait, name, alias tag, address, role + org hue pills, **How they talk** = the contact's Voice via `voiceInfo` with "from <main>" when inherited — empty = a link into Contacts to add one — a pencil → `openAdminTab('contacts', {select})`, **Answer as** = every identity of that NPC with the one they wrote to first (`identityOrder`), a hint when you answer as someone else, and **What they last said** = the last 3 replies). Pure rules in `messagesModel.js`: `DESK_FILTERS`, `DESK_HOT_MS`, `deskPasses`, `waitMeter`, `deskQueue` (waiting rows longest-wait-first, answered most-recent-first, counts from every row), `deskDefaultKey` (the desk opens on the longest wait, else the latest answered), `deskThread` (day lines), `identityOrder`, `deskVoice`, `deskSentLine`. Tab: `_replyContext` → `{ filt, chips, playersWaiting, linesWaiting, quietN, queue, hasQueue, emptyText, desk }`, `_deskDetail(g)` → `{ head, entries, typedNotes, compose, persona }`; ops `convSel` / `rFilt` / `includeGm` / `deskIdent` (the Reply-as pick, a ScopePicker value) / `deskContact` / `replySend` / `markAnswered` / `replyAnon` / `replyComposer`. Inline partial `ncmMsg2DeskRow`; CSS `ncm-msg2-desk*` / `ncm-msg2-dq*` (amber waiting · red hot · violet alias · green answered; the pop-out widens the columns). **The half-built state bit the dev live (2026-10-28 evening): the agent building it was rate-limited after moving the tab + model to the Desk but before the template, so Needs a reply rendered EMPTY — a tab's context and its template must change in one step.** **Cleanup pass (2026-10-29, mock `dev/mockups/admin-desk-cleanup-mock.html`):** the queue head is ONE row — a **Show ▾** dropdown (All lines / Waiting / Answered with counts, plus Include mail I sent and Show quiet inboxes, op `deskQuiet`, default off) · "N waiting" · a fold button; the queue folds to a **rail of faces** with waiting counts (`deskFoldQ`); **Mark answered lives on the status it clears** — the thread head's amber "N waiting" IS the button (`.ncm-msg2-desk__wait`, hover/focus swaps to a green "✓ Mark answered"; an answered line shows a green "answered" tag); the reply box is the textarea + ONE bar: "as **<identity ▾>** → to V" (the identity is a dashed act-colour dropdown, `.ncm-msg2-desk__as`, only when there are several) (+ a violet anonymous tag, and anonymous recolours the whole Reply button violet) · a split **[Reply as X | N ▾]** (N = identities, tooltip lists what is behind it) — both open ONE inline partial `ncmMsg2DeskReplyMenu`, which holds Answer as (the identities, "they wrote here" on the one written to — the persona no longer lists them, it only warns when you answer as someone else), Send anonymously and Open in the full composer; the id-card button in the thread head folds the persona (`deskFoldP`). Folds live in a module-level `DESK_FOLDS` map keyed by the window id (`_deskFolds`), so they last the session per window; by default the queue is open and the persona is open only in a pop-out or a panel ≥ 960px wide. The dropdowns are the generic `ncm-msg2-dd` (<details>, `--down`/`--up`, closed by the same outside-click closer as Junk ▾); a `<summary>` primary button needs its own rule (the base `:is(button, summary).ncm-msg2-btn` is 0,2,1). Preview `admin-messages-preview.html` gained `?open=<selector>` to open those menus. Sim `test-messages-desk.mjs` (110: rules, wiring, a real Handlebars render incl. both folds); `test-admin-messages` drives the real tab (475). Not live-tested.
- **All mail = the CALENDAR NAVIGATOR (2026-10-28, dev-approved version A of `dev/mockups/admin-mail-navigator-mock.html`; B, the drawers, is a record — its quick shortcuts were borrowed).** The left column is a calendar you navigate INTO: **YEAR** (12 month tiles — a bar per week scaled to the year’s busiest week, amber where unread; count; unread / not-delivered badges; empty months dim; the game clock’s month edged in act) → **MONTH** (a cell per day tinted by volume against the month’s busiest day, count, amber dot = unread, red dot = not delivered, today underlined; WK buttons under the grid pick a whole week; the month’s mail lists under it) → **WEEK** / **DAY** (the grid folds into a 7-day Monday strip, the day’s mail split morning / afternoon / evening, newest band first). Breadcrumb (`2045 › August › Mon 14`, with `Week 33` when the day was opened from a week) walks back to any level; ‹ › (and ← →) step a sibling — next day / week / month / year, the foot names both neighbours with their counts; Esc / Backspace go up (a document listener with a containment guard, `_calKeys`, removed in onClose); Today · This week · This month jump on the game clock. **Rules are pure (`messagesModel.js`): `calendarCounts(rows)` = ONE pass → Maps of `{n, unread, failed}` per year / `year-month` / Monday week / UTC day (aggregates, never a mark per message — 10,000 rows is one loop); `yearTiles` / `monthCells` / `weekStrip` / `monthWeeks` build the levels, `calRange` / `inRange` the span, `calStateFor` / `calStep` / `calUp` / `calCrumbs` the navigation, `daySplit` / `orderBands` the bands, `isoWeek` / `weekMonthOf` (a week belongs to the month holding its Thursday).** State on the tab: `_calLevel/_calYear/_calMonth/_calWeek/_calDay/_calViaWeek`, `_calLimit` (the level’s list is paged 50, `calMore`), `_calAnim` (the entrance the NEXT render plays once — grow / fold / step-next / step-prev / up — stamped as `.is-fresh[data-cal-anim]` on `.ncm-msg2-cal__body`; vetoed by OS reduced motion and the module animation pref), `_calFollow` (`openTo(message)` lands on the day holding it, a base id resolving to its fan-out row), `_calAuto` (a search / Who / Filter change whose hits sit elsewhere jumps an EMPTY view to the newest hit’s month, or year). **Every filter narrows every count**: counts come from `res.filtered` (search · Who · Filter picker · the legacy date range, shown as a removable `dates` chip), so tiles, cells, badges and “N of M” (M = in this span; the tooltip adds the grand total) stay honest. The ScopePicker callbacks compare the value to what was rendered before acting (they fire on open too). Group by Day / Week / Month, the date folds and the Dates button are GONE from the UI (`periodOf` / `groupByPeriod` / `foldShut` stay exported — Inboxes folds and the sim use them). A **grip** under the month grid (`[data-cal-grip]`, `_calGridH`) drags the list taller (the grid scrolls when squeezed), double-click resets, ↑ ↓ nudge — DOM-only, remembered for the session. **The reading pane’s action row is ONE line** (`.ncm-msg2-acts--line`, never wraps): ONE **Reply as** (the identity the mail reached, then the NPC’s other identities — `_acts.replyAs`; one choice acts, several open `showNamePicker`, op `actReply`) · ONE **Inbox** (every copy’s mailbox, same rule, `actInbox`) · Share to chat · Copy to terminal · Junk ▾ · Delete — the quiet ones icon-only with tooltips at 820px (`.lb` hidden, `--quietact` wears `--icon`), labelled again in the pop-out. Pane typography was tightened to the left column’s vocabulary (15px display subject, label-tone keys, address tone, 13px body). CSS `ncm-msg2-cal*` (tokens only; the cell tint is capped at 28% act and the numbers take the heading tone, the amber badge is a tint on the light presets — measured with the preview’s `?contrast=1` walker). Sims `test-mail-periods.mjs` (67: the rules + wiring) and `test-admin-messages.mjs` (457: the real tab through every level, filters narrowing counts, paging, openTo, one-button acts, the real Handlebars render of each level — `NCM_DUMP` writes `msg2-all-<level>.html` for `admin-messages-preview.html?view=all&cal=year|month|week|day`). **Not live-tested.** Also **All mail**: `_gatherActivity` → `_collapseFanout` → `_mergeSentTwins`; the real sender of anonymous mail shown to the GM only.
- **The selection rule is the same on every tab (dev 2026-10-28):** a new selection opens its group ONCE; after that the GM's fold wins (Contacts `_dirSelKey`, Shards `_baySelSeen`, Terminals the ` sel` marker). Never force the selection's group open on every render — that made Shards spring back and Terminals ignore the click.
- **Default folds (dev-approved 2026-10-28) — one pure rule, `messagesModel.foldShut(key, {defaultOpen, shut, open, forced, holds})`**: All mail opens only the MOST RECENT period (`newestPeriodKey` — first group in Newest order, last in Oldest), the Inboxes table only Players (`inboxGroupDefaultOpen`); a closed head keeps its count + amber unread (+ a red `.f` not-delivered count on date heads, `groupByPeriod` → `failed`); the selection's group is forced open; a search / Who / dates / From / flag filter (or a non-All inbox view) opens everything; the GM's fold / unfold / fold-all is recorded in `_shut`+`_open` / `_ibShut`+`_ibOpen` (`recordFold`, a key in one set only) and wins for the session; a regroup resets them. Needs a reply and Outbox are untouched. Sims `test-mail-periods` (30), `test-inbox-groups` (21).
- **Outbox**: **Didn't arrive** = world setting `deliveryFailures` (cap 100) written by `MessageService.recordDeliveryFailure` (relay-failure GM toast + GM fan-out refusals); Retry re-sends to failed recipients only, never eddies. **Waiting to deliver**: the queue lives on the player's client — `_queueMessage` reports it to the GM (`SOCKET_OPS.MESSAGE_QUEUE_SYNC`), GM delivers through `handleMessageRelay` and `MESSAGE_QUEUE_CMD` tells the player to drop it (the old panel called methods that never existed). **Scheduled** · **Timers** (self-destruct; Stop clears it). Gaps: refusals on the player client before the relay aren't logged; cancelled held eddies aren't refunded.
- **Junk senders — spam by ADDRESS, not by folder** (`scripts/utils/junkSenders.js`, pure; sim `test-junk-senders.mjs` 26). `buildJunkMatcher({templates, list})` → `(email, metadata) → {reason, label, entry?}|null`: **generated** (`metadata.spamSource` — `createMessage` now PERSISTS spamSource/spamCategory/spamTemplateId; it dropped them before, so a player un-spamming generator mail erased all trace) · **template** (a spam template's sender; `{{tokens}}` match one-or-more chars) · **listed** (world setting `spamJunkSenders`: addresses and `@domain`s, `SpamService.setJunkSender(entry, on)` GM-only, `junkMatcher()` cached). All mail hides `isSpam || junk` unless **Spam & junk senders** is picked; rows wear a red **Junk sender** flag whose tooltip is the reason; the reading pane says why in ONE line, and the action row carries a **Junk ▾** branching button beside Share to chat (a native `<details>`, `.ncm-msg2-junkbtn*` — NOT `ncm-msg2-split`, which is the tab's list/detail layout) whose menu offers **This address** / **Everyone at @domain** (domain only for senders with no actor/contact; never a player's address, never anonymous or sent copies) and **Not junk** for listed ones (`junkOn`/`junkOff`; shown when `junkMenu`). **Delivery:** `_shouldSpamFilter` routes a LISTED sender to the recipient's Spam folder first, before the unknown-sender switch is even read (not anonymous / sent / broadcast). Template senders are only hidden in the admin feed, not re-routed. Not built: a list editor (entries are managed from the reading pane).
- **"Where it went" (2026-10-27, dev: "just use the where it went stuff from admin-guides-mock.html")** — the delivery line below is now DRAWN as that mock's journey: a "Where it went" band (route on the right), the steps as stops on one track (`messagesModel.journeyTrack` — the message's network slots in as a stop after Sent when it got that far; a held message shows its wait stop instead), the line's sentence + fixes in a tone strip (`.ncm-msg2-jr__why`, hidden for a finished green message with nothing to do), and the grid behind "More about it" (`dlToggle`, same open Set). A group is one row of segments per person (`journeySegs`: Sent · Delivered · Read · Replied) with the why + fix under a row and the foot below. `MessagesTab._journey` adds track/route/strip/segs on top of `_deliveryFor`. Stop/segment field names avoid helper names (`subline`). CSS `ncm-msg2-jr*` (root keeps `ncm-msg2-dl` for the toggle). Action row: **Junk ▾ and Delete live together in `.ncm-msg2-acts__end`** at the right; the Junk menu opens toward the side of the reading pane with room (`toggle` listener) and an outside click closes it (`_junkCloser`, removed in onClose). **Round 2 (same day):** the band is a button that FOLDS the whole journey (`jrFold`, `_jrShut` — one choice for every message this session; folded it keeps the route + a one-word status chip from `journeyStatus`, or for a group the single most important count). Colour hierarchy: the strip's lead words in its tone, the rest secondary; ONE filled button per strip (the first when none is marked), the others `ncm-msg2-btn--quiet` (neutral outline). Group segments join centre to centre (`.seg::before { left:-50%; right:50% }` — the first cut drew only half a cell and left gaps). **Any number of people** (`journeyPeople`): problems sort first (failed › held › unread › read › answered); the list scrolls (300px) under a sticky header; past `BIG_GROUP` (8) a proportional summary bar + filter chips Problems / Not read / Read / Everyone (`jrFilter`, DOM-only, `data-jr-f` on the journey + `data-b` per row, picked per message in `_jrFilter`; opens on Problems when there are any); past `SEARCH_GROUP` (12) a name search (`[data-jr-search]`, overrides the chip, "Nobody here" when empty). Chips carry their own message id (no `../` inside the nested blocks). Sim `test-mail-journey.mjs` (21).
- **Delivery line** (guide mocks `admin-guides-v4-mock.html?only=dl` / `v5?only=grp`, approved via `admin-guides-final.html`): the reading pane carries ONE line under From/To/When — pure `messagesModel.deliveryLine` / `groupDeliveryLine` (names escaped, the line prints raw), built in `_mailDetail` for the open message only. Green = read/answered/delivered to an NPC · amber = held / unread / copy gone · red = refused / not delivered; pips for one message, one square per person for a fan-out. Starts closed, toggles DOM-only (open ids kept on the tab). Opened: steps with times, why, what the player saw (a small screen block), what happens next, and fixes (`dl*` ops: forceDeliver, scene networks, `sendOnly` off/on, answer as, Retry = `retryDeliveryFailure`, open inbox, remove a dead member from a GM group). **Nudge** (new): online → re-sends `MESSAGE_NOTIFY`; offline → a whisper to that user only; remembered per session. **Held / undelivered mail now has its own All-mail rows** (from the reported queue + `deliveryFailures`; page-only buttons hidden). **`status.readAt`** (game time, `MessageRepository.readStamp()`) is stamped by `markAsRead` / `markAllAsRead` / the panel's mark-read writers and persisted field-by-field; old mail reads "read" with no time. Sim in `test-admin-messages.mjs` (318).
- **Inboxes = LIST + DESK** (2026-10-29, dev-picked A of `dev/mockups/admin-inboxes-rethink-mock.html`; prefix `ncm-ib-*`). It REPLACED the same-day **Mailroom** wall + intake belt (`admin-inboxes-postmarks-mock.html`, kept as a record) — the dev: "a bit messy to see and navigate": five stacked layers, two filter vocabularies, decoration over information, popovers and a drawer moving content under the cursor. Rules now: ONE toolbar row (search, `/` focuses it · **Show** select = `INBOX_VIEWS` Everyone / Has unread / Gone quiet (7d+) / Empty / Owner gone, with counts · **Group** select Kind / Role / Organization · **Sort** select = `INBOX_SORTS` · ⋯ = Mark everything read / Export all / Purge old mail / fold-open every group). The three selects are `data-msg2-ibsel` with CHANGE listeners in `_wireInboxes` (the select-vs-data-action gotcha). **Left**: the mailboxes grouped (players first, owner gone last, the standing fold rule: only Players open, the selected row's group forced open, a search / non-Everyone filter opens all; `data-msg2-ibfold` on `[data-ib-group]`), each row a `<button>` (`ibSel`, `data-owner-id` — `data-owner` is the TAB) = face · name (+ a violet `+N` alias tag) · **the line its sort uses** (`slotMetric`: "last mail 2h ago" / "2 unread · waiting 5d" / "9 in 14 days" / "quiet 20d" / "N mail") · an amber unread count or a gone / none / quiet tag. **Right, nothing picked = NEEDS A LOOK**: most unread (with how long the oldest has waited), gone quiet, owner gone — each row picks that mailbox. **Right, a mailbox picked**: Open inbox (or Fix in Orphan Audit) + Write as (NPC) / Write to (player) first; Latest mail (6, received + sent, each with a small network **postmark** — a cut-corner tag in the network's colour with its 3-letter `postmarkCode`), Last 14 days (`inboxTraffic`, amber = still unread, received / sent / unread), Networks it gets mail over, Talks to, Housekeeping (Mark read · Export · Purge old… · Delete inbox = `ibDelete`). Picking the same row again or Esc goes back to Needs a look. Sorting: Latest mail (default) · Most unread (ties → longest wait) · Busiest (14 days) · Quietest (never-mailed last) · A–Z; each option carries its meaning (`INBOX_SORTS` [key, label, hint]). The scan carries `network` + `encryption`. Sims `test-inbox-groups.mjs` (33) + `test-admin-messages.mjs` (485, the real tab + renders of Needs a look, a mailbox and an orphan); preview `admin-messages-preview.html?view=inboxes|inboxes-drawer`. Measured 0 text nodes under 4.5:1 in all three presets (the older avatar initials / nav counts excluded — they are on the contrast to-do). **Not live-tested.**

## Architecture: Admin → Terminals

Mock `dev/mockups/admin-terminals-redesign-mock.html`; context `term2`; CSS `ncm-term2-*`. Filters all / logged in / needs you / secured / this scene / not pinned (+ a scene scope picker over pinned scenes); **Needs you** = a live lockout, an armed resident, heat ≥ 25, a trace countdown, or `gmBypassed`. Groups location / pinned scene / org / folder / preset / none (sticky, foldable). **No summary header** (2026-10-28): the toolbar is search · **View** · Select · Import · Pop out — View is ONE button ("View  By location · Name ▾") whose menu has two radio sections, Group by and Sort; the menu is rendered in the template and moved to document.body by `_mountViewMenu` (a portal carrying `.ncm-app` + the window's admin theme, one per window keyed by the app id, closed by an outside pointer-down or Esc; state `_viewOpen/_group/_sort` on the tab, so a pop-out keeps its own). The quick filter chips stay visible under the toolbar. Detail = the rack layout below. Reset (`relock`) is a header button; ⋯ = force-unlock / place on this scene / export / Security tab / remove; Select mode = bulk reset / export (`{ncmTerminals:1, terminals:[…]}`) / remove. `openTo(itemIdOrUuid)`. Sim `test-admin-terminals.mjs` (381).
- **The rack + information detail (2026-10-28, dev-approved mock `dev/mockups/admin-terminals-rack-modules-mock.html`, Version A).** LIST = a server rack per group (`.ncm-term2-rack`, two railed sides): each terminal a rack unit (`.ncm-term2-ru`) = icon tile (the item art as a background image, else the preset glyph, tinted by the organisation hue) · name · "location · N accounts" (wraps, never cut) · four lights (`leds`: green someone in · red blinking a live lockout · amber an armed plant · violet every account secured; reduced motion stops the blink) · an 8-segment heat bar off the hottest operator (`maxHeat`, `_maxHeat`) · a red trace timer (`traceTag`) while a countdown runs; a legend with per-light machine counts sits above. Filters, grouping, search, select mode (checkbox replaces the rack screw) and pop-out are unchanged. DETAIL (`_buildDetail`, only the SELECTED terminal pays) = labelled modules with corner ticks (`.ncm-term2-mod`): **header** (icon tile, name, location · org · preset, ONE status line in words `statusParts`, Open / Configure / Reset + the ⋯ menu); **Right now** (`nowRows`, one aligned row each — icon · what · time · the button it needs: sessions → Log out, lockouts (live SecurityService state OR replayed from the Security log via `triesFromLog`) → Clear, armed plants → Defuse (`onDefuse`, works on carried terminals), the trace countdown → Call off, heat ≥ 25 → Clear / Escalate; Call off / Clear / Escalate delegate to the Security tab handlers with the same data attributes; nothing → "Nothing happening."); **Accounts table** (Account · Lock · Status · Last — lock in words via `_lockParts`, first bypass skill + the full list in the tooltip, violet lock / green open; status = who is in / who is out + time left / tries left / quiet; last login from `getLastLogin`); a row click expands its facts DOM-only (`[data-acct-row]`, kept per account in `_acctOpen`; the first account with activity opens by default): Belongs to (linked contact or character + address), Lock (full), Mailbox (`getAccountMessages(…, {enforceGates:false, includeShared:false})` → messages · unread · files), Lockout rule (tries → lockout · used), Logins here (last 3), and Edit the lock / Add a lock, **Open terminal** (there is no GM path to read one account's mailbox, so the button says what it does), Reset tries (`acctResetAll`, every character with tries or a lockout there); **Recent logins** (`_attempts`: login footprints + the Security log's misses, replayed so the miss that hit the limit reads "locked out"; character + "(player)" from `getPlayerCharacters`, → account, result, when — never a method); **Setup** (the old key/value facts). The per-character party boxes are gone; `accountStatus.js` still supplies the rules. No odds / chances anywhere in the tab. Sim `test-admin-terminals.mjs` (381); preview `admin-terminals-preview.html?ctx=…&h=1500&open=<accountId>&t=…`. **Colour pass (same day, dev: "hard to tell each account from another"):** every account is its own block (`.ncm-term2-acc`: zebra card/pane-alt + a separator, an edge and a swatch in the name's hue, the expanded facts an inset card edged in that hue); tiers = name heading · meta label · counts `--ncm-text-num` · times `--ncm-text-time` · states their meaning pulled toward the heading tone (`--t2-good/bad/attn/secret/act`, so they hold 4.5:1 on tinted rows); buttons sit on bg-card (the canvas failed on clean-light). `?contrast=1` on the preview walks every text node: 0 under 4.5:1 in clean-dark and clean-light.
- **Round 2026-10-28:** rack units show location as a hue `.ncm-tagp` (pin / building / hand icon, `placeReal`/`placeIcon`) + an account-count chip; accounts ALL start folded (no default-open); each account row leads with its portrait (`_portrait()`, initials `avText` in the name hue) and its Admin/Hidden/Private tags sit inline after the name, not by the chevron.
- **Default rack folds (2026-10-28, dev-approved rule):** first group open, the rest closed; a NEW selection opens its group once (marker `' sel'` in the same fold memory; skipped while a search/filter/scene pick opens everything, so clearing it restores the GM's folds) and after that the GM can fold it — only a search/filter forces groups open (dimmed chevron, "Open while a search or filter is on"); a search / non-All filter / scene pick opens every group; the GM's own fold choices last for the session, per window and per grouping (`rackFoldMemory(app.id, group)` — module-level, so a pop-out keeps its own and reopening the panel keeps them; picking a grouping no longer clears them). A CLOSED head shows its count plus count chips, needs-you first: red locked out (blinking LED) · trace · amber armed · heat 25+ · GM-unlocked · green in · violet all locked — count chips rather than a light-per-unit strip because they stay a fixed width in the 334px column and can carry heat/trace; a closed head that needs you gets a red/amber left edge. Pure rules `tabs/terminalsRack.js` (`rackFoldState`, `rackHeadSummary`); CSS `ncm-term2-gsum*`. "Right now" never folds. Dump a busy closed head with `NCM_DUMP_SEL=bbs NCM_DUMP_SHUT='Arasaka Tower'`.
- **Lock odds in the account editor** ("How hard for your party", Configure → Accounts → an account → Security): `_paintLockOdds` reads the editor's unsaved DOM (`_readLockFromEditor`) and paints from the pure `scripts/utils/lockOdds.js` — per character from `getPlayerCharacters()`, base = `SkillService.getSkillData` stat + level (role-ability rank mode respected), best bypass row with its own DV, plain d10 chance "needs N+"; ≤4 characters = cards, 5+ = summary + best-first list with can't-rolls folded; Require login off = one line. Never re-renders the sheet. CSS `ncm-lodds-*` in terminal.css. A skill the actor lacks counts as can't roll (performCheck would allow stat-only). Sim `test-lock-odds.mjs` (65).

## Architecture: Admin → Shards

Mock `dev/mockups/admin-shards-redesign-mock.html`; context `shards2` (+ the shared `shards` — every shard, UNFILTERED — and `shardSummary` for Overview/HUD); CSS `ncm-sh2-*`. **No header / census strip any more (2026-10-28, dev: "take out the top")** — `shardSummary` is still produced for the Overview + HUD. **ONE toolbar row**: search (✕ = `clearQuery`) · **View ▾** · **Filter ▾** … Select · **+ New ▾** (blank / convert / Import JSON / presets) · **⋯** (relock all / Copy player shards to Items / unconvert / pop out / purge bricked); Select swaps the row for the bulk bar (relock/export/unconvert). View + Filter copy the Terminals tab's View menu: rendered in the tab, mounted on `document.body` as a portal by `_mountPop` (`.ncm-sh2-dd`, keyed per window by app id, outside-click / Esc close, `data-pick` listeners → `_pickPop`). **View** = radio sections Group by (Holder default · Maker · Preset · Folder · State · No groups) / Held by (Anyone · World items · Characters) / Sort (Name · State · Last opened · Integrity, lowest); the button reads "By holder · Name". **Filter** = multi-select checkboxes with counts (taken over search + holder): State (Locked · Black ICE · Breached · Open · Bricked — OR together) and Has (Unclaimed eddies · Damaged · Player-made — AND); `_filters` is a Set (was the single `_filter`), the button carries a count badge, active filters + a non-default Held by show as removable pills under the toolbar with "Showing N of M" + Clear all, and the no-match state names what hid everything (`noMatchWhy`) with a Clear button. Then the **Player Authoring strip** (On: facts from `resolveEncoderConfig` + `gateSummary`, Open Player Authoring; Off: Set up + Turn on) → list + detail. **The list is CHIP CARTRIDGES (2026-10-28, dev-approved mock `dev/mockups/admin-shards-modules-mock.html`, frame B-left)**: pure rules in `tabs/shardsModel.js` (`cartridgeView`, `notchStates`, `integTier`, `entryState`, `integrityView`, `peekLights`, `bayColor`); each shard a cut-corner cartridge (`.ncm-sh2-slot > .ncm-sh2-cart`) with contact pins on the left in a socket rail, item art (chip glyph when none / it fails to load — `onRender` swaps it), name, meta (holder in its hue · files · integrity %), five layer notches + state light + word, and a full-height integrity strip in its tier colour (striped when integrity is off). Bays per View group (header band with a rotating chevron, count, fold hint); a bay FOLDS DOM-only (`group` op toggles `.is-shut`, grid-rows 1fr↔0fr) and keeps a row of state lights (22 then +N); a search opens every bay; each bay scrolls past ~6 (scroll memo per bay). Selecting EJECTS the cartridge 10px toward the detail with a caret (`_ejectLive` swaps on the live DOM, `_replayEject` replays old→new on the re-rendered nodes, the detail slides in with `.is-fresh`); bulk mode puts a tick box where the caret was. Detail: header (art, name, state pill filled in its meaning colour, holder hue, preset, folder, Made by / GM-made, Editable, Hardened…, last touched; Open · Configure · Relock · Permissions (world items) · Export · ⋯ force decrypt / pop out / **Turn integrity off** / unconvert) → bands Breach path · Integrity · Entries. **Integrity = the A2 ladder track** on the real data (`integrityView` over the resolved config + `effectiveHit`): tier + cur/max, "N more failed hacks to <tier>, M to bricked", the segmented cell with a needle, a clickable tier scale (`setShardIntegrity` to the tier's top), Damage stepper (`igStep`, the existing `damageShard` + roll data loss), Repair +repairPts (`repairIntegrity`), Full (`restoreShardIntegrity`), Brick (`setIntegrity(…,0)` after a confirm), Salvage when bricked (`salvageShard(item,'GM',true)`), and a history from `cfg.history` with tier crossings; off → "Turn on" (`updateConfig({integrity:{enabled}})`). Entries: content-type icon + state badge (whole / part-lost / lost from `corruption`), encrypted (violet + DV), eddies (gold), a mini battery whose cells set the damage (`setShardEntryDamage`). Contrast measured with `admin-shards-preview.html?audit=1` (≥ 4.5:1 in clean-dark / clean-light / high-contrast; `?fold=N`, `?pop=view|filter`, `?sel=filt`, `?h=`). **Not live-tested.** **Security = the BREACH PATH** (mock `dev/mockups/admin-shard-security-mock.html`, option A, dev-picked): each layer a round stop from the outside in (network wifi · key item key · login user-lock · encryption lock / ICE skull), ending at a Contents stop; the line is green as far as cleared, the next lock has an amber ring, ICE is red with a halo; a summary line ("2 of 3 open — next: Black ICE") + meter above it. Each stop's tooltip says what the lock IS ("Only reachable on CitiNet", "Black ICE at DV 20 — a failed breach hits the runner") and what clicking does; the click is the unchanged `toggleShardLayer`. Four layers shrink the stops (`is-tight`); past that the path scrolls sideways. CSS `ncm-sh2-path*` / `ncm-sh2-pathsum*` (the old `ncm-sh2-chain/layer` rules are gone). **Export/Import** = `{ncmShards:1, shards:[{name,img,type,config,entries}]}` (Import creates new world items → `convertToDataShard` → `addMessage`); **Unconvert goes through `removeDataShard(item, false)`** (restores name/art). Bulk "Move to…" dropped; a "prices to approve" queue NOT built. Sims `test-admin-shards.mjs` (137), `test-shards-cartridges.mjs` (103). **Round 2026-10-28:** cartridge icons match their detail counterparts (chip tile in the shard's name hue, notches = breach-path stop colours incl. the amber "next" ring, state word via `stateTone`, PM badge via `madeByLabel`); entries are colour-coded by `ENTRY_HUES` (shardsModel — each content type → a `--ncm-hue-*`, eddies = `--ncm-money`; the raw content accents collide with meaning colours) with a left edge + icon tile + zebra rows; Breach path / Integrity / Entries bands fold DOM-only (op `sec`, `_secShut`, `d.shut`, role=button div because Entries holds a button). **Default folds (2026-10-28, dev-approved rule):** only the FIRST bay (World items, else the first) starts open, every other bay closed (count + state lights still show); a NEW selection opens its bay once (`_baySelSeen`, recorded as an opened choice; skipped while a search opens everything) and after that the GM's own fold wins — the bay holding the selection CAN be folded and stays folded — a search opens all, and the GM's own fold/unfold choices win for the session (`_collapsedGroups` / `_openedGroups`, pure `shardsModel.bayShut`; Group by resets them). The three detail bands all START folded (`_secShut = new Set(DETAIL_SECTIONS)`) and a folded band shows a one-line summary beside its heading (`d.sum` from `pathSummary` / `integSummary` / `entsSummary` → parts `{text, tone}`, inline partial `ncmSh2Sum`, CSS `.ncm-sh2-sec__sum` hidden while open, ellipsised, the band's open-state extras hidden while shut): "1 of 2 open · next: Encryption · 2 failed attempts" / "50% · Degraded" or "Off" / "4 files · 1 damaged · 1 encrypted · 500 eb unclaimed", meaning colours only. Preview `?open=path,integ,ents` / `?bays=all`.

## Architecture: countermeasure visibility + Admin → Security

**Countermeasure visibility** (mock `dev/mockups/countermeasure-visibility-mock.html`): everyone sees THAT a thing is locked and the ways in; only an expert or a good scan sees what guards it (ICE, damage, trace, attempts) and, separately, the DV. Pure rules `scripts/utils/countermeasures.js` (`resolveCountermeasures(worldCfg, override, facts)` → open | unknown | spotted | identified + showDV/canScan/show.*; `cmvReadout` — an unknown value is NEVER in the DOM). Foundry side `scripts/utils/countermeasureView.js` (`getCmvConfig`, `isCmvExpert` = `canOperate(…,{standalone:true})`, an EMPTY expert rule = nobody; `cmvForViewer`; override editor `cmvOverrideContext` / `wireCmvOverride` / `readCmvOverride`). World setting `countermeasureVisibility` (default `{mode:'open'}` = unchanged behaviour). Per-object override `{visibility, scanDV, dv}` on `network.security.countermeasures`, `account.security.countermeasures` and shard `config.countermeasures` — ONE partial `templates/partials/countermeasure-override.hbs` (`TEMPLATES.CMV_OVERRIDE`) in the network editor's Access step, the terminal account editor and the shard config Security tab. Player side: `NetworkAuthDialog` (networks + terminal accounts) renders the readout + a collapsible "Scan the defences" strip (`performCheck` + Roll Reveal, remembered per actor+object on this client for the session, one try, traced only if configured); Roll Reveal **`hideDV`** shows "DV ??" until the verdict. Shards: lock screen / hack header / breach dialog hide ICE, attempts and DV for a layperson (no scan strip on shards yet). GM always identified. CSS `styles/components/countermeasures.css`. Sim `test-countermeasures.mjs` (122).

**Admin → Security** = five sub-tabs: Live (stats, ONE "Needs your call" queue, latest rows) · Activity (the seismograph + the full log; the log is marked seen only while open) · Heat (thermometers + countdown clocks) · Machines (plants + recycle bin) · Hacking rules (fold cards: Netrunning resolution, Operator access, Countermeasure visibility, Role ability rolls). Folds are DOM-only. `openTo(view)`. Every older `security*` action kept. `fa-dice-d10` (not in FA free) → `fa-dice-d20` everywhere. Sim `test-admin-security.mjs` (216).

**Security modules (2026-10-28, dev-approved mocks `admin-security-heat-modules-mock.html` + `admin-security-seismograph-mock.html`, version A of each).** Rules in the pure `scripts/ui/AdminPanel/tabs/securityModel.js`; contexts `security.thermo` / `security.sg`, built only while Security AND that view are on screen. **Heat = thermometers**: one gauge per operator (hottest object = the fill, a side tick per object, a red SVG clock ring when a countdown is closing in on them — the arc starts at 12 via the `transform` ATTRIBUTE, not CSS), click a gauge (`heatPick`) → the Selected pane: every object (`heatObj` picks one), the countdown + Call off, and Adjust / Clear / Escalate through the EXISTING `securityHeatAdjust` / `securityHeatClear` / `securityEscalate` (no object picked = act on the hottest; Clear = `heatClearActor`, every object). Countdowns now record `startedAt` + `minutes` (older ones guess the ring's length). **Activity = the seismograph**: the `traceLog` as a needle line over Last hour / Session (6 h) / Day of GAME time — a failure kicks it (height = heat, damped ringing), a success is a tick on the rail; Trace = Combined / Coloured (who hues) / Per character (a lane each). The toolbar (Who / Target / Severity / Result / Action + Trace, and the log's Sort) is seven **ScopePickers** (`_sgIds()`, per-window ids, counts = "what you'd see if you picked only this"); what narrows the view shows as removable pills (`sgUnpick`, `sgClearAll`). Hover = nearest event tooltip, click = that event ±4 min (`sgSpike`), drag = a range (`sgBrush`) — all in `_wireSeismograph` (document listeners once per window via an AbortController, DOM-only until release); Key is DOM-only (`sgKey`). The log under it keeps ↗ deep links + pagination, a row click finds it on the trace (`sgSel`), a name/target narrows (`sgWho` / `sgObj`), and page 1 carries the "N new since your last look" divider. **`securityLogSeen` is now stamped on the GAME clock** (trace rows are game time; the real-time stamp never compared), and the tab snapshots it when Activity opens (`_lookFrom`) so the marker holds while the GM works the view. CSS `ncm-sec-admin-band / -mod / -hx* / -sg* / -sglog*`. Sim `test-security-modules.mjs` (235); preview `admin-security-preview.html?ctx=<dump.json>&key=activity|activityPicked|heat` renders a real context (`NCM_DUMP=… node dev/tools/sims/test-security-modules.mjs`). **Not live-tested.**

## Player Contact Manager — last interaction (2026-08-14)

The detail panel's **Last Interaction** card and the **Recent** sort are live (they were TODO placeholders for years). `MessageRepository.getLastMessagesByCounterparty(actorId)` scans the actor's mailbox ONCE and indexes the newest message per counterparty three ways (`byEmail` lowercased / `byActorId` / `byContactId`) — the counterparty is always the OTHER side (sender on received mail, recipient on a `-sent` copy). `MessageRepository.pickLatestFor(index, {email, actorId, masterContactId})` (static) resolves one contact against it, newest across the three keys. `ContactManagerApp._prepareContext` builds the index per render (`_lastMsgIndex`), `_lastMessageFor(contact)` feeds both the `recent` sort (no-history contacts last, A→Z) and the selected contact's card (`{subject, timeAgo}` via the shared `_relativeTime` pattern). Also usable one-off: `getLastMessageWith(actorId, contact)`. Sim coverage in `dev/tools/sims/test-footprints.mjs`.

## Quick Import Actor → contact

Admin → Contacts → New → **From an actor…** opens `ActorImportPickerApp` (NPC actors not yet linked to a master contact, live search; rows hide via the `hidden` attribute + a `[hidden]{display:none !important}` rule — the row's own `display:flex !important` beats an inline `style.display`). A pick opens the Contacts tab's create form pre-filled from the actor (`openAdminTab('contacts', {select:{create:'actor', actorId}})`). Enter picks the single match, else the first name-prefix match, else the top row.

## Tools tab — Purge Messages (scoped) + Orphan Audit

Admin → Tools → Danger Zone → **Purge Messages** (`purgeMessages` action → `ToolsTab.onPurgeMessages`) is a **scoped** purge, not all-or-nothing. It enumerates targets — character + contact inboxes (via `MessageRepository.identifyInbox`, with per-inbox message counts) and data shards (via the `isDataShard` flag, world + actor-owned, counted by `DataShardService.getShardMessages`) — and presents a grouped `<select>` (plus an "ALL inboxes" option) in a `Dialog`; empty targets are skipped. A confirm dialog shows the exact count, then it deletes. `_purgeInboxJournal(journalId)` deletes only message pages (`flags.<MODULE>.messageId`) so the empty inbox survives; `_purgeShard(uuid)` removes entries via `DataShardService.removeMessage` so the shard item survives. Deletions fire the normal document-sync hooks, so open viewers update. (Read the select via `html.find(...)` in the Dialog callback — `FormDataExtended` is unreliable.)

**Orphan Audit** (`orphanAudit` → `ToolsTab.onOrphanAudit`, 2026-08-14) replaced the two dead Danger-Zone buttons: "Reset Module" (a double-confirm that ended in "not yet implemented") and "Rebuild Index" (called `repository.rebuildIndex()`, **which never existed** — optional chaining swallowed it and it reported success; don't re-add either without a real implementation). The audit scans for NCM data whose owner is gone — inbox journals whose actor/master-contact no longer exists (`_gatherOrphans` via `identifyInbox` + `masterContactService.getContact`), scene pins pointing at deleted terminal items (`flags.<MODULE>.terminalItemId`), and `traceHeat` entries keyed to deleted actors — lists them in a Dialog, and deletes/prunes on confirm.

## Architecture: who may author shards (the encoder gate)

**There is ONE answer — `canEncode(actor)` (helpers.js) — and every door reads it.** The Agent's Encode pill (`MessageViewerApp` context flag), the item right-click + convert button + unconvert (`ShardSheetOverride.canAuthor`), the Program Deck's blank cartridge, the `game.nightcity.openEncoder` funnel, and `ShardEncoderApp._permissionProblem()` (which runs on open AND again before the roll, because the operator is switchable from inside the window). A character who does not qualify sees **no button**, never a button that opens and refuses.

It returns `{ok, reason, off}` — `off` distinguishes "the GM has not switched player authoring on" (a world setting) from "this character does not qualify" (a gate the GM wrote), because the fixes differ.

**The gate is `encoderConfig.gate`, byte-for-byte the shape of `DEFAULTS.OPERATOR_GATE`, evaluated by the same `canOperate`** — mode / logic / roles+roleLogic / skill+min / itemGroups (OR-of-ANDs) / actors allowlist / allowGM. No second gating dialect. It is a **separate object** from the terminal operator gate on purpose, so "anyone may operate a terminal, only a Tech may forge a shard" is sayable.
- **`canOperate` gained `{ standalone: true }` for exactly this.** Its normal path merges `DEFAULTS ← the operatorGate SETTING ← override`, so passing the shard gate as a plain override would silently import the terminal's rule for every field the shard gate omits — two gates that look independent in the UI but are not. It also takes `label` (default `'Operator tools require'`); the encoder passes `'Encoding a shard requires'`.
- **Gated with nothing configured is INERT and says so.** `gateSummary()` reports `{gated, inert, parts}` and the GM card prints the live answer ("Right now a character qualifies by: **Tech / Netrunner OR Interface 4+**"). No accidental lockout.
- Sanitising lives in `sanitizeGate` inside the pure `EncoderModel.js` (sim-testable): unknown mode → `anyone`, empty groups and nameless entries **dropped** (an unsatisfiable gate reads as a broken button), rank coerced to a non-negative integer.
- GM card: **Admin → Shards → Player Authoring → "Who can make them"** tab. Role/gear/character choosers are the searchable **`showNamePicker`** dialog, not `<select>`s.

**`ShardConversionFlow` is RETIRED (2026-09-22)** — the file, `templates/dialogs/shard-conversion.hbs`, and both `shard-conversion.css` copies (the admin-panel one was already an orphan, never imported) are deleted, along with `TEMPLATES.SHARD_CONVERSION`. The three settings only it read — **`playerShardFloor`, `playerShardSkill`, `playerBlackICE`** — are no longer registered: a `config: true` setting nothing reads is a knob in Module Settings that silently does nothing. Their Setting documents stay on disk, inert (the namespace-migration precedent). `playerBlackICE` had already been orphaned; the Encoder asks `encoderConfig.lock.blackIce`. The old two-route statics (`encoderEnabled` / `playerAuthoringOn`) are gone with it — one route, one question.

**`scripts/utils/namePicker.js` is THE searchable world picker — every dropdown over a world collection goes through it (2026-10-20).** `showNamePicker({title, hint, entries, allowNone, selected, chips, placeholder, taken})` + `documentEntries(docs, {group:'folder'|'type'|fn, sub, note, fallbackImg})` + `folderPathOf(doc)` + the pure `matchesPicker(entry, q, group)`. Tester: "it's tedious to scroll through hundreds of items… a search, filter or default directory." Rows carry the Foundry **folder path** as a sub-line, **filter chips** are one per top-level folder (or type) with counts, search matches name OR path, Enter picks the first visible row, `allowNone` adds a leading clear row (resolves `{none:true}`), `selected` highlights + scrolls. Routed: Admin → Shards preset Quick Create and Terminals → Import (world items), the composer's three "Link a Scene" chips (ONE `_pickScene` helper), the dossier Linked actor + payload macro (hidden input + read-only label row `.ncm-picker-row`, repainted by `_paintPickerLabels` after edit-prefill), the contact form's Linked Actor (the partial is shared by the player Contact Manager AND the GM Contact Manager — both register `pickLinkedActor` and pass `linkedActorName`), the terminal's Download-to-Agent / Forward targets. **Rule: a `<select>` is for a short enum (skills, networks, folders, players, the handful of ICE actors); anything that lists `game.items/actors/scenes/macros` uses the picker** — `dev/tools/sims/test-world-picker.mjs` (69 checks) greps for a whole-collection `.map(… <option`. It is an `adminDialog` (`.ncm-pkr-dialog`, admin-preset themed, every colour a `--ncm-*` token — the literal greys that were near-white on clean-light are gone; chips are display-font 10px/700 uppercase 2px like the rest of the panel). It had been written four times before this and the copies had drifted. **It filters with inline `style.display`, never the `hidden` attribute** — `[hidden]`'s UA rule loses to the picker row's own `display:flex`, which is why the Encoder config's blank-item search silently did not filter (the ActorImportPicker trap). A chosen entry renders **greyed, not hidden** (`is-taken`), and the click is refused in JS. **Second sweep (same day, dev: "cover all our bases") — eight more surfaces, and the picker is now the ONLY list-of-world-names dialog in the module:** the GM Contact Manager's two **Share-to-character `<select>`s listed EVERY actor in the world** (a template `{{#each}}`, so the JS-only guard never saw it — `_pickShareTarget`, Players chip first then top folders); Tools → **Purge Messages** (a grouped select over every inbox + shard → picker with Everything / Character / Contact / Shard chips, the ALL-inboxes row kept); the **Encoder's key-item picker** (its copy filtered with the `hidden` attribute — the search box never worked); the terminal **burn target** (Carried / World chips, shards tagged first); the terminal-account and both config-sheet (**shard + Network Manager**) key-item pickers; the Security tab's **gate choosers** (whose private `_availableItems` copy silently CAPPED the list at 150 — now `worldItemEntries`/`worldRoleEntries`/`itemImgFor`/`roleImgFor` from the picker); and the network-auth **credential picker** (`scripts/utils/itemPicker.js` is DELETED — literal brand hex in an inline `<style>`, list rebuilt per keystroke; its world variant was already dead code). **`showNamePicker({ admin: false })` for a player-facing caller** (Encoder, terminal viewer, network auth): a plain Dialog whose tokens resolve from `:root` = the player theme; GM tools keep the default admin dialog. `isCarrier(item)` / `NON_CARRIER_TYPES` are exported as the one gear filter. Also fixed on the way: **`NetworkAuthDialog` read `game.user.character` in six places** (a player owning several PCs authenticated and was lockout-tracked as the ASSIGNED one) — it now uses `resolveOperator()` like every viewer. Left as-is because they already search: Admin → Shards → Convert Item (search + per-owner tabs + type filter), View Inbox, Actor Import, the terminal account's Link Identity. The sim guards all of it: no picker-row builder outside namePicker.js, no `.hidden = !!q` filter, no `{{#each …actors}}<option`, itemPicker gone, `admin:false` → no admin class. **Redesign (same day, dev-approved mock `dev/mockups/name-picker-redesign-mock.html`, left = old / right = shipped):** the picker now owns **`styles/components/name-picker.css` under the `ncm-pkr-*` prefix** (dialog class `ncm-pkr-dialog`) — the old `.ncm-burn-dialog` host was a CLASS COLLISION with the Email Setup burn-confirm's content wrapper (email-setup.css forces `* { margin:0 !important }` + literal greys under that class, and it bled into every picker). What changed on screen: sticky **folder headers** in the list while All is active (hidden the moment a chip or query narrows it — the "default directory" without a click), a search **toolbar** with a live shown/total count + clear, chips as **spans** in one scrolling row (a `<button>` in an admin dialog is restyled by admin-theme.css with `!important`), **↑↓ walks a cursor and ⏎ picks it** (Enter with no cursor still picks the first live row), the match highlighted in each name, an empty state that offers "Clear search" / "Search all groups", and a status strip (keys; in multi mode "N selected · select all shown"). The JS contract is unchanged — no caller changed. Tokens only, so admin presets and the player theme (`admin:false`) both drive it. Probe: `dev/mockups/name-picker-probe.html?t=…&mode=multi&q=&cursor=` mounts the REAL `showNamePicker` against the real cascade. Sim: `dev/tools/sims/test-world-picker.mjs`.

Sim: `dev/tools/sims/test-shard-gate.mjs` (34 checks — gate shape, the full `canEncode` matrix, gate independence in BOTH directions, and that the retired wizard left nothing behind).

## Architecture: shard theming

**The shard palette is DATA, not CSS.** Each `SHARD_PRESETS` entry carries an 11-key `palette` (the `COLOR_VAR_MAP` keys); `buildShardThemePrefs(theme, presetKey, {textScale, contrast})` (helpers.js, the twin of `buildTerminalThemePrefs`) layers the shard's own overrides on top; `ItemInboxApp._applyShardTheme` applies it element-scoped through `ThemeService.applyTheme`. The twenty `--sp-*` tokens are **derived from `--ncm-*` in ONE place** (the block at the top of item-inbox.css, shared with `.ncm-data-shard-composer` and `.ncm-malware-workshop`).

This replaced `styles/themes/shard-presets.css` (deleted), which hardcoded all twenty tokens for each of eight presets. Three things that fixed:
1. **The per-shard contrast slider did nothing on 8 of the 9 presets.** Contrast writes `--ncm-*`; a preset's hardcoded `--sp-*` never read them.
2. **`--sp-accent` read `var(--ncm-color-secondary, #19f3f7)` — a variable that does not exist**, so even the blank preset ignored the player's accent and fell through to literal brand cyan (same for `--sp-bar`, `--sp-chip-active-text`).
3. **Typed content could never follow the shard's theme** — the 18 content types render on `--ncm-*` by design.

Verified by `dev/mockups/shard-theme-probe.html` (renders all nine presets through the real ThemeService into the real cascade, diffs every token against the old stylesheet, reports worst drift in `<title>`): **10 of 11 mapped tokens reproduce EXACTLY**; the only drift is `--sp-text-muted`, now a `color-mix` of textMuted toward the accent so one formula reproduces each preset's tinted footer (Δ29–78, same hue family). Small drift for a clearer derivation was the agreed trade.

**`COLOR_VAR_MAP` gained `border` + `textMuted`** — shard presets carry a tinted border and footer as identity. `CONTRAST_SPREAD` had already tone-mapped both under those exact key names, so this closed a half-built contract. No THEME_PRESET defines them, so the player theme is unchanged until someone sets an override, and `variables.css` keeps the `:root` defaults. Adding a key here grows the player Theme Customizer grid and the terminal Theme tab automatically — they all iterate the map.

**Where things are written, and why:**
- The **palette goes on the APP ROOT**, not `.ncm-item-inbox`: the window FRAME sits outside the viewer element and must follow the shard (`.ncm-app.ncm-item-inbox > .window-header` — **compound**, because this window's app root carries both classes while the template root repeats `ncm-item-inbox`; the descendant form is the inner element), and `.ncm-item-inbox` owns `--ncm-text-scale` as a calc of two knobs that `applyTheme` would stomp with a single number. Text scale is passed as the **composed** player×shard product so the header matches the body.
- **Contrast is applied separately, onto the inbox, with `{ baseFrom: appRoot }`** so the tone-map spreads THIS shard's colours (or the player's, when the shard has none) rather than the brand constants `applyTheme`'s own contrast pass falls back to. The viewer therefore passes `contrast: 100` into `buildShardThemePrefs`.
- `config.theme` has **three legal shapes** and the builder tolerates all of them with no migration: the palette object (`{colors, fonts, wallpaper, wallpaperStrength}`), the dead `'classic'`/preset-key **string** `_onSaveConfig` used to write, and the preset **CHROME block** (`accent`/`headerBg`/`watermarkIcon`/`colorTemp`) the Encoder writes — which has no `colors` key, so they coexist.

**Config → Theme tab** (`item-inbox-config.hbs` `data-panel="theme"`, wired by `ItemInboxConfig._wireThemeTab`). Live preview strip, preset cards, an 11-key colour grid with per-key reset, font selects, Text Size + Contrast (moved here from Identity), wallpaper + strength, and the two libraries.
- **The draft contract: theme controls are UNNAMED and data-keyed (`data-stc`), mutate `this._themeDraft`, and NEVER re-render.** `_prepareContext` reads the STORED config, so a `render()` would discard unsaved edits in Identity/Security/Network/Systems/Boot. Everything repaints by hand (`_repaintThemeTab`, `_rebuildSavedThemeList`, `_rebuildFullPresetList`). The one exception is `fullApply`, which writes immediately after a confirm that says so.
- **A cleared override is stored as `''`, never deleted.** `updateConfig` merges through `mergeObject`, which would keep a removed key's old value; `''` is falsy so the builder falls through to the preset exactly as an absent key would.
- The preset **cards drive a hidden `<input name="preset">`** — that is what `_onSaveConfig` and `_onApplyPreset` read.
- Only the borrowed `.ncm-tc__*` widgets are reused; their **button/select resets are root-scoped per host**, so `.ncm-item-inbox-config` had to join those selector lists in theme-customizer.css. Keep them in step.

**Two libraries, and the security rule that separates them:**
- **`shardThemePresets` — saved LOOKS.** Palette, fonts, wallpaper, plus a `basePreset`. Carries **no security block by construction**.
- **`shardPresetLibrary` — full shard presets.** Look + startup + identity, and the **security block ONLY if the GM ticked the box when saving** (unticked by default). An entry that carries one is chipped `SECURITY` on its row and the apply confirm names it. `SHARD_SECURITY_KEYS` is an explicit list, so a config field added later does not silently start travelling inside a preset. Import never auto-applies.
- The reason for all of it: several shipped presets ship Black ICE at DV 20, and reaching for "make another one like this" must never arm a shard without saying so. Same rule as the Encoder never handing `convertToDataShard` a preset KEY.

**Players can wear the GM's looks.** The Encoder's Look row offers the built-in presets and the saved looks (`encoderConfig.look = { presets, savedThemes }`, both default on, each gating its own source). A saved look and a preset are **mutually exclusive picks** — two lit chips claiming to set the same thing reads as a bug. `_lookConfig` layers the saved palette onto the preset chrome (`{...out.theme, ...t}` — no shared keys) and still carries **no security**.


**Optional depth — three real rules (2026-09-22).** `encoderConfig.optional` was four bare booleans that nothing read. It is now `{gear, time, assist}`, each an object with a rule behind it; **`signature` was REMOVED** rather than left as a dead switch (dev: "lets just remove that"). `sanitizeOptional` **lifts a legacy boolean into `{on: <bool>}`** over the defaults, so a world that stored the placeholder shape upgrades with no migration.
- **gear** — `gearBonusFor(cfg, carriedNames)`. Matched by item name the space/punctuation-insensitive SkillService way. `stack: 'best'` (default) counts only the most useful item; `'sum'` adds them, which is how a +9 happens by accident. It returns `from[]` so the readout can NAME what helped — a silent bonus is one the player cannot check, argue with or learn from.
- **time** — `timeCostFor(cfg, dv)` = base + perDV × DV, rendered by `formatMinutes`. Charged whatever the outcome: a rejected forgery still took the afternoon. Advancing the clock is a GM-only write, so `_spendTime()` moves it when it can and **always reports the cost**, whispering the GM when a player cannot move it themselves. Both the clock write and the report are try/caught — running out of permission must never cost a player the shard they just rolled for.
- **assist** — `assistBonusFor(cfg, {helperRank})`. Candidates come from `getPlayerCharacters()`; a helper who fails `requireSkill` is still OFFERED but chipped "no help" and refused on click, so the rule teaches itself instead of leaving the player wondering why the number did not move.

**Every bonus reaches the roll through ONE accessor — `ShardEncoderApp._bonuses()`** (perk + gear + assist). `_base()`, both ladder previews and the roll's `modifier:` all read it. A bonus added at a call site is exactly how the readout and the roll end up quoting different numbers; the sim asserts no preview still adds `_blank().perk` by hand.

**The shard viewer's window title is the shard's own name** — no `DATA SHARD:` prefix (dev, 2026-09-22). It prefers `config.shardName`, then the item name.

**The Player Authoring window's copy is PLAIN, per the standing rule that GM tools use plain language** (in-world vocabulary belongs on player-facing immersive surfaces). Every section subtitle says what the control does, not why it is interesting — the aphoristic first pass read as confusing to the dev. Two lines were also factually stale, claiming the Lock and Content stages were "not built yet" after they shipped; when a stage lands, grep this window for its copy.


Sim: `dev/tools/sims/test-shard-theme.mjs` (45 checks — the palette move, every `buildShardThemePrefs` shape incl. both legacy ones, the viewer wiring, the tab's save contract as a template↔handler drift guard, a REAL Handlebars render of the panel with real data, and the security opt-in).

## Architecture: data shard conversion + entries

Two convert paths, both gated through `ShardSheetOverride._launchConversionFlow(item, actor)` (the right-click "Convert to Data Shard" on actor sheets and the Items directory):
- **GM** converts **instantly with the default config** — `DataShardService.convertToDataShard(item)` (no overrides) — mirroring the Admin → Shards tab "Convert" button. **No wizard.** The GM customizes afterward via the "Configure Shard" context option. (Pre-change the GM also got the player wizard; that was deliberately removed.)
- **Players** get the **Shard Encoder**, gated by `canEncode(actor)` (see the encoder-gate section). It opens on the BLANK stage with the item seeded — deliberately NOT `stage: 'program'`, which used to force every item right-click into the Program branch, so a player who wanted to forge a dossier landed on a page asking which program to build. (The tier-gated `ShardConversionFlow` wizard that used to live here is retired.)

**Admin → Shards → "New Blank Shard" CREATES a fresh world Item** (`ShardsTab._createBlankShard`: CPR `gear` type when offered, name auto-numbered, default icon), converts it with the blank preset, and opens Configure — it used to open the same pick-an-item dialog as the preset quick-create buttons, so it read as a duplicate of "Convert Item" (dev, 2026-09-10). The preset buttons still convert an EXISTING item.

**Shard entries must be written through `DataShardService.addMessage(item, entryData)`** — never by hand-writing a journal page. The reader `DataShardService._pageToShardMessage` (used by `getShardMessages`/`getVisibleEntries`, which the `ItemInboxApp` viewer renders) **discards any page whose flag `type !== 'shard-message'`** and keys on `messageId`/`contentType`. `addMessage` stamps exactly that shape — as does the Encoder, via `programToEntry` / the composer's collected `entryData`. (A prior bug in the retired conversion wizard wrote a raw page with `entryType`/no `messageId` and the entry was silently invisible; that is the failure mode to watch for in any new writer.) **Per-entry `icon`/`accentColor` are NOT stored** — the viewer derives icon/accent from `CONTENT_TYPE_INFO[contentType]`.

**Players can't write to the GM-owned shard journal** (created `OBSERVER`). So the wizard's initial entry **rides along in the conversion socket relay**: `_requestPlayerConversion(item, config, entryData)` → `SHARD_CONVERT_REQUEST` carries `entryData` → the GM handler in `SocketHandlers.js` runs `convertToDataShard` then `addMessage` in one round-trip (entry failure is non-fatal). Eddies debited up-front in the wizard are **refunded on any conversion failure** (`_refundActorWealth`).

**Integrity is opt-in per shard, not a regression.** It renders only when `config.integrity.enabled` is true. `DEFAULTS.SHARD_CONFIG.integrity.enabled` is **`false`** and **no preset enables it** (an Encoder blank tier can, via its `integrity` checkbox), so default-converted shards show no bar until the GM ticks Integrity in Configure Shard → Systems.

**Shard integrity (rebuilt 2026-10-18, tester report "nothing ever looks corrupted") — design + as-built in `dev/design/SHARD-INTEGRITY.md`.** Three root causes it fixed: cosmetic mode had NO visuals at all (the tier only coloured the bar); mechanical corruption silently failed for players (the page write on the GM-owned journal threw inside a swallowed `.catch`); corruption was all-or-nothing. Standing rules:
- **Two layers.** Shard integrity (one number, the bar) drives the visuals + death at 0. Each entry's `corruption` page flag (0..1, quarter steps; legacy `corrupted:true` reads as 1) drives what is lost (mechanical mode). **Which units are lost is DERIVED, never stored** — `IntegrityModel.isUnitLost` gives each unit a fixed seeded threshold, so loss is monotonic and reconstruct returns the newest losses first.
- **All rules live in the pure `IntegrityModel.js`** (`resolveIntegrity` layers any stored block over `INTEGRITY_DEFAULTS`, 0-safe — no migration); DOM paint in `IntegrityFX.js`. **Lost data is removed BEFORE `enrichContent`** (`applyLoss`), never hidden with CSS — the leak rule.
- **Cosmetic garble is PAINT-OVER**: the real character stays in the DOM (`-webkit-text-fill-color: transparent` + a `::before` glyph), so copy/paste and screen readers get the truth. Capped at 1,200 glyphs per entry. Motion is vetoed by OS reduced-motion or the animation pref; the garble stays.
- **Every integrity write is GM-side; players relay** via `SOCKET_OPS.SHARD_INTEGRITY_REQUEST` (ops degrade/entry/repair/salvage). `degradeIntegrity` writes **pages first, then the item** — the viewer re-renders off the item update. The threshold is a **percent** (it used to compare raw points). **The config save carries `currentIntegrity`/`history`/`salvaged` over** (`_collectIntegrity`) — it used to reset to Max, healing the shard on every save.
- Protected money (eddies/banking never lose eb), programs turn **unstable** (`programInstability` → `unstable` on `runPayloadExecute`/`runMalwareDeploy`: +DV and a misfire chance) — both GM toggles. Uploads bake the damage into the copy (`exportEntry`; a fully lost entry can't be uploaded).
- The ShardsTab **Integrity** box has a Damage button (+ `game.nightcity.messenger.damageShard`, GM macro "Damage Selected Shard") and an **Entry damage** list; the network access-mode picker's card-clearing is now scoped to its own group because the Integrity section reuses `.ncm-cfg-am-card`.
- **The index strip never says more than the cards** (`_applyIntegrityToChips`): chips take their title from the ENRICHED entry (garbled at 75%+ for players, "Sector lost" when gone — it used to print the real stored subject), carry a 4-segment damage bar, and give the GM the exact % as a tooltip. Chip titles get the paint-over garble at half rate from Unstable down; the chip itself is a `data-action`, so its title is garbled with a narrower skip list than card bodies.
- Sim `dev/tools/sims/test-integrity.mjs` (40); probe `dev/mockups/shard-integrity-probe.html` (real viewer code); design mock `shard-integrity-mock.html`. **Test setup:** `dev/tools/test-integrity-setup.macro.js` (GM Script macro) builds four shards on a carrier actor — Breach Me / Pre-damaged / Visual Only / Bricked — and lists what to try.

**Shard text size (2026-09-10, tester "old eyes") — two knobs, multiplied.** `item-inbox.css` and the shared `content-types.css` had **zero** `--ncm-text-scale` plumbing (226 + 218 fixed-px font-sizes), so the player's Theme Customizer Text Size did nothing in the shard viewer and only partly in typed content anywhere. Both are now fully swept to `calc(Npx * var(--ncm-text-scale, 1))` (mechanical; the sweep skips `font-size: 0` and comments). On top of that a **per-shard Text Size slider** (80–150, step 5) sits in the shard config's Preset & Theme section → `config.textScale` (default 100, `DEFAULTS.SHARD_CONFIG`). The composition is pure CSS: `ThemeService.applyTheme` publishes the player scale on `:root` a second time as **`--ncm-text-scale-base`** (root only — a custom property can't read its own inherited value), the viewer template stamps `--ncm-shard-text-scale` inline on `.ncm-item-inbox` (from `shardTextScale` in `_prepareContext`), and the item-inbox.css root block defines `--ncm-text-scale: calc(base × shard)`. So a player at 130% reading a shard the GM set to 120% gets 1.56×, live from either slider. The config slider previews DOM-only onto every open viewer of that item (`_previewShardTextScale` sets the var, no render) and `close()` reverts an unsaved drag. **Admin-tagged windows now force `--ncm-text-scale: 1`** (admin-theme.css root rule): GM tools size text with the admin *zoom*, and before this the player scale stacked on top of it for every calc'd surface inside a tagged window (GM-view viewer/composer, the entry composer's live preview — and, post-sweep, the entry composer's own form). Terminals are untouched: they set `--ncm-text-scale` on their own root and never read the base. **`malware-workshop.css` is swept too (2026-09-12, all 77 sizes)** — inert inside the GM Workshop (admin-tagged → scale forced to 1, the admin zoom sizes it) but required because the player-facing Shard Encoder (designed, not built — `dev/mockups/shard-encoder-fui-mock.html`) is hosted in the Workshop frame and reuses its `.ncm-fw-*` classes; the dev's rule is that the Workshop's type tiers are the Encoder's model. The FUI modals (security-console / upload-console / bank-heist / operator-select) still have no scale plumbing. Verified in headless Chrome against the real core+CPR+module cascade (shard 1.2×1.5 → 23.4px from 13px; default stamp → player scale only; admin-tagged → 13px; terminal → its own 1.1×).

**Shard contrast (same day) — a per-shard Contrast slider (70–140) beside Text Size → `config.contrast`.** Same OKLCH tone-map as the Theme Customizer (`adjustLightness` around `CONTRAST_SPREAD.pivot`, accents excluded, images untouched), packaged as **`applyContrastToElement(el, pct, {baseFrom})`** (helpers.js): for each `CONTRAST_SPREAD` token it reads the base colour from **`:root`'s computed value** (i.e. the player's own already-contrast-adjusted palette, via `cssColorToHex`; the constants' hex when :root has nothing), pushes it from the pivot, and writes it **inline on the viewer root** — where the shard's `--sp-*` aliases (which read `--ncm-*` on the SAME element) and the shared content-types CSS pick it up. A slider drag or re-render can't compound, because the base is always re-read rather than accumulated; **100% removes the inline overrides** so an untouched shard keeps following the player theme live. Applied in `ItemInboxApp._applyShardTheme` right after the preset class (`shardContrast` in context); the config previews live on open viewers through the same helper and `close()` reverts an unsaved drag (`_displayPreviewed`/`_displaySaved` cover both sliders). Terminals already had contrast through `buildTerminalThemePrefs` → `ThemeService.applyTheme` and are untouched.
