> **CORRECTED 2026-09-22.** This paragraph used to claim "shard presets never override the `--sp-*` palette in CSS … presets only carry accent/headerBg/icon". **That was wrong**, and the error was load-bearing: `styles/themes/shard-presets.css` hardcoded all **twenty** `--sp-*` tokens for each of the eight themed presets, and since the contrast tone-map writes `--ncm-*`, those presets' `--sp-*` never read it — **the contrast slider silently did nothing on 8 of the 9 presets.** The fix is the palette move described in the Shard theming section: presets now carry a JS `palette`, the `--sp-*` tokens derive from `--ncm-*` in ONE place (item-inbox.css), and `applyContrastToElement` is called with `{ baseFrom: <app root> }` so it tone-maps *this shard's* colours rather than the player's. The `baseFrom`-less form still reads `:root`, which is what the Encoder and other callers want.

**Index strip open/close animation.** The viewer's Index nav chips cascade in/out via CSS keyframes staggered by a per-chip `--ncm-chip-i` custom property (set in `item-inbox.hbs`). `_onToggleIndexStrip` adds `--anim` (flow-in, forced reflow to restart) on open and `--closing` (flow-out) on close, then a JS timer (`220 + chipCount×25`ms) adds `--collapsed` (`display:none`) once the out-animation finishes. Rapid toggles resolve via an "is collapsed OR closing" check + `clearTimeout`; `prefers-reduced-motion` collapses instantly.

## Architecture: the ENVELOPE — mail vs file (#37)

Every entry used to wear an **email envelope** (from / subject / received) even when it was a camera feed. A terminal is a **machine**, and machines hold **files**. So an entry now carries **`envelope: 'mail' | 'file'`** (default `'mail'` — **every legacy entry is untouched, zero migration**).

- **`file`** = a machine artifact: **filename / path / owner-node / modified / size** instead of a sender. It also **suppresses every mail affordance**: no avatar, no Reply, no Add-to-contacts, **no threading**, **no delivery toast**, and **no master-contact auto-create** (a file's owner is a NODE — `SECCAM-B2`, `root`, `kowalski` — not an Agent).
- **`resolveEnvelope(entry)`** (ContentRenderers, the one source of truth for all three viewers + the composer) returns the file view-model, **deriving everything the GM didn't author**: `deriveFileSize` (12 frames → ~14 MB; a **live feed derives NO size — it's a stream, not a file**), `suggestFilename` (subject → slug + per-type extension via `FILE_TYPE_INFO`; a feed gets `.feed`, not `.img`), and the **type-aware 4th cell** (Channels / Rows / Lines / Subjects / Frames…). A live feed also **renames itself**: `typeLabel` is **CAMERA FEED**, not "Image Gallery" — the mode is its identity — with the broadcast icon and a green accent.
- **`isStream`** is load-bearing: a stream has **no size and no modified date ("LIVE")**, and it cannot be copied off the machine (see exfil).
- **Composer**: a **Show as MESSAGE / FILE** segmented control at the top of ② DELIVERY swaps the section's fields (DOM-only, so a flip-flop doesn't lose unsaved edits). `FILE_DEFAULT_TYPES` pre-picks FILE for the obviously-file types (feed gallery / syslog / malware / schematic / payload / spreadsheet / record / credentials / chatlog / avlog / document) — **a default, not a rule**; one click flips it.
- **Persistence — FOUR field-by-field spots, all four must carry it**: `TerminalService._buildMessageFlags`, `MessageRepository.createMessage`, `MessageRepository._pageToMessage`, **and `DataShardService.addMessage` + `_pageToShardMessage`** (a shard holds files too, and a burned file must stay a file). The edit-prefill openers (`TerminalViewerApp._onEditMessage`, `ItemInboxApp._onEditEntry`) must ALSO carry `envelope`/`file`/`exfil` — the edit path writes `entryData` straight to flags, so a missing key silently resets a FILE back to mail on the next save.
- **Page name**: a file titles its journal page `[FILE] <filename>` — the mail formatter would call it "Unknown".
- **Threading**: `resolveThreads` skips `envelope: 'file'` outright. Signal 4 (subject + shared counterparty) would otherwise merge two unrelated `dock-cam.feed` files from the same node into one "conversation".
- **AUDIT (same round)** — two more silently-dropped fields found in `_buildMessageFlags` and fixed: **`status.eddiesClaimed`/`eddiesClaimedAt`** (hardcoded false → a mirrored/exfil copy of an already-claimed eddies drop was **re-claimable**) and **`selfDestruct`** (hardcoded null). Same class of bug as the `spam`/`folder`/`recovered*` drops. **When adding a message field, grep all four builders.**

**Per-entry exfil override (#37).** `entry.exfil = { mode: 'inherit'|'custom', <action>: { state: 'inherit'|'allow'|'deny', dv? }, denyReason, hideDenied }`, resolved by **`TerminalService.resolveEntryExfil(item, entry)`** layered over `getExfilPolicy`. Rules:
- **`mode` is the control people expect and it MUST exist** (it was in the mock, got dropped in the first build, and its absence made the whole feature read as broken). `'inherit'` (default) = an untouched action follows the terminal's Exfil tab — so "set one action to ALLOW and hide the rest" changes **nothing visible**, because the rest are still inheriting an *allow*. **`'custom'`** = the entry is the whole story: anything not explicitly ALLOWed is **denied**. `custom` + `hideDenied` is the "allow only this one, lock everything else away" intent. The composer surfaces it as a **FOLLOW TERMINAL / ONLY WHAT I ALLOW** toggle with a live note explaining what INH means in the current mode, and `resolveEntryExfil` emits a `log.debug` of the resolved per-action outcome (debugMode) so "the override isn't doing anything" is diagnosable.
- **The terminal's `allowExfil` master is a HARD CEILING** — master off = nothing leaves, and no entry can widen it.
- **A live camera feed can never be copied** (`agent`/`shard`/`forward`/`sell`/`leak` are force-denied with reason **LIVE STREAM**, regardless of authoring — *you cannot download a stream*). **WIPE still works** (you can always pull the plug) and **SAVE FRAME is the escape hatch**. This is physics, so it's enforced in the resolver, not left to the GM to remember.
- **A denied action renders DEAD WITH ITS REASON, it does not vanish** (`.is-denied` + `.ncm-term__exfil-why`) — a missing button reads as a broken UI, a stated reason teaches the rule. `hideDenied` forces the silent hide for things whose existence is the secret.
- Composer UI: a per-action **INHERIT / ALLOW / DENY** tri-state grid in ③ SECURITY & ACCESS (it's an access policy, not envelope metadata), with per-action DV, denied-reason and hide-instead.

**SAVE FRAME (`TerminalService.exfilSaveFrame`).** You can't take the stream — you can take a **picture** of it. One channel's frame becomes a **single-image gallery** in the operator's Agent (Recovered), stamped with the cam id + the feed clock, carrying a **file envelope**. A **looped** cam captures its *frozen* time (loop a cam then save a frame and you've captured the loop — the correct, cruel outcome). GM writes directly; **players relay** via `TERMINAL_EXFIL_REQUEST` `dest: 'saveFrame'`. Deliberate simplification: the capture is the frame's **source image**, not a pixel-crop of the PTZ view — cropping would mean rasterizing to a data URI and stuffing hundreds of KB of base64 into world data on every capture; instead the **PTZ aim (pan/tilt/zoom) rides in the caption**. The lightbox button only renders when the host stamps `data-save-frame` on the thumb (`TerminalViewerApp._stampFrameCapture`) — ContentRenderers stays policy-free because it serves all three viewers.

**Sidebar (terminal)**: one **mixed list**, and **both row kinds share ONE hierarchy** — this is the rule, don't drift from it:
- **line 1 = the ANCHOR** (sender name / filename) — same size + weight in both, `text-primary`; mail uses the body font, a file uses mono (it's a machine name, not a person's). The priority dot ↔ the **content-type icon** swap here.
- **line 2 = what it is** — the subject, or a small **type chip** (+ size). Typed *mail* gets the same chip, so a spreadsheet reads the same whether it arrived as mail or sits there as a file.
- **line 3 = muted detail** — the body snippet, or the file's path.

The first cut anchored mail on a **14px bold subject** while a file anchored on its **11.5px filename**, so a mixed list read as **two competing lists** (dev: "the actual list of messages/files are confusing"). Unread files show **NEW** (a file has no urgency — it just wasn't there before); a live feed's date column reads **LIVE**. **ALL / MAIL / FILES** solo chips ride the gallery chip driver (`[data-env-act]`, document-delegated) and only render when the folder holds both kinds.

**The exfil folder's NAME is a setting, and every surface reads it.** The Agent folder that catches exfiltrated intel (downloaded mail, burned files, captured camera frames) defaults to **EXFIL**, not "Recovered". Two reasons the old name broke down: the player clicks **EXFILTRATE → To my Agent** and it landed in a folder with a *different* word (two vocabularies for one idea), and "recovered" implies something was lost and found — a **SAVE FRAME capture of a live feed was never lost**, it wasn't even a file until you made one. The label is the world setting **`exfilFolderName`** (GM-renameable — Salvage / Cache / Recovered / The Haul…), read through **`SettingsManager.getExfilFolderName()`**. **The folder tab, the per-message provenance chip, and every exfil toast (including the socket-relay ones) all read that one getter** — hardcoding the word in any of them makes them disagree the moment a GM renames it. The stored flag key stays `metadata.recovered` and the filter key stays `'recovered'`, so renaming is a pure label change with no data migration.

**Terminal motion (#38) — the terminal inherits the MessageViewer's motion vocabulary, it does not invent its own:** `cubic-bezier(.22,1,.36,1)` (expo-out), **0.3s** entrances / **0.18s** micro-interactions, **translate + fade only** (never scale-only, never bounce), all `prefers-reduced-motion` gated. Tokens live on `.ncm-terminal-viewer` (`--ncm-term-ease/fast/base`). What moves: message rows **cascade in** (`--ncm-i` stagger, capped at 9 so a full page can't become a wait), the **detail pane slides** when a different message is opened, the **sidebar collapse animates the grid column**, the **account-switcher dropdown unfolds** from its top edge, and the **login pick** holds the chosen card while the others fall away (`_playCardPick` — cards/roster/portraits; crt/lockscreen/keypad opt out by having no wrapper).

**A CSS transition cannot survive an AppV2 re-render — collapse/expand toggles must be DOM-ONLY.** `render()` **replaces** the element, so it is born in its final state and the transition has nothing to animate *from*: the panel snaps. Both collapse toggles (`TerminalViewerApp._onToggleSide`, `MessageViewerApp._toggleListPanel`) therefore flip the class on the **live node** and update the chevron by hand, and the template still stamps the class from state so it survives the next real render. This is why the terminal's collapse "wasn't working" — it was rendering, not transitioning. Same rule applies to any future animated toggle.

**The MessageViewer runs the same motion system** (row cascade, detail slide, animated list-panel collapse, refine-bar hover) — `MessageViewerApp._stampMotion` is the twin of the terminal's, with the list signature keyed on actor · filter · sort · page · search · env-solo · tab · message ids.

**THE trap, and the reason `_stampMotion` exists: the terminal re-renders on EVERY click.** An entrance animation tied to "a render happened" would make the list twitch every time you select a message. So `_stampMotion` compares a **signature of the list's CONTENTS** (account · folder · env-solo · page · message ids — deliberately **excluding the selection**) against the last render and only stamps `is-fresh` when the list actually changed; the detail's `is-fresh` keys on the selected id changing, so re-rendering the *same* message (claiming eddies, decrypting a frame) doesn't re-slide it. Rule: **motion on arrival and on state change, never on idle.** Sim: scratchpad `test-motion.mjs` (12/12, drives the real `_stampMotion`).

**Terminal sidebar collapses (`terminalToggleSide` → `_sideCollapsed`).** The mailbox folds to nothing so a spreadsheet / camera multiplex / schematic gets the machine's full width (the MessageViewer `--list-collapsed` precedent). Two details that matter: the pane is **`display:none`d, not just width:0** (its children carry min-widths and would spill into the detail pane), and the **toggle floats on the divider, outside the collapsing pane** — a toggle that collapses along with its panel can't bring it back.

**Filter chips that live in a re-rendering app must be APP STATE, not a CSS class.** The MAIL/FILES chips first shipped as the gallery's display-only `[data-*-act]` driver — but the message viewer and terminal **re-render on every click**, so the filter reset itself the instant you opened an item. They're now real `data-action`s backed by `_envFilter`, and the filter **narrows the message list array** rather than hiding rows with CSS (hiding leaves pagination counting things you can't see, and counts computed off the paginated *page* make the chips lie on page 2). Counts are taken from the whole folder BEFORE the solo applies; the solo is force-cleared when the folder isn't mixed, so a stale solo can't empty a folder that has no chips left to undo it. The gallery's chips stay display-only — correct there, since a gallery re-render is scoped to its own entry.

**Modal layer order (learned the hard way).** `.ncm-seccon-backdrop` (SecurityConsole) is **z-index 130**, above the gallery lightbox's **110** — every roll launched from the MONITOR rail (SAVE FRAME, LOOP, CUT, TAKE CONTROL) opens **while the lightbox is still on screen**, and at its old z-index 100 the console rendered *underneath* it ("save frame appears underneath the gallery pop up"). The console is the thing being interacted with, so it's always topmost; the lightbox deliberately stays open behind it (you see the frame you're capturing) and the flow closes it afterward. BankHeist (60) / OperatorSelect (90) never open over a lightbox, so they sit below it.

**The ACCESS-GRANTED badge scan rides `--ncm-primary`, not `--ncm-secondary`.** The secondary slot stays brand cyan in most presets, so on Arasaka (red primary) the verification scan swept **blue across a red card** and read as a foreign element. Primary = the machine's identity colour, so the scan always belongs to the card it's scanning. (Same reason the welcome line under ACCESS GRANTED uses `:has(+ .ncm-term__badge-welcome)` to drop the granted line's bottom padding — it stacked with the welcome's top margin into a dead 24px band.)

**Terminal chip/button sizing.** The sender-header chips (Add to contacts · Reply via Agent) and the whole **exfil rail** (`.ncm-term__btn`) are deliberately **small** (9.5px mono, 3–4px padding, 4px radius) — they were 11–12px with 6–9px padding and dominated the detail pane. There can be **seven** exfil chips at once (six actions + SAVE FRAME, several of them dead deny-chips), so they must stay chip-sized; the GM edit/delete/move controls match at 24px.

## Architecture: paste hygiene + Import from Journal (2026-10-23)

Tester: pasting a journal page into a shard **Document** gave black text on the dark theme and white blocks, typing afterwards stayed black, the light theme was no better, and "one paragraph per box" made bringing journals over impractical (PDF copies arrive hard-wrapped on top). **Root cause: a browser's default paste writes the SOURCE's computed styles inline** (a journal is `#191813` on parchment → every span lands with its own `color`/`background-color`), and a container's `color … !important` can never beat a child's inline declaration; the block was saved as raw `innerHTML` and rendered raw, so players got it too. Typing "stayed black" because the caret was inside the pasted span.

- **`scripts/utils/richText.js` is the ONE pure module** (no Foundry/DOM; sim `dev/tools/sims/test-rich-paste.mjs`, 96 checks). `cleanHtml(html, {mode, colors})` is a tokenizer + tolerant tree whitelist cleaner. Modes: `inline` (a list item/caption), `block` (a Document text block: inline + `p`/`br`/lists; `div`→`p`, headings→bold line, blockquote/pre→p, img/hr/tables dropped), `rich`/`mail` (a message body: + headings/blockquote/pre/hr/img/tables). **Colour policy: a colour survives ONLY if it is the module's** — a `var(--ncm-*)` value or a hex in `RICH_TEXT_PALETTE` (the shard RTE's swatches; `RTE_COLORS` now aliases it). At PASTE time even palette colours are dropped (`colors:'none'`). Also kept: `font-weight/style`, `text-decoration`, margins/paddings, relative `font-size` ≤ 2em, `ncm-*` classes, `data-label`, safe `href`/`src` (no `javascript:`/`data:`). The Google Docs `<b style="font-weight:normal">` wrapper is unwrapped, Word's `o:p`/comments dropped. **Block contract: a one-paragraph block is bare inline HTML, a multi-paragraph block is a `<p>…</p>` sequence** (Enter in the editor writes `<div>`s → normalised to `<p>` at save).
- **Three layers, never just one:** PASTE (the editor's `paste` listener: shard Document blocks via a delegated listener on `.ncm-docpage` → `_onDocPaste`; the shard message RTE → `_onRtePaste`; the Agent mail editor in `MessageComposerApp._setupEditorSync`), SAVE (`DataShardComposer._collectDocRow` cleans `block`, `_collectEntryData` cleans the body `rich`), RENDER (`ContentRenderers` document enricher cleans text `block` + list items `inline`; a plain message body `rich`; `MessageViewerApp._renderMessageBody` cleans `mail` BEFORE the encrypted-block regex — attribute order is preserved so it still matches; `TerminalViewerApp._sanitizeBody` now routes through it, so a bold line/list no longer flattens to plain text on the machine). Render-time cleaning heals shards and mail saved before this shipped with no migration. **A new contenteditable surface = a paste listener + a save clean + a render clean**; the sim counts them.
- **Paste → blocks (`pasteToBlocks` / `htmlToBlocks`)**: headings start a section and become the heading of whatever follows (text, a list, a figure); consecutive paragraphs stay in ONE text block (a block is a SECTION, not a sentence); lists/figures/quotes are their own blocks; a table becomes one paragraph of rows; `@UUID[…]{Label}` enrichers flatten to the label (`enrichers:true`, the journal-import path). **Plain text (a PDF viewer puts only text on the clipboard) is REFLOWED** (`reflowText`): hard-wrapped lines rejoin (a non-sentence-ending line ≥55% of the paragraph's longest line, or a lowercase successor), `word-` + lowercase dehyphenates, ligature glyphs (ﬁ ﬂ ﬀ) expand, NBSP/soft-hyphens normalise, bullet runs become list blocks, a block whose longest line is under 40 chars keeps every break (an address, verse), and a bare page number (`12`, `Page 3 of 9`) is dropped only when the paste has ≥3 paragraphs. The reflow GUESSES, so **an after-paste strip (`.ncm-docpaste`) offers Undo and, when several paragraphs landed in one block, "Split into N blocks"**; a per-block `docSplitBlock` tool (`has-paras` class, shown only where it applies) does the same later. Undo rebuilds the list from a `_collectDocBlocks()` snapshot — the native undo stack cannot restore blocks that live outside the contenteditable.
- **Import from Journal** (`docImportJournal`, the Body fieldset's actions row): the shared name picker over every journal TEXT page (sub-line = journal · folder path, chip = top folder), `fromUuid` → `page.text.content` → `htmlToBlocks(…, {enrichers:true})`; Replace/Append dialog when blocks already exist; an empty Title takes the page name.
- **Viewer/editor fixes that rode along:** the text block renders as `<div class="ncm-ct-doc__p">` (a `<p>` cannot hold paragraphs; CSS + the declassified tint cover both); `.ncm-docpage` background is `--sp-bg-deep` (was a literal `#14141c` — near-black under clean-light); the editor's heading input wears the viewer's accent mono look (it was bright display text, so "white while typing, orange displayed" read as a bug); `.ncm-docpara__t *` inherits colour/background so a legacy pasted block reads correctly in the editor before its first save; the Agent viewer gained rules for h1–h6/blockquote/code/pre/hr/ul/ol/img/table in mail bodies (they rendered in Foundry/CPR defaults); the Agent composer's monospace tool writes a bare `<code>` (its inline literal hex ignored the theme). List items used to render raw `{{{this}}}` with no escaping (a typed `<3` broke the list) — cleaned `inline` now.
- Probe: `dev/mockups/doc-paste-probe.html?t=clean-dark|clean-light|high-contrast&raw=1&strip=1&pdf=1` lifts the REAL `_docBlockHtml` out of the composer source (CRLF-safe regex — the repo is CRLF on Windows), renders the real `content-body.hbs`, and reports worst text contrast in `<title>`: the raw paste measures **1.06:1** (the tester's screen), cleaned **8.81:1** dark / **6.79:1** light. **Not live-tested in Foundry.**

## Architecture: terminal FILE REPOSITORY — shared volume, folders, gates, the FILES view (2026-10-25)

Tester (Aegaeon): "how do I add files? do I need a separate terminal? can I combine mail and files to replicate a workstation… let someone browse a file depository." One terminal had always held both — every entry is a typed content entry and a file is one with `envelope: 'file'` — but the ONLY door was the `New message (GM)` button at the bottom of the mailbox sidebar (visible only after logging in), a file's folder was a free-text `filePath` box in the composer, nothing could live on the MACHINE rather than on a login, and nothing was gated. Dev-approved mock: `dev/mockups/terminal-file-repository-mock.html` (three frames; the first flat-list version of frame 2 was rejected because it did not say what goes in what folder — the shipped one is folder-first).

- **The rules are the pure module `scripts/utils/terminalFiles.js`** (no Foundry globals; sim `dev/tools/sims/test-terminal-files.mjs`, 109 checks, drives it + the REAL TerminalService methods over a mocked journal). **A file's folder IS its `file.path`**, stored NORMALISED (`records/2046`, no leading slash — `_buildMessageFlags` and `updateAccountMessage` normalise), so every entry ever authored keeps its place and the tree is built from the paths. **A SHARED VOLUME entry is a page whose `accountId` is `SHARED_SCOPE` (`'__shared__'`)** — on the terminal, not on a login. **`config.files.folders` is the folder REGISTRY** (`{id, scope: '__shared__'|accountId, path, gate:{mode:'everyone'|'admin'|'clearance'|'accounts', min, accountIds}}`, `sanitizeFilesConfig`): it adds what a path string cannot carry — an empty folder, and a GATE. A folder that exists only because files sit in it is DERIVED and ungated; registering it is what the who-can-open select does. **Gates inherit downward and an inner `everyone` never reopens a locked parent** (`effectiveGate`); admin accounts and the GM pass every gate; `clearance` compares the FIRST INTEGER of the account's free-text clearance (`LEVEL 3` → 3, `OMEGA` → never passes a numeric gate). An unknown gate mode sanitises to `everyone`, never to a lock.
- **ONE reader enforces it — `TerminalService.getAccountMessages(item, accountId, {includeShared=true, enforceGates=true})`**: shared pages are included for every account, each message is stamped `accountId` + `shared`, and for a non-GM every file in a folder the account cannot open (or under one) is REMOVED from the list (`visibleEntries`) — so every by-id reader (content actions, exfil, edit, upload) is covered without a second check, and a hidden file is never in the DOM. `getAllMessages` / `getSharedMessages` are the GM-tooling readers. Folder CRUD is GM-only: `saveFileFolder` (upsert by scope+path), `renameFileFolder` / `removeFileFolder` (**both rewrite every file's `file.path` under the old path** via `rewritePlan` — a delete moves files UP a level, nothing vanishes), `moveFile(item, id, {scope, path})` (re-homes an entry across volumes AND re-files it; `updateAccountMessage` accepts `accountId`/`file`/`envelope` for the same reason). `addAccountMessage` accepts `SHARED_SCOPE`. `buildFileTree(item, scope, accountId)` / `canOpenFolder`.
- **Viewer: MAIL | FILES is a VIEW SWITCH (`_view`), not a filter** — the old ALL/MAIL/FILES solo chips (`_envFilter`, `terminalEnvFilter`) are GONE. MAIL = the folder strip as before, holding mail (and a file only in the Trash). FILES = `_filesContext` → the directory tree in the sidebar (`ncm-term__fs-*`: roots HOME + SHARED, dirs with depth, root-level files; a locked dir is `is-locked` with `data-locked="1"` + `data-lock-label`, and **`_onFsOpen` refuses it with the reason**; the GM sees it unlocked with a small open-lock tag) and, in the detail pane, the open folder's **LISTING** (`fs.showListing` branch BEFORE `{{else if selectedMessage}}`: breadcrumb, stats, `..` row, sub-folders, files with type chip / size / modified / owner; a locked sub-folder is a dead row stating why). A file opened from the tree gets a FOLDER back chip (`fsBack`). `_fsSel = {scope, path}` is app state; `_stampMotion`'s signature carries `_view` + `_fsSel`. The GM button reads "Add message or file (GM)" / "Add a file here (GM)" and, in FILES view, opens the composer pre-filed on the open folder and volume. Pagination is off in FILES view (the listing scrolls).
- **Config → Accounts holds the content (`TerminalContentPanel.buildContentContext` → `templates/terminal/partials/config-content.hbs`, `TEMPLATES.TERMINAL_CONFIG_CONTENT`).** A SHARED VOLUME row above the accounts; every account row folds open (`ctToggle`, chevron + mail/file counts) into MAIL (the Identity-tab folders, each with a `+` and collapsible rows) and FILES (the tree as rows: a folder row = chevron · icon · name [click = rename] · **who-can-open `<select>`** · count · + file / + sub-folder / delete; files nest under their folder with a **Move-to `<select>`** over every volume's folders + "＋ New folder…"; a dashed "New folder in /x" input ends every tree). **A tab's context must be SPREAD** — `content` + each account row's `ct` are built in `_prepareContext`; the three selects and the input get manual listeners in `_wireContentTab` (the select-vs-data-action gotcha; every one `stopPropagation`s its click so the account row's `editAccount` does not fire). `ctGateAccounts` / the `accounts` mode uses `showNamePicker({multi:true})` over the accounts. Text prompts go through `_askText` (an `adminDialog`).
- **Composer ② DELIVERY (terminal targets only): the free-text Path box is REPLACED by `Store on` (this account / SHARED, `storeSeg` → hidden `fileScope`) + a `Folder` select per volume (`data-store-of`, options = the live tree ∪ "＋ New folder…" → `fileFolderNew`).** `_fileStoreContext` builds it from `target.accountId` / `target.homeAccountId` / `target.folderPath` (the viewer and the config sheet pass all three; `homeAccountId` is what "this account" means when the composer was opened from the shared volume). `_collectEntryData` writes `entryData.file.path` (normalised) AND `entryData.accountId` — the edit path writes entryData keys straight to flags, so changing Store-on on an existing file re-homes it; `_persistEntry` registers a "new folder" pick via `saveFileFolder` before `addAccountMessage(item, entryData.accountId, …)`. The mail `Folder` select is `data-env-only="mail"`. A shard target keeps the plain path box.
- Also in this round, the tester's two other reports: **boot speed was a dead chip** (`_playBoot` hardcoded 2100 ms; now `TerminalViewerApp.bootHoldMs(boot)` — `BOOT_SPEED_MULT` fast .5 / normal 1 / dramatic 1.9 × `BOOT_BASE_MS` 2100, plus a **`custom` chip + Boot length seconds** field in Boot & Login → `boot.customSeconds`; the fill transition, the log-line stagger (inline `animation-delay` beating the CSS nth-child fallback) and the hold all scale off that one number), and **"red on black" on Arasaka**: the terminal base palette's `--ncm-text-muted` was still `#555570` (2.9:1 on Arasaka's bg-deep, 67 uses) → `#8e8ea8`, every one of the 12 `TERMINAL_PRESETS` palettes now carries a tuned `textMuted` (≥ 5:1 on its own bg-deep; the five CSS attribute rules carry it too), and the boot log mixes 55% primary instead of 70% (7:1+ on every preset). Probe: `dev/mockups/terminal-files-probe.html?view=fs|cfg&admin=1&file=1` renders the REAL viewer template (FILES view) and the REAL config-content partial against the real cascade. Phase 6 gained **6.18**. Test seed: `dev/tools/test-terminal-setup.macro.js` step 6 (shared volume with records/ cams/ secure/ [admins] hr/ [clearance ≥ 3] + a home drafts/). **Not live-tested.** Known limits: no drag-to-reorder (Move-to covers it); a per-file visibility switch was deliberately NOT added (encryption is the per-file lock; a second switch beside the folder gate would confuse the two); gating is per folder only.
- **Dev live-test revisions (same day, pm):** (1) **an account ROW click FOLDS its content open** (`ctToggle`); only the pen / "edit login & security" opens the editor. (2) **Every data-action re-renders the sheet, so `TerminalConfigApp._preRender` memoises `.ncm-cfg-content` + every `[data-ct-scroll]` scroller and `_onRender` restores them** (`_scrollReset` on open/add account starts at the top); the viewer's `_preRender` memo gained the FILES tree + the listing (keyed on `_fsSel`). (3) **Each folder's rows sit in their own `.ncm-acctl-scroll`** (240px max): a dir row carries its direct `files` (rendered right under it via the inline partial `{{#*inline "ncmCtFile"}}`), root files are one `isRootFiles` holder, each mail folder's rows scroll too — mail folder objects carry `scope` so the key needs no `../`. (4) **The account editor's sticky footer is FLUSH**: `.ncm-cfg-content:has(.ncm-acct-editor)` drops its bottom padding and the editor section its bottom border (the strip under the footer was those two). (5) **Composer ② DELIVERY opens with the question** "Is this a message someone sent, or a file sitting on the machine?" over two cards (`.ncm-envpick`, same `envSeg` action + hidden `envelope` input), and the collapsed section's first chip names the envelope (`data-cmp-chip="env"`). (6) **MAIL | FILES are TABS inside an account's fold** (`ncm-acctl-tabs`, action `ctTab`, `_ctTab` Map per account, MAIL default; the shared volume has no strip and shows FILES) — dev: "tabbing both makes more sense than sub-collapsing, that just adds more busy-ness". Sim 117. (7) **Accessibility pass, MEASURED not reasoned** (dev: "how is the accessibility/scaling on all these?"): a contrast walk over every text node of the real config partial (`_a11y-probe` = the files probe + `?t=` + `?zoom=` + a ratio walker) found 47 of 98 nodes under 4.5:1 on clean-dark and 61 on clean-light — the muted token at 9px, repeated by me in the config block. Rule applied: **informational text is `--ncm-text-secondary` at ≥ 10px; muted is decoration only**; the `CT_COLORS` accents (chosen for a dark screen) are pulled toward text on `[data-ncm-admin-theme="clean-light"]` with `color-mix` (the composer network-pill precedent). Result 0 under 4.5 on dark and high-contrast; on light only the pre-existing `.ncm-cfg-btn-sm` (4.14) and avatar initials remain. **Icon actions are real `<button>`s with `aria-label`s** (the folder chevron carries `aria-expanded`), so the fold works from the keyboard; the viewer's listing column widths, head icon and lock panel scale with `--ncm-text-scale`. The 150%-zoom clip the walker reported is a probe artefact — Foundry's admin text size also grows the window frame.
- **Boot VIDEO (same day, dev: "a video as a boot screen, use its audio, however long it is it transitions into the content… let the video stand").** `scripts/ui/content/BootVideo.js` (`playBootVideo(host, {url, onDone})` → `{skip, destroy}`) is the ONE player for terminals AND shards: no choreography, no boot sound (the video's own audio at the module's `soundVolume`, muted when sound is off), the boot lasts as long as the video (`ended`), a click skips (after a 400 ms guard so the double-click that opened the window cannot skip it), and a wall-clock cap (duration + 2.5 s once metadata is known, else 180 s) plus the `error` event mean a bad path can never leave a black stage; reduced/off motion skips the video like it skips the choreography. Config: `boot.videoUrl` + `boot.videoOverlay` (title/subtitle over the picture, off by default) — a **Boot Video** section in the terminal's Boot & Login tab (FilePicker `type:'video'`, a clear button, the splash preview says "VIDEO BOOT — <file> plays instead") and two rows under the shard's boot block. The terminal boot template branches on `boot.isVideo` (the choreography is not rendered); the shard adds `is-video` + the overlay in `_playBootSequence`. `styles/components/boot-video.css` (`.ncm-boot-video*`, imported after toast.css); both viewers `destroy()` the player in `close()`. Harness: `scratchpad/bootvideo-harness.html` drove the real module (bad file → one `error` callback per host, destroy cleans the stage). **Small fixes (same day, after the first live look):** the switcher footer ("Log out → login screen") has a pointer cursor + hover; Desktop's switcher lives INSIDE the account chip and opens straight up from it; Configure Terminal → Accounts folds (accounts, the shared volume, mail folders, file folders) animate open and shut — `_foldClose` shrinks the LIVE body before the state change + re-render, `_onRender` → `_foldOpenAfterRender` grows a just-opened body from 0 (the body is the element right after the toggle's row; reduced motion skips). **Mailbox STYLES (same day, BUILT — dev: "all the mailbox styles are great, just make sure it's configurable somewhere and obvious in the config"; mock `dev/mockups/terminal-mailbox-styles-mock.html`, a Classic frame added at the dev's ask so the default stays an option).** `config.mailboxStyle` ∈ `TERMINAL_MAILBOX_STYLES` (classic | tabs | tty | desktop, default classic). `terminal-viewer.hbs` branches `msTabs` / `msTty` / `msDesktop` → `templates/terminal/mailbox/tabs|tty|desktop.hbs`, else the classic layout — and the DETAIL pane, the FILES tree and the list ROWS were split OUT of the viewer into `mailbox/detail|tree|rows.hbs` (`TEMPLATES.TERMINAL_MAILBOX_*`), which every style INCLUDING classic renders. **A style is a SKIN over ONE view-model and the SAME data-actions** (the sim asserts every action a partial uses is in the viewer map) — a new row field, action or detail affordance lands in all four at once; never fork the detail. `_mailboxContext` adds the flags, per-folder `unread`, `ttyPrompt`, `deskWindow`, `deskClock`; the desktop builds the files context in MAIL view too (drives + folders as icons, listing hidden). **TTY has a prompt** (`[data-tty-prompt]` → `_onTtyCommand`: a number opens that shown row — `_ttyList` is the page/listing as displayed; `m`/`f` switch views; `cd <folder>` / `cd ..` / `cd home|shared`, a locked folder refuses with its reason + `login-fail`; `n`/`p` page; `ls`; `?`); rows stay clickable so the mouse works. Config: **Boot & Login → Mailbox Layout** cards ABOVE Login Screen (CSS-glyph thumbs, `.ncm-cfg-mstyle*`; hidden input `mailboxStyle` = the login-style save contract; the preset KIT overlay and the full-preset snapshot carry it); 9 presets pick a non-classic style (corp consoles = tabs, BBS / DOS / chopshop / Blackwall = tty, lounge / rig = desktop); Admin → Terminals facts show it. CSS `styles/components/terminal-styles.css` (`ncm-tmtabs-*` / `ncm-tmtty-*` / `ncm-tmdesk-*`, tokens only, imported after crt.css). Two layout facts the renders taught: the desktop window is narrower than the classic detail, so its listing DROPS the OWNER column and tightens TYPE/MODIFIED (the fixed-layout table had handed NAME zero width); the TTY files listing needs its own grid (`--f`) because the mail grid gives the 4th column the slack and that is SIZE there. **First live look (dev, Arasaka + Tabs): the account dropdown was trapped in the tab bar** — the bar is an x-scroller (`overflow-x: auto` for many folders) and a scroller clips BOTH axes (the standing overflow gotcha), so the switcher opened inside a 40px strip you had to scroll; the dropdown now renders as a SIBLING of the bar (`.ncm-tmtabs__acdrop`, positioned in the style root) and the bar carries a log-out glyph at its right end. **TTY had no way out at all** — `logout` / `exit` / `su` / `switch` at the prompt and a `logout` link on the mount line go to the login screen (where another account is picked). Sim `dev/tools/sims/test-mailbox-styles.mjs` (131 — registry, partials, actions, the real Handlebars render of all four × MAIL/FILES, config + admin wiring, css prefixes/tokens, the theme-carries-layout contract); Phase 6 **6.19**; probes `terminal-files-probe.html?view=fs&ms=tabs|tty|desktop&files=0|1` and `config-theme-probe.html?tpl=terminal&tab=boot&focus=.ncm-cfg-mstyles` (`?focus=` hides the sections above a selector; the probe's Proxy context serves a real `mailboxStyles` list). **Not live-tested.**
- **A THEME CARRIES ITS MAILBOX LAYOUT (same day, dev: "are mailbox styles attached to themes? make it attached — you can modify it, but a preset theme comes with it, and saving your own theme saves it alongside").** Three carriers, one reader: a **preset card** brings its layout through the staged kit (every one of the 12 presets now states `mailboxStyle` explicitly — three had none and silently kept whatever the terminal had); a **saved theme** (`terminalThemePresets` entry) now stores `mailboxStyle` on save / overwrite and stages it on apply (a pre-2026-10-25 entry without one leaves the layout alone), and the theme JSON envelope ships it (`{ncmTerminalTheme:1, theme, mailboxStyle}`, import stages it); the **full preset library** already snapshotted it. `TerminalConfigApp._currentMailboxStyle()` is the ONE reader — `_stagedMailboxStyle` (a manual card pick or an applied saved theme) → the staged kit's → the saved config → classic, validated against the registry — and the Boot & Login card, the live viewer preview and the saved-theme writer all read it. **A manual card pick is STAGED, not just written to the hidden input**: the sheet re-renders on every data-action and the context would otherwise snap the card back to the kit's value (the login-style cards still have that latent snap). A pick also previews live on the open viewer: the preview overlay carries `mailboxStyle` and the re-render trigger is a kit SIGNATURE (`presetKey|layout`), not the preset key alone. Picking a preset card clears the manual pick (the preset's own layout wins); save and close clear it. Copy says it in three places: the Mailbox Layout card's description ("The layout travels with the theme…"), the Saved Themes label ("palette, fonts, CRT film & mailbox layout" + a hint), and the kit note; saved-theme rows show the layout's icon. Guarded by `test-mailbox-styles` (131).
- **Preset accessibility pass (same day, dev: "run through and clean up the preset themes, make sure they're okay and accessible").** A WCAG contrast audit over every shipped palette found: muted text at **3.8–4.5:1** on the lighter surfaces of ten terminal presets; secondary text under 4.5:1 on **six Agent presets** (down to 3.7 on Netrunner / Afterlife); muted text down to **2.7:1** on shard presets; two terminal presets naming boot animations that are NOT `BOOT_ANIMATIONS` keys (`glitch-stutter`, `instant-dump` → they rendered the base fade; now glitch-flood / crt-power-on); three presets with no mailbox layout. Fix = raise the LIGHTNESS of the failing text colour in HSL until it clears the target on its darkest-failing surface (hue and saturation untouched, so the identity holds — the scratchpad `fix-presets.mjs` approach; 34 values moved, most by one or two hex steps), mirrored into the five `[data-terminal-preset]` fallback rules in terminal.css; Netrunner and Combat Zone got their OWN `textMuted` because the root grey-violet `#8e8ea8` sat at 3.96 / 4.44 on their elevated surfaces (every other Agent preset inherits it fine). **The thresholds are now a durable sim — `dev/tools/sims/test-preset-accessibility.mjs` (270): terminal presets textPrimary ≥ 7, textSecondary / textMuted ≥ 4.5 on ALL FOUR surfaces, the three accents ≥ 3 on deep + surface, bgDeep on primary ≥ 4.5 (the TTY's inverted row), every enum field a registry key, a crt block, the CSS mirror equal to the JS; Agent presets the same text rules with the inherited or own muted; shard palettes text ≥ 7 / 4.5 / 4.5.** A new preset that fails it is not done.
- **CRT film v2 — ONE PANE OF GLASS, every layer a knob, three homes + a player cap (same day; dev: "the bezel doesn't look like a terminal… the glass is cool… all of this should be customizable", then "just worry about softness and increase that generally").** Supersedes the pseudo-element film described next. `applyCrt(el, crt)` now stamps attributes + `--ncm-crt-*` vars on a **WINDOW ROOT** (header included) and builds ONE click-through `.ncm-crt-pane` child with seven layers (cut [multiply] · light [screen] · grille · room [vignette/sheen/primary bloom] · grain · roll · bezel); glow + red/cyan fringe are a text-shadow on the host; the raster is masked toward the edges (curvature). **Looks:** off · glass · tube · arcade (`CRT_BASE` = the mock's values with softness raised); v1 keys map (fine→glass, classic/phosphor→tube, grille→arcade) so stored blocks need no migration. **A block is SPARSE** `{style,intensity,vignette,roll, …any moved layer}`; `effectiveCrt()` lays it over the look and scales the STRENGTH layers by intensity (35 = as designed; geometry never scales). **One config UI, three hosts:** `templates/partials/crt-panel.hbs` (`TEMPLATES.CRT_PANEL`, chips + intensity + vignette/band + an Advanced fold of one slider per layer + Reset) wired by `wireCrtPanel(panel,{draft,onChange})` in the Theme Customizer, the terminal config Theme tab, and — new — the **shard config Theme tab**. **Shards have their OWN film now**: `config.theme.crt`, else the preset's (`SHARD_PRESETS[k].crt`, all nine carry one); `ItemInboxApp.refreshCrt()` paints it on the shard window root over the player's. Terminals likewise paint on the WINDOW root via `TerminalViewerApp.refreshCrt()` (the viewer element carries none). **The player's cap** — Theme Customizer → Effects → "Film on terminals & shards": As designed / Dimmed / Off (`playerTheme.crtCap`, `capCrt`): a ceiling applied by both viewers on this client only (dim = half intensity, no motion); it never raises a film. **Second tuning pass (dev in Foundry: "too thick, too wide apart, too intense, no animation"), three causes:** (1) the looks were judged on **2× headless renders**, where a 1px line looks half as thick — **render CRT checks at `--force-device-scale-factor=1`**; (2) **softness was a `filter: blur`**, which smeared each 1px line into a 2–3px band — softness now LOWERS the lines' contrast in `crtAttrs` (never widens; the sim fails on a blur filter in crt.css); (3) the old player default `classic` was aliased to Tube, so every pre-v2 Agent got a bezel and a dark cut — `classic` now maps to **Glass**. Looks retuned at 1× (glass: 2px pitch, light .05, no cut), and every look carries a faint rolling band by default (its alpha scales with intensity). **Third pass (dev: "the terminal presets need fixing… still lines in the tabs on active"):** built-in terminal + shard films capped (glass ≤ 25, tube/arcade ≤ 30, rolling band on — the sim fails above that); **schema migration v2** (`MigrationRunner`, `tameCrtDeep`/`tameCrtBlock` in crt.js) brings every STORED film down to those levels — terminal/shard configs on world items and on actors, plus `terminalThemePresets`, `terminalPresetLibrary`, `shardThemePresets`, `shardPresetLibrary` — because a terminal set up from a preset carries its own copy of the old values; the **light line takes `--ncm-primary`**, so under screen blend it adds nothing to anything filled in the machine colour (active tab, selected TTY row, primary buttons) while still reading as a faint phosphor line on the dark; Tube's multiply CUT still crosses fills by design (Advanced → Cut 0 removes it). Three leftover baked films in terminal.css were deleted (login hero stripes, secure-shell console scanlines, CRT login-style scanlines) — **a `repeating-linear-gradient` line film anywhere outside crt.css is a bug**. **Fourth find (dev: "I turned off CRT film and still see the lines in the tabs"):** `styles/effects/scanlines.css` — a LEGACY body-class film (`body.ncm-scanlines .ncm-app:not([data-ncm-scanlines])::after`, 3px lines at z 300) — is DELETED. ThemeService still puts `ncm-scanlines` on the body when the player's Agent film is on, and the terminal's inner `.ncm-terminal-viewer.ncm-app` never carries the flag, so it drew lines over every terminal regardless of the terminal's own CRT setting. **Probes never set that body class, which is why every headless check looked clean** — when Foundry shows something the probe does not, look for body-class / setting-driven CSS the probe lacks. Sim `test-crt.mjs` 84; the files probe takes `?crt=glass|tube|arcade&ci=&cap=` through the REAL crt.js; mock `dev/mockups/crt-glass-mock.html` (serve it) is the tuning bench. Not live-tested.
- **ONE CRT film (same day — dev: "rather than building scanlines into each and every thing, one scanline layer on top that's well built and can be toggled and configured… I don't like the basic scanline stuff we have").** `scripts/utils/crt.js` + `styles/components/crt.css`. There were FOUR baked-in films — the terminal's always-on `::after`, the coarser one behind its Scanlines switch, atmosphere.css's player film on every window, item-inbox.css's on the shard root — **all deleted; don't add a fifth.** The film is pure CSS keyed on attributes that ONLY `applyCrt(el, crt)` stamps: `data-ncm-crt` = off | fine | classic | grille | phosphor, `--ncm-crt-intensity` 0..1, `data-ncm-crt-vignette`, `data-ncm-crt-roll`. `::after` is the raster in **SCREEN blend, light lines only** (third cut, same day — dev: "weird lines through a button": multiply/dark lines were INVISIBLE on a near-black UI, and normal blend with a dark line SLICED every bright button and selected row as hard as it crossed the background; a light line under screen reads as a raster on the dark and adds almost nothing on a bright fill, so **the lines live in the dark and the UI sits on top of them** — verified in the 2× harness over dark / surface / lit panels), `::before` the vignette / phosphor glow / rolling band; a `[data-ncm-admin-theme]` host never shows it; reduced motion stops the roll. `resolveCrt(src, fallback)` is pure (sim `test-crt.mjs`, 36): the `crt` block → the legacy `scanlines: true` boolean (→ classic, so old prefs keep their lines) → the caller's fallback (a terminal preset's `theme.crt`; all 12 carry one) → off; an unknown style falls back, never crashes. Hosts: `BaseApplication._applyAtmosphere` (every player window from `playerTheme.crt`; `scanlines: false` = `CRT_OFF`), the terminal viewer ROOT from the TERMINAL's own theme (its app root is forced off so the player's film can't double it), the shard viewer through BaseApplication. UI: Theme Customizer → Effects → **CRT Film** (the old Scanlines master switch, look chips `setCrtStyle`, intensity 5–100, vignette / roll — live on every open non-terminal window via `_applyLive`); the terminal config Theme tab has the same rows (`themeCrt`, draft key `_themeDraft.crt`, live on open viewers + the preview strip).

## Architecture: shared content renderers (shards + terminals)

Typed content (a SPREADSHEET / CALENDAR / SYSLOG / GALLERY / … vs. a plain message) renders through **one shared registry** so a file looks identical wherever it's opened — design doc §8c, §9b. The two halves live in `scripts/ui/content/ContentRenderers.js` + `templates/content/content-body.hbs` (`TEMPLATES.CONTENT_BODY`) and **must stay in lock-step**:

- **`CONTENT_TYPE_INFO`** — the single source of truth for per-type `{ label, icon, accent, indexIcon }`. **Moved here from `ItemInboxApp`** (it was a module-local const); the shard viewer now imports it. `ACCENT_HEX` (palette key → hex for chips/dots) lives here too.
- **`enrichContent(entry, { isGM, interactive })`** — turns a raw entry (`{ contentType, contentData, subject, from, body, timestamp, encrypted, decrypted, … }`) into the view-model the partial renders: `is<Type>` flags (generated from `CONTENT_TYPES`, so `avlog`→`isAvlog`), `formattedDate`, `contentTypeLabel`/`entryIcon`/`accentColor`, `isLocked` (`encrypted && !decrypted`), and per-type processed `contentData` (eddies/dossier/payload/avlog enrichers in `ENRICHERS`). `isGM`/`interactive` ride **on the enriched object** (not `../`) so the partial reads them directly.
- **`content-body.hbs`** renders ONLY the decrypted content body (the `{{#if isMessage}}…{{else if isEddies}}…` chain + fallback). The **caller owns** the corrupted/encrypted overlays and the entry header. Interactive affordances (claim/execute/goToScene) are gated on `interactive` — the shard passes `interactive:true`, read-only consumers pass `false`.

**Adding a content type = four touches, nothing else:** one `CONTENT_TYPES` entry · one `CONTENT_TYPE_INFO` row · (optional) one `ENRICHERS` fn · one `{{else if isX}}` branch in `content-body.hbs`. (CSS for a new type goes in `styles/components/content-types.css`, `.ncm-app`-scoped, **only `--ncm-*` tokens** so it renders in both viewers — never bare `--sp-*`.) A fifth touch to make it **authorable**: a `{{#if isX}}` fieldset + a `_collectX(data)` case in `DataShardComposer` (below).

**Composer shell redesign (2026-07-12, mock-approved `dev/mockups/composer-redesign-mock.html`).** The nine-equal-fieldsets stack became: a **pinned type bar** (type icon + name + one-line intro with a "How does it work?" expander `data-cmp-howbox`; **Change type** opens the 18 pills as a **grouped dropdown** `TYPE_GROUPS` — Comms&Records / Data&Finance / Media&Intel / Hazards; Example + a **Templates ▾ menu** replacing the two always-visible tpl bars) → the FORM as the scroller holding three **collapsible sections** (`.ncm-cmp-sec`, `data-cmp-sec`, state in `_secOpen`, toggled DOM-only so unsaved edits survive): **① CONTENT** (open; all per-type fieldsets, which shed their border chrome inside a section) · **② DELIVERY** (the old Entry Header — From/timestamp/folder) · **③ SECURITY & ACCESS** (per-entry encryption + network visibility; per-type unlock/declassify rows deliberately stay with their content) → a **pinned footer** (Cancel/Save — no more scrolling to save). Collapsed ②③ show **live summary chips** (`data-cmp-chip`, filled by `_syncCmpChips` in the debounced input handler — from/when/folder/enc/net). Sections MUST be `flex: 0 0 auto` (the form is a flex column — without it they shrink-clip instead of scrolling; headless `composer2.png`). Security auto-opens once when editing an entry that uses it (`_secAutoDone` guard so re-renders don't fight the user). **Entry Identity was DELETED** (dead UI — `entryIcon` was never collected; icon/accent derive from `CONTENT_TYPE_INFO`). **List engine v2:** every cell = a tiny label over its input (`_rowHtml` cellWrap — `lbl` in the field schemas; placeholder-only fields were the layperson's #1 confusion), numbered rows (`_renumberList` on add/remove), hover-revealed remove, rows as separated cards (`align-items:flex-end` since labels ride on top), `dl` key = a `<datalist>` (chat senders auto-collect via `_syncChatSenders`; record statuses static). **Per-type interactivity:** schematic **interactive blueprint stage** (see the schematic §), gallery rows show a **live thumbnail** (`.ncm-cl-thumb`, instant non-debounced echo), spreadsheet gets **focus mode** (⛶ on the CONTENT header → `.ncm-composer-shell--focus` hides preview + ②③; survives re-renders via `_cmpFocus`), **Enter = move down a cell** (adds a row on the last one) and a **sticky grid toolbar**. Window 920×640. **Polish round (same day, dev retest):** (1) modifier-less `.ncm-shard-btn`s rendered Foundry-white — the base class had NO background/color (only `--primary/--secondary/--danger` did); base now carries the secondary-style visuals + `width:auto !important` (modifiers still win — equal specificity, later in file). (2) Composer-scoped control normalization: `input[type=checkbox]` forced 13px (Foundry's huge default checkboxes were the "huge boxes") and `.ncm-config-input` height 26px. (3) **`.ncm-fg` field grid** — the scalar-field counterpart of the labeled list cells: every multi-field row (`ncm-config-row` with `margin-left:8px` label hacks + scattered inline max-widths — "textboxes all over the place") became label-over-input `.ncm-f` cells (`--fw` width var, `--flex`, `--check` for bottom-aligned checkboxes) that wrap into a tidy grid. Swept: banking account+siphon, syslog host/path/prompt, gallery source+lock-roll, document letterhead (8 fields, one grid) + declassify, record type, chatlog app/channel/owner (owner autocompletes from the senders datalist), schematic doc-ref/scene, spreadsheet + dossier declassify, avlog seal, malware type/threat/size + deploy gate. Grep `margin-left:8px` in the composer template = 0; keep it that way — new multi-field rows use `.ncm-fg`.

**WYSIWYG editors (2026-07-12) — "the editor IS the artifact".** The dev's core ask (took three rounds to land — the layout polish wasn't it): each type's data entry should feel like USING the thing being made, the reason the spreadsheet grid editor works. `_wzBuilderFor(key)` returns a per-type card/line builder that replaces `_rowHtml` for: **chatMsgs** (texting app — feed of side-switching bubbles, compose bar, Enter sends, click-to-edit), **syslogLines** (live console — colored severity KEY cycles info/ok/warn/err/debug, Enter starts the next line, ★ mark), **credEntries** (vault cards — reveal eye [editor-only, swaps input type], clickable strength pips, LOCKED toggle), **galImages** (thumbnail grid `.ncm-cl-list.ncm-galgrid` — live thumb, corner 🚩💀🔒 toggles, DV box appears only when locked), **docParas** (write ON the page — heading + textarea blocks, hover toolbar, REDACT renders the black bars live), **recRows** (database cards — initials avatar fills as you type, ↻ cycles common statuses), **calEvents** (timeline rail), **bankTxns** (ledger lines — ▼/▲ chip flips debit/credit and recolors the amount, ⏳ pending). Note's textarea is styled as the sticky paper itself (`.ncm-notepaper`, `data-paper` follows the color select live). **The save/prefill contract is unchanged:** every visual item is still a `.ncm-cl-row` whose NAMED controls match the field schema — schema checkboxes stay real (CSS-hidden `.ncm-wz-flag`) checkboxes (`_collectList` reads `.checked`), select-typed fields ride hidden inputs (collect only reads `.value`); compose-bar/UI-only inputs are UNNAMED (the ss-grid precedent). Generic interactions: `wzCheck` (toggle a schema checkbox + `is-<f>` row class), `wzCycle` (value list into a hidden input + `_wzRepaint`), `wzReveal`, `wzPip`. **CSS ordering gotcha (bit twice):** the base `.ncm-cl-row`/`.ncm-cl-list` rules sit LATER in item-inbox.css than the WYSIWYG blocks — per-type layout overrides (grid displays, align-items) need the compound selector (`.ncm-cl-list.ncm-galgrid`, `.ncm-cl-row.ncm-credcard`) to win. Foundry centers input text — the composer forces `text-align:left` on all text controls (bank amount re-rights itself). Headless-verified all eight (`wysiwyg4.png`, `chatedit.png`). **Sizing pass (dev feedback):** composer inputs are 22px tall, number boxes (DVs, amounts) drop the spinner chrome and cap width so an unconstrained one can't stretch the row. **DV/number-box `!important` fix (2026-07-12):** the height/min-width rules lacked `!important`, so the **Cyberpunk RED system's aggressive input styling won** — DV boxes rendered taller AND wider than their text siblings (invisible in headless, which has no CPR stylesheet). Now `.ncm-data-shard-composer input[type="number"]` forces `height:22px !important; min-height:0 !important; min-width:0 !important; max-width:84px !important` and `.ncm-config-input` height is `!important` — so a `--fw:60px` DV cell stays 60px. Any new small-number input inherits this; don't drop the `!important`. **Real dates ALWAYS use the module's CyberTimePicker, never raw date inputs** — the calendar card's date is a `.ncm-ctp-trigger` targeting the row's hidden indexed input by name (`_onOpenTimePicker` binds by input name, so per-row `calEvents__when-N` targets just work); syslog/chat/gallery time fields stay free text ON PURPOSE (they're diegetic clock strings like `04:12:09`, not dates).

**MESSAGE body = a rich-text editor (2026-07-12).** The plain body textarea became a `.ncm-rte` editor: a formatting **toolbar** (bold/italic/underline/strikethrough · H3/quote/bullet/numbered list · link/color/emoji · clear) over a **contenteditable** `.ncm-rte__surface`, with a hidden `<textarea name="body">` mirroring its HTML for `FormDataExtended` (and `_collectEntryData` reads the surface's `innerHTML` directly so no keystroke is missed). Commands run via `document.execCommand` (deprecated but works in Foundry's Chromium); the toolbar/popover buttons `preventDefault` their mousedown (wired in `_onRender`) so the surface **keeps its selection** while a command runs. Actions: `rteCmd` (execCommand), `rteLink` (a Dialog — robust vs Electron's disabled `window.prompt`; saves+restores the selection range around the dialog), `rteColor`/`rteColorPick` (swatch popover → `foreColor`, `RTE_COLORS`), `rteEmoji`/`rteEmojiPick` (reuses `CHAT_EMOJI`, `insertText` at caret). The body renders in the viewer via `{{{body}}}` (already raw HTML), so formatting passes straight through. The `.ncm-rte__surface` styles h3/blockquote/lists/links + an `:empty::before` placeholder. Verified: `test-message-rte.mjs` 6/6 (real enricher+partial: bold/color/list/quote/emoji pass through) + headless `message-rte.png`. Reusable for other body fields later (document/note) — keep it message-scoped for now.

**Authoring composer (`DataShardComposer`) is shared by shards AND terminals.** Generalized via a `target` descriptor `{ kind:'shard'|'terminal', item, accountId? }` (legacy `shardItem` still accepted = shard). `_item`/`_isTerminal`/`_service`/`_journal()` resolve the right side; `_persistEntry(entryData)` creates via `dataShardService.addMessage` or `terminalService.addAccountMessage`, and edits via a generic `page.update` (find page by `messageId` in `_journal()`). The form is read by **`_collectEntryData({validate})`** (one method, shared by Save and the **live preview**). **From + Timestamp live in ONE shared "Entry Header" fieldset** rendered for every type (the 18 per-type timestamp rows and the message-only From row were removed — the dev couldn't edit from/timestamp on typed entries without flipping back to Message; keep it single-instance, duplicate `name="timestamp"` inputs corrupt `FormDataExtended`). **From ↔ GM Master List:** the From input carries a `<datalist>` of every master-contact email (context `masterEmails`), and after a successful save `_syncFromToMasterList` runs the account link-or-create precedent — an email-shaped From (`includes('@')`, no spaces) not in the directory creates a master contact (name title-cased from the local part, provenance note naming the item) and toasts the GM; non-email Froms (SECCAM-B2) are system nodes and skip; failures never block the save. **Templates & Examples** (top of the form, `EntryTemplates.js`): per-type built-in **Load example** (`BUILTIN_EXAMPLES` — the macro seeds, timestamps stripped) + a GM **template library** (world setting `entryTemplates` `[{id,name,type,entry}]`; save-current-as / load / delete / JSON import-export, envelope `{ncmEntryTemplates:1}` — the spam-template pattern). Loading either sets `this.editData` **without an `entryId`** and re-renders — the whole edit-prefill machinery (list rows, selects, spreadsheet grid) reconstructs the form, and `_persistEntry`/`isEditing`/`get title()` all key on `editData?.entryId` (NOT bare `editData`) so a loaded template still saves as a NEW entry. Live preview: a pane (`.ncm-composer-preview-body`) that re-renders the real `content-body` partial (`enrichContent` → `renderTemplate(TEMPLATES.CONTENT_BODY)`) on every `input`/`change` (debounced in `_onRender`). Repeatable rows use a **generic list engine** — schema arrays (e.g. `BANK_TXN_FIELDS`) drive `_rowHtml`/`_onAddListRow`/`_onRemoveListRow`/`_collectList`; containers are `[data-list="<key>"]`, rows `.ncm-cl-row`, inputs named `<key>__<field>-<idx>` (prefix-matched on read). `TerminalViewerApp._onNewMessage`/`_onEditMessage` open this composer with a terminal target. **Built so far: Banking · Calendar · Syslog · Credentials · Gallery · Document · Chat Log · Records · Schematic · Note · Malware** (banking = lore/account/txn-list/siphon; most others are list-engine forms — calendar auto-builds the `categories` color map at save via `_calendarCategories`, preserving colors already assigned when editing; note/malware are scalar forms with textareas). List rows can wrap to a second visual line via a `{ brk: true }` schema entry (renders `.ncm-cl-brk`, `flex-basis:100%`; `_collectList` skips it) — used by calendar/syslog/credentials so flex fields aren't squeezed out at the ~470px form width. The engine also supports `type:'date'` (native date input; ISO values sliced to `yyyy-mm-dd` on prefill) and select values never count toward the row's "has content" check (a default `<select>` isn't user intent — empty rows are dropped at collect). New entries seed one blank row per list. **The spreadsheet grid editor** was the marquee editor piece (all 18 types are now composer-authorable): an editable table (column label + type per column; type drives the viewer's alignment/pills/Σ) where clicking a cell selects it (`focusin` → `.is-active`) and a format toolbar applies **bold / text color / fill / flag+reason / redact / clear**. Formatting is **DOM-only state** — data attributes on each `<td>` plus mirror classes (the same `ncm-ct-c--x`/`ncm-ct-bg--x` utilities the renderer uses, so the editor IS the preview) — which means add/remove row/column operations move formatting with the cells and `_collectSpreadsheet` just walks the table into the authored shape (`columns`/`rows`/`flagged`/`redacted`/`flagReasons`/`totals:true`). Grid inputs are deliberately **unnamed** (invisible to `FormDataExtended`; their `input` events still bubble to the form and refresh the live preview). Editing reconstructs the grid from `contentData` (`_ssBuild` → `_ssRender`); an authored `totals` array collapses to `totals: true` on re-save, and `selected` re-derives from the first flagged cell. **CSV import** (`ssImportCsv` → hidden `<input type=file>` → `_ssApplyCsv`) loads a local .csv/.tsv into the grid, replacing it: `_parseCsv` is a minimal RFC-4180 parser (quoted fields, `""` escapes, CRLF, embedded newlines) with the delimiter (`,` `;` tab) auto-detected from the first line; `_inferColType` guesses each column (all-status-words → `status`, numeric with a currency mark/`eb` → `currency`, numeric → `number`, else `text`); the "first row = column labels" checkbox controls header handling (unchecked → columns labeled A, B, C…); imports over `SS_IMPORT_ROW_CAP` (500) keep the first 500 with a warning, and the file name auto-fills the empty File-name field. Parser + inference are unit-tested standalone (scratchpad `test-csv.mjs` pattern) since they're pure functions.

**Self-explanatory copy (§12b).** The composer shows a **per-type intro banner** (`TYPE_INTROS` map → `typeIntro` in context, rendered `.ncm-composer-intro` under the type pills) — a plain-language *what it is* + *How:* walkthrough for every type — plus `.ncm-config-hint` field hints. Player-facing affordances in the **viewer** carry **immersive** (no rules-speak) native `data-tooltip`s — siphon / REUSED badge / crack / reveal — per the standing "no immersion-breaking rules-text" rule (the siphon **DV** label is kept; DV is player-facing CPR vocabulary).

**Types shipped (18 — the §8c catalog is COMPLETE; AUDIO/VIDEO ride `avlog`):** the original 6 (`message`/`eddies`/`dossier`/`payload`/`avlog`/`location`) plus 5 Phase-2 types plus wave 3 (`gallery`/`document`/`chatlog`) and wave 4 (`record`/`schematic`/`note`/`malware`), both 2026-07-11. The Phase-2 design thesis is **"every file looks like the app that made it"** (fixes the monotone/illegible first cut): each renders like its own program. Authored `contentData` shapes (with all options) are documented inline in each `ENRICHERS` fn; `dev/tools/test-terminal-setup.macro.js` seeds one of each exercising the options.
- `spreadsheet` — cell-ref/formula bar, A/B/C column letters + row numbers, frozen header, **per-cell formatting** (`{value, bold, color, bg}`), typed columns (`text/number/currency/date/status` → align + **status pills**), `flagged`/`redacted` cells (+ `flagReasons`), `totals` (Σ row), `selected` cell.
- `calendar` — scheduling app (redesign 2026-07-13, #34): **TIMELINE ↔ MONTH view toggle** (authored `defaultView`; month grid = one grid per event-bearing month, ‹ › nav, today ring, category-colored pips that **jump back to the timeline** scrolled to the event), a **NEXT-EVENT hero strip** (nearest readable upcoming + compact time-aware countdown `in 1d 4h` via `_relCal`), past events **collapsed behind a "SHOW PAST (N)" divider**, per-event GM **Go-to-Scene** (`sceneId`, `interactive`-gated like schematic), colored meta chips (time/location/who/note), urgency bar. **`when` is a FULL ISO date+time** (the composer's single When picker writes it; the enricher derives the time chip from an ISO that carries a clock, and a legacy free-text `time` string still wins). **Locked events fog for players** (`locked` + per-event `unlockSkill`/`unlockDV`/`traceOnFail` beating the vault-level pair): date masked `??`, title/chips/scene stripped from their DOM, the fogged card **sorts to the END of upcoming** (its rail slot must not leak the date) and drops off the player's month grid entirely; one DECRYPT roll (`contentUnlock` kind `calendar`, `data-index` = the ORIGINAL events index — stamped as `idx` before sorting) reveals it. GM sees the real card + a LOCKED chip and an italic "encrypted" pip. The view toggle / past collapse / month nav / pip-jump are display-only state via a **document-delegated capture click on `[data-cal-act]`** (the record-search precedent — deliberately NOT a `data-action`, so it works in all three viewers + the composer preview with zero per-app wiring). **Composer = a card builder** (records precedent — cards injected in `_onRender`, `_collectCalendar()` walks the DOM, all inputs unnamed except the randomID-named When target): a **category strip** owns the palette (`[data-cal-cats]` chips = `{name: color}` source of truth; swatch click cycles the CT palette, removing a category clears it off its events; per-card typo-proof quick-pick chips), cards mirror the render (live day/month block + past dim + `data-ct-color` edge via `_calRepaintCard`), the rail **auto-sorts on date pick**, cards **collapse** (summary line) and **duplicate**, lock row (skill dropdown/DV/trace) + scene-picker Dialog, and **CSV import/export** (`_calApplyCsv` reuses `_parseCsv`; timezone-less `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM` strings parse as **UTC** via `_calParseCsvWhen` — `Date.parse` would shift them by the GM's local offset; unmergeable legacy time strings survive via `data-legacy-time`). **The calendar TICKS LIVE** (#34b): `refreshCalendarClocks()` (ContentRenderers, exported) re-derives countdown labels, urgency-bar fills, soon/past dimming, the NEXT strip, and the month grid's today ring from the time provider — driven by a module-level 1s `setInterval` (the MessageViewer HUD-clock pattern; ~free when no calendar is on screen) plus Foundry's `updateWorldTime` hook, so **Simple Calendar advances (and rewinds) move an open calendar instantly**. It patches DOM keyed on the `data-ts` each READABLE event carries — **fogged events deliberately carry no `data-ts`** (it would leak the withheld date) and the ticker skips them. The urgency bar is now ALWAYS in the DOM (CSS-hidden unless `.is-soon`) so a card can *become* soon while the window sits open; a NEXT strip whose event passes hides itself (`.is-over`) rather than showing a stale "next". Structural changes (an event re-filing under SHOW PAST, the strip picking a new next event) still wait for the next render — the dim/label/hide keep the display honest in the meantime.
- `syslog` — console: filter legend w/ counts, line-number gutter, **icon severity badges** (info/ok/warn/err/debug), `mark` callout, scanlines, blinking prompt; toggles `showLineNumbers/showFilter/scanlines/showPrompt`.
- `credentials` — vault: bold host, **user (cyan) + key (gold)** stand out, reveal eye, strength meter, auto **REUSED ×N** badge (secret shared across hosts = the pivot signal), `encrypted && !cracked` → "CRACK TO REVEAL" button.
- `banking` — finance app (upgrade 2026-07-12): a **bank letterhead** (lore) + a **credit-CARD hero** (`.ncm-ct-bank__card` — `bankColor` gradient via `--card-c`, chip, **masked number** `•••• 0007` from `accountNumber`, owner+type, balance, and a **status badge**), cleared/`held` split, running-balance ledger with **color-coded category icons** (`BANK_CAT`). **`status`** (active/frozen/flagged/overdrawn/vip → `statusColor` badge; **frozen blocks siphon** in the enricher AND `runSiphon`). Composer adds: a **card-color picker** (`bankColor`), a **status** `<select>` (`data-edit-select="bankStatus"` → `selectMap` `cd.status`), the txn category is a **datalist combobox** (`ncm-bankcat`), account-type is a datalist, and a **↻ sync-balance** button (`bankSyncBalance` recomputes balance = Σ credits − debits from the txn rows). **SIPHON is now a bespoke FUI heist** (`scripts/ui/content/BankHeist.js` + `styles/components/bank-heist.css`, `@import` after security-console.css) — `runBankHeist(opts)` REPLACES the SecurityConsole for siphon: a framework-free promise modal appended into the viewer (`host` → inherits its `--ncm-*` preset), source→packet-stream→wallet FUI with rotating secure-channel rings, corner brackets, hex noise; pick the amount (slider+number), **JACK IN** rolls the security check (`SkillService.performCheck`, posts the chat card), then the **DRAIN animation** (source balance ticks down, wallet ticks up via rAF `animateCount`, progress fills) → TRANSFER COMPLETE / TRACE LOCKED; resolves `{ok,amount,total,cancelled}` (same shape `runSiphon` read). GM-without-character → force-transfer (no roll); reduced-motion → instant. **Siphon risk model (2026-07-12b):** **DV scales with the amount** (`siphonDVScale` + `siphonDVPer` — base DV +1 per N eddies; `runBankHeist`'s `effDV()` recomputes it **live** as the amount slider drags, shown in the status line, and rolls against it). **Failure = lockout then freeze** (`siphonLockout` + `siphonMaxAttempts` + `siphonLockoutMin`): `runSiphon` checks `SecurityService.isLockedOut(actor.id, 'siphon::<messageId>')` before the heist (blocks with a countdown), `initTracking`s the limits, and on a failed roll `recordFailedAttempt` — once the attempts are burned it **locks out AND persists `status:'frozen'`** on the account (via `persistContentData`, GM writes / player relays), so the account can't be siphoned again by anyone. Verified: `test-bank.mjs` 9/9 + `test-siphon-cfg.mjs` 5/5 (config collect + `effDV` +1-per-N formula) + headless `bank-render.png` (card) & `heist-render.png` (FUI). Mock `dev/mockups/banking-redesign-mock.html`.
- `gallery` — photo app / evidence board **OR (`mode:'feed'`) a live camera multiplex** (redesign 2026-07-13, #36). ONE type, two skins — the mode re-reads the SAME `images[]` (image = camera channel, label = CAM id, `corrupted` = OFFLINE, `locked` = ENCRYPTED CHANNEL), so there's no 19th content type and no migration.
  - **STILLS**: thumb grid (or `layout:'strip'`) — a toolbar with **clickable filter chips** (solo FLAGGED / ENCRYPTED, the syslog-filter model), the **first flagged viewable frame as a 2×2 hero** (`isHero`, evidence-board focal point — no new authored field; strip layout and no-flag galleries stay uniform), CCTV label/time chips **on** the image, a hover caption scrim (always-on for the hero), and `corrupted` → animated NO-SIGNAL static.
  - **FEED**: cams drift-zoom + scan-sweep under a scanline film with a **ticking clock** (`data-feed-clock` → `refreshFeedClocks()`, riding the calendar ticker's 1s interval + `updateWorldTime` hook). Per-cam states: ONLINE · OFFLINE (authored dead **or** player-`cut`) · ENCRYPTED · **LOOPED** (frozen frame + a stale `loopedTime` — deliberately NO `data-feed-clock`, the stale clock IS the heist tell, and it survives every clock mode). Per-cam `signal` (1–4 bars), `sceneId` (GM Go-to-Scene in the monitor).
  - **Camera clock is an authored choice (`clock`), because "live" isn't always right.** `'game'` (default) reads the **time provider** (`_nowMs`) and formats via `formatCyberDate`, so the wall is in sync with every other NCM clock and Simple Calendar / world-time / disguised modes all move it. **But in `manual`/`world-time` provider modes the game clock only advances when the GM advances it**, so a "LIVE" feed would sit on a frozen timestamp and read as if every camera were already looped — hence `'real'`, which ticks off the wall clock regardless (display-only flavor, NOT a countdown, so it may bypass the provider; the standing "all expiries go through the time provider" rule is untouched). Also `'authored'` (each cam shows its own diegetic `time` string, frozen — recovered footage, not a live wall) and `'off'` (no timestamps). The **attribute value carries the mode** (`data-feed-clock="game|real"`) so one page can hold both kinds of feed; `refreshFeedClocks` computes each mode's label once per tick. Composer: a **Camera clock** select in the feed row (with the manual/world-time caveat spelled out in the hint).
  - **Cam actions (`camAction` → `SiphonFlow.runCamAction`)**, wired in all three viewers: **LOOP** (freeze the channel on old footage, DV `loopDV`) and **CUT** (kill it, DV `cutDV`) roll via SecurityConsole using the gallery's `camSkill` (default Electronics/Security Tech) and persist `looped`/`cut` through `persistContentData` (GM writes, players relay — `canPersistContent` guards first); a miss fires the vault-level `traceOnFail`. **Blank `loopDV`/`cutDV` = that action is OFF.** The **GM force-toggles** (no roll, the `schemLockToggle` model) and gets **RESTORE** on looped/cut cams — but NOT on an *authored* dead cam (that's authored content, edited in the composer).
  - **PTZ (`ptz: true`)** — a steerable camera head: the authored image is far bigger than the frame and the monitor view becomes a **clipped viewport you drive** (D-pad · arrow keys · drag · +/− zoom · ⌂ recenter · **PATROL** auto-sweep when nobody's driving), with a **minimap** and a PAN/TILT/zoom readout. Pure display state (`_ptzMem`, keyed `messageId::index`, survives re-renders; **nothing persists — looking around needs no GM online**). Optional **`controlDV`** gates TAKE CONTROL (`runCamAction` act `control` → `grantPtzControl`, a client-side session grant; until then the player just watches the patrol). GM always drives. Reduced-motion = a static center crop.
  - **Lightbox = gallery navigation.** `openGalleryImage(target, {host})` collects the clicked thumb's **sibling frames** inside its `[data-gal-box]` → ‹ › arrows + a counter + arrow-key nav. Locked/dead frames never render a `galleryView` button, so they're skipped **by construction** — nothing leaks. Single-image callers (schematic stage, document figures, chat photos) get no arrows. A feed cam opens the **monitor skin** (REC + live clock + channel counter + the LOOP/CUT/TAKE-CONTROL/GM-Go-to-Scene rail); a PTZ cam swaps the stage for the steerable viewport. GM **SHOW PLAYERS** still broadcasts via `ImagePopout.shareImage`. **The PTZ panel needs a DEFINITE size** (`.ncm-imgv--ptz`) — the stage is a `height:100%` viewport, not an `<img>`, so an auto-height panel collapses it to zero ("a monitor with no picture").
  - Per-image **`locked: true`** renders an ENCRYPTED tile (the `<img>` src and caption are **not in the DOM** until revealed) with a DECRYPT roll vs `unlockSkill`/`unlockDV` (per-frame beats vault-level; see roll-to-unlock). The DECRYPT is now a **full-width bar owning the tile's bottom edge** (`.ncm-ct-gal__unlockbar`) — the old centered pill was squeezed inside a 128px cell.
  - **Composer = a contact sheet**: the thumb **IS** the picker (click empty → FilePicker, click filled → lightbox; the path row reveals on hover), **⋮⋮ drag-to-reorder** (only the handle is `draggable` — a draggable card breaks text selection in its inputs; `_collectList` walks DOM order so no input renaming is needed), auto frame numbers, **IMPORT FOLDER** (`FilePicker.browse` → every image in a directory becomes a card, the CCTV-dump workflow), STILLS/FEED + GRID/STRIP **segmented controls** (hidden inputs `galMode`/`galLayout` — the login-style save contract), and feed-only rows (Loop/Cut/Ctl DVs + a scene-picker chip) revealed by `[data-gal-cfg][data-gal-mode="feed"]`. `looped`/`cut`/`loopedTime` are in `GAL_IMG_FIELDS` so **table state rides through an edit** — re-saving a feed mid-heist doesn't un-loop a camera.
  - **CSS gotcha (bit us):** the stills grid sizes rows with an explicit **`grid-auto-rows`**, NOT per-cell `aspect-ratio`. With auto rows, a hero spanning two of them fights the aspect-ratio cells and trailing rows collapse — the "flagged frames are squished vertically" bug. Fixed row height ⇒ uniform cells and a hero that is exactly 2 rows + the gap.
  - Verified: `test-gallery.mjs` 41/41 through the real `enrichContent` + real partial (hero rule, filter arming, feed states, GM-vs-player affordances, PTZ grants, and four **leak** assertions: locked src/caption and cut-cam src are never in the DOM) + headless renders of stills, feed, the PTZ monitor, and the composer against the real CSS.
- `document` — memo/report viewer (upgrade 2026-07-12): doc-type tag + **classification stamp** (SECRET/EYES ONLY/BLACK → red, else amber), letterhead meta, **watermark ghost** (`data-watermark` + `::before`). **Body blocks now have kinds**: `text` (heading + inline-HTML `text` — rendered RAW `{{{text}}}` so bold/italic pass through; `quote`/`redacted` variants), `kind:'list'` (`ordered?` + `items[]` → `<ol>`/`<ul>`), `kind:'figure'` (`image`+`caption` → `<figure>`, `galleryView` lightbox). Legacy flat paragraphs (no kind) read as text. **`date` is now the CyberTimePicker** (ISO stored; enricher formats to `dateLabel` via `formatCyberDate` dateOnly, legacy free-text shown as-is). **`signedImage`** (a scanned signature image) beats the `signed` text line.
