Resolution is `resolveTerminalSound(setKey, soundId)` (helpers.js — unknown set/unmapped id = identity, so legacy configs are untouched). **Every terminal-played sound must route through it**: `TerminalViewerApp._playTermSound` (overlay-aware — a staged preset kit previews its sound set too) covers boot/login/net-join/exfil-FX, and `TerminalAuthAdapter._play` covers the method-card dialog's password/skill results. `TerminalService`'s convert/relock sounds are deliberately unrouted (GM authoring feedback, not machine personality). Picker = the Boot & Login tab's **Sound Profile** card row (hidden `soundSet` input, the login-style-card save contract) with an **Audition** button that plays boot → login-success → login-fail through the selected set (real `setTimeout` — UI feedback, not game time). Each `TERMINAL_PRESETS` entry carries a `soundSet`, staged/saved with the kit like loginStyle. (Related fix: `SOUND_PATHS['shard-decrypt']` pointed at the nonexistent `shard/decrypt.ogg` while the real file is `security/shard-decrypt.ogg` — the shard-unlock splash AND the terminal login flourish were silently mute; the sound-set logic sim in scratchpad `test-soundsets.mjs` now asserts every referenced asset exists on disk.) The screensaver also carries `iconMode` (`boot` follow-the-boot-icon | `fa` | `image` | `none`), `faIcon`/`imageUrl`, and `motion` (`drift`/`bounce`/`static` — `data-ss-motion` on the overlay), resolved by `_screensaverContext`. The Boot & Login tab has **live previews for both the login style and the screensaver**: `_syncLoginPreview` builds a mini mock of the selected style from the terminal's REAL accounts (`.ncm-cfg-lprev-*`), `_syncSsPreview` mirrors the logo/text/motion (`.ncm-cfg-ssprev`). **TERMINAL_PRESETS is now a 12-entry library** (original 5 + trauma-team / militech-checkpoint / ncpd-precinct / nomad-rig / bd-lounge / maelstrom-chopshop / blackwall-relay), each bundling theme+security+boot+loginStyle+**login (MOTD/welcome)+screensaver+folderNames**+seed accounts — every built-in is a COMPLETE terminal kit. **A preset card stages the FULL KIT** (the "not obvious presets affect boot" fix): palette+fonts ride `_themeDraft` (live preview), and `_presetKit` (the preset KEY) overlays the Boot & Login/Identity tabs' context (boot fields, login style, MOTD/welcome, screensaver, folder names) — so Save reads everything straight from the (kit-prefilled) DOM inputs, then seeds the preset's accounts only when the terminal has none; a staged kit shows an amber notice bar on BOTH the Theme and Boot & Login tabs, with a **"palette only"** escape (`clearPresetKit`). Kit cleared on save/close. **Open viewers preview the staged state live** via the config-preview channel: `_applyThemeLive` pushes an overlay (`{theme, boot, loginStyle, login, screensaver, folderNames}`) to every open viewer of the item → `TerminalViewerApp.setConfigPreview(overlay, {rerender})`; the viewer's `_effectiveConfig()` **shallow-merges** it over the saved config (shallow on purpose — the draft theme's deleted overrides must fall through, a deep merge would resurrect them) and all render paths read it. `rerender` fires only on KIT transitions — palette-only updates ride cheap inline vars so slider drags can't cause render storms. Overlay cleared (rerender:true) on save/close/"palette only". **Boot entrance animations are real now** — `BOOT_ANIMATIONS` (standard-fade / holographic-snap / scan-sweep / warm-dissolve / glitch-flood / crt-power-on), CSS variants keyed by `data-boot-animation` on BOTH `.ncm-term__boot` and the config preview splash (picking an animation chip replays it in the preview via attr-swap + forced reflow, and the preview strip has an explicit **▶ REPLAY** button — `[data-boot-replay]`); legacy/unknown values render the base look. **GM EDIT gear on the login screen** (`.ncm-term__login-gm`, hero stat + CRT top bar, gated `isGM`) opens the config without logging in. **Full Terminal Preset Library** (Theme tab, world setting `terminalPresetLibrary`): "Save This Terminal" snapshots theme/boot/loginStyle/login/screensaver/folderNames/metadata/soundSet + account SEEDS (`_collectFullPreset` strips ids + masterContactId/actorId — world links don't travel); Apply confirms, overwrites those config blocks, and seeds accounts only when the terminal has none; rows offer overwrite/export/delete and the JSON envelope is `{ncmTerminalPreset:1, name, config}` (import appends to the library, never auto-applies). The config **Boot & Login tab** (renamed from Boot) mirrors the Theme tab's live-preview discipline: a data-bound boot-splash preview strip, an icon-source segmented control with a **searchable visual icon picker** (`TERMINAL_ICON_LIBRARY` — curated FA6-FREE classes only; an invalid class renders an EMPTY square, the arasaka lesson) + free-class input + image FilePicker, animation/speed **chips**, a "generate flavor lines" dice (`BOOT_FLAVOR_LINES`), **login-style cards**, and MOTD/welcome/screensaver fields — all mirrored into hidden inputs bearing the ORIGINAL field names (the account-editor save contract; wiring in `_wireBootTab`). Wallpaper/effects live in the **Theme tab** as draft keys (live-preview + Save like colors; effects preview only on an open viewer, the strip can't show them).

**Authoring surfaces.** GM: right-click an item → **Convert to Terminal** (instant + opens `TerminalConfigApp`) / Open / Configure / Remove (`ShardSheetOverride`, directory badge + left-click-to-open mirror the shard pattern; unconvert re-renders the directory so the badge clears). The `TerminalConfigApp` (tabs Identity/Accounts/Theme/Boot/Network/Exfil, reuses `ncm-cfg-*` styling, import/export JSON) and an in-viewer GM **EDIT** gear + per-message edit/delete + New Message + **Saved/Trash toggles** (bookmark = `status.saved`, archive-box↔restore = `status.deleted`, written per-leaf via `TerminalService.updateAccountMessage({status})`; the red trash-can stays the separate confirmed *permanent* delete). Admin Panel → **Terminals tab** (`TerminalsTab`, tab key `6`) is a **split-panel manager** (2026-07-11 redesign, NetworkManagement-Scenes pattern): summary strip → toolbar (debounced search over name/org/preset/account names, sort `<select>` [manual `change` listener — the standing gotcha], All/Online/Locked filter chips, Import) → left = paginated row list (10/page, `TerminalsTab.PAGE_SIZE`, selection persists via `_selectedId`, auto-selects first visible) → right = the selected terminal's detail (identity+classification, facts strip: preset/login style/sound set/uplink [`getUplink().network?.name` else LOCAL ONLY]/reach/exfil, account chips with lock/admin/hidden/net-gated badges, live sessions, actions). **"Reset" = `TerminalService.relock`** (it already wipes sessions + lockouts + gmBypass and returns the uplink to default — the button is just honestly labeled now); **Export** downloads the full config as `{ncmTerminal:1, name, config}` JSON. New actions `terminalSelectAdmin`/`terminalFilterAdmin`/`terminalPageAdmin`/`terminalExportAdmin` follow the static-delegator pattern. **Buttons are the tab's own `.ncm-term-admin-btn`** (text buttons: auto width, mono uppercase, `--primary`/`--danger`/`--icon` variants; Remove sits right of a flex spacer) — do NOT use the shared `.ncm-action-btn` here, it's a **26px icon button** (tab-shards.css) and text labels inside it render crushed against the borders. The detail facts are a **labeled key/value grid** (`.ncm-term-admin-fact`, auto-fill minmax(128px,1fr): Uplink · Reach · Exfil · Preset · Login · Sound · Location), not icon chips. Layout headless-verified against the real admin theme (`term-admin2.png`).

**Config sheet scroll contract:** `terminal-config.hbs`'s root MUST carry `.ncm-cfg` and the panel wrapper MUST be `.ncm-cfg-content` — that pair (flex column + `flex:1; overflow-y:auto`, from shard-config.css) is the ONLY scroll path, because `.ncm-item-inbox-config .window-content` is `overflow:hidden !important`. A rename to anything else (the original build used a CSS-less `.ncm-cfg-panels`) makes every tab unscrollable and Save unreachable.

**Account editor UX (mock-approved rebuild, config-account.hbs + `_wireAccountEditor`).** Six widgets, all wired via manual listeners in `_onRender`: **(A) Link Identity picker** — live search over master contacts + world actors (`_identityCandidates()`, actors deduped when already linked to a contact); picking autofills name/email/portrait **DOM-only** (`_applyIdentityPick` — a render would discard unsaved edits) and stages the link in `this._editorLink`, which overrides the stored link in `_editorContext` and at save (reset on add/edit/cancel/save). **(B)** portrait tile = the FilePicker button. **(C)** clearance chips (`CLEARANCE_PRESETS` + AUTO + custom) and classification pills. **(D)** last-access chips stamping the game clock (`timeService.getCurrentTimeMs` → `formatCyberDate`) + datetime-local fallback; free text stays legal (badge flavor, not a timer). **(E)** security method cards with toggle knobs (hidden checkbox + styled knob), skill `<select>` from `SKILL_MAP` (a legacy value is kept in the list), key-item picker (the shared name picker), password generator (`PASS_WORDS`). **(F)** sticky Save footer (`.ncm-acct-foot`, sticks to the `.ncm-cfg-content` scrollport) + dirty indicator. **The save contract is unchanged:** chips/pills mirror their state into hidden inputs bearing the ORIGINAL field names (`acctClearance`/`acctClassification`/`secAuthLogic`/`secFailureMode`), so `_onSaveAccount` reads one contract — keep it that way when adding widgets.

**Accounts ↔ GM Master Contacts (link-or-create).** On account save, a non-empty email resolves against the master directory: `getByEmail` match → link; no match → `MasterContactService.addContact` (name/email/portrait/role/org/location + a terminal-provenance note) — so terminal identities are real, portrait-bearing directory entries. Precedence: an explicit picker choice/unlink this session (`_editorLink`) → the stored link; an existing link survives unless the email now matches a *different* contact; the account pulls the linked contact's portrait when it has none. Unlink + save re-links if the email still matches (documented in the editor hint).

**Scene access pins (drag a terminal onto the canvas).** `scripts/integrations/TerminalScenePins.js` (registered in `registerTerminalSystem`, ready/64, instance at `game.nightcity.terminalScenePins`). A GM drags a terminal item onto a scene (or right-clicks it → **Place on Current Scene**) → a **map Note** linked to the terminal's journal is created at the drop point (icon = the item's art, `iconSize` 64, label = `terminalName`/item name, flags `terminalItemId`+`terminalItemUuid`). Players **double-click** the pin to log in: the core `activateNote` hook returns `false` (suppressing the journal sheet) and calls `game.nightcity.openTerminal`. Design constraints that must not regress:
- **Drop interception is a capture-phase `document` `drop` listener, NOT just a `dropCanvasData` hook.** Core binds the canvas drop as an `ondrop` **property** on `#board`, and every drop-handling module (Item Piles turns dropped items into loot piles) rides the `dropCanvasData` hook fired inside it — so a document-capture listener wins **deterministically regardless of module load order**. It claims the event ONLY for `isTerminal` items (`preventDefault` + `stopImmediatePropagation`); everything else — including data shards, which SHOULD become Item Piles loot — passes through untouched. A `dropCanvasData` hook returning `false` stays as belt-and-braces. Players dropping a terminal get a "GM only" warn.
- **Pins are only visible/clickable when the core client toggle `notesDisplayToggle` is on — and it defaults OFF.** World setting `terminalPinsAutoShow` (default on, in Module Settings) flips it at ready on every client. Note visibility also requires OBSERVER on the journal — terminal journals are created OBSERVER, so don't tighten that ownership.
- **Optional proximity gate:** `config.scenePin.range` (grid squares, 0 = off; field in the config **Network tab → Scene Access Point**) — a non-GM needs an owned, non-hidden token within range (+0.5-square grace so "adjacent" counts) or the click is denied with a warn + `error` sound. Distance is plain center-to-center pixels ÷ grid size. Opening from the item itself is deliberately ungated.
- **Cleanup:** `deleteItem` and the unconvert flag-wipe (`-=MODULE_ID` or `-=isTerminal` in `updateItem` changes) remove that terminal's pins across all scenes, gated to `game.users.activeGM` so multi-GM setups delete once. Stale pins (item gone) warn on click instead of opening. Multiple pins of one terminal are allowed.
- **Known compat caveat:** `Hooks.call('activateNote')` stops at the first `false`, so a journal-takeover module registered earlier (e.g. Monk's Enhanced Journal) could swallow pin clicks. No clean fix; revisit if a user reports it.
- Logic-simmed via the real class + mocked globals (scratchpad `test-scenepins.mjs`, 31/31 — drop claiming both paths, coord transform, range gate, cleanup).

**Test setup:** `dev/tools/test-terminal-setup.macro.js` — paste into a Script macro to build a fully-authored test terminal (4 accounts incl. hidden, priority-coded messages) and open it. **`dev/tools/test-upload-setup.macro.js`** is its UPLOAD/PLANT counterpart: it seeds the design's on-ramp — a go-bag shard ("Ghost's Go-Bag") of four **PLANT** forgeries at escalating forge DVs (chatlog/gallery/dossier/document) to carry to a terminal and push via OPERATOR TOOLS → UPLOAD. Two things it encodes that bite otherwise: the shard must be **actor-owned** (`_gatherCarriedPrograms` scans `actor.items` only — a world item is invisible to the picker, and the actor must match the HUD's OPERATOR chip), and it seeds **no EXECUTE** entry on purpose (`_gatherCarriedPrograms` sets `disabled: family === 'execute'` until thread #6, and `runUpload` only installs `plant` residents — a payload/malware entry would render as a dead SOON row).

**Planned work (backlog — not built yet; full detail in the `terminal-expansion` memory note's ▶ RESUME HERE):**
1. Rest of the per-type locked/encrypted states (roll-to-unlock slice 1 SHIPPED 2026-07-11: gallery frames / sealed avlog / document-dossier-spreadsheet DECLASSIFY + traceOnFail; schematic markers #33; calendar events #34; dossier masked-identity + encrypted combat profile #35 — remaining candidates: record rows, chatlog, per-entry variants).
2. ~~PAYLOAD type overhaul~~ — SHIPPED 2026-07-12 (utility-program identity, module-native effects, timed active/kill, disguise trojan, hybrid manifest→console→active card; see the `payload` type bullet above). Follow-up candidates: a player-facing "analyze any file" SNIFF affordance (today disguise reveal is GM-driven), effect appliers for more host types, sound-cue pass on execute.
3. Config-sheet polish pass (dev feedback pending) · sound-set rate/volume ear-tuning · thread-transcript typed-body cosmetic gap · spreadsheet column sort · player-relay uplink two-client verify. (Relayed-write lag FIXED 2026-07-12 — the viewer's journal-page hooks.)
   (Everything else from earlier backlog lists SHIPPED — §8c catalog 18/18, method-card auth dialog, sound sets, SecurityConsole + 6-action exfil, image lightbox, entry templates/examples, From↔Master-List both directions, sender add/reply chips, Admin Contacts per-role grouping + group-by selector, Admin Terminals split-panel manager.)
4. **UPLOAD / FORGE — the missing player→machine direction (NEXT BIG ARC, design first).** Every content action today flows terminal→player (siphon/exfil/claim/save-frame). Upload is the inverse — the netrunner "get in, leave something behind." Mock: `dev/mockups/eddies-and-upload-mock.html`. The insight: it's mostly **composition of what exists** (composer authors 18 types · payloads carry module-native effects · shards carry typed entries as a go-bag · SecurityConsole runs skill-gated actions w/ trace-on-fail). Two families = dev's two examples: **PLANT** (upload forged content — chatlog/gallery/dossier — that the next login finds; the blackmail case; +a FORGE check, botched forgery leaves tells) and **EXECUTE** (payload effect engine pointed at a host you don't own — wipe a log, jam uplink, forge a backdoor account, leave Black ICE). Dev's on-ramp ask: an **NPC hands the player a shard** (or a message) → carry it to a terminal → **upload through the Agent or the shard** → security gates the push. Recommended phasing: **P1 PLANT** (cheapest, delivers blackmail now) → **P2 EXECUTE** (malware gets authored effects + becomes uploadable; malware/payload converge) → **P3 a real creator UI**. **P1 hard requirement:** a GM audit view for "what's been planted where" (forged content must be trackable). Also **discuss the malware GAME MODEL holistically** before building — dev flagged it's worth its own conversation.
5. **TRACE needs teeth + a persistent GM log (dev: "if a player does something the trace needs more consequence… at least the GM needs to see it and have a log of it").** Current state is inconsistent and EPHEMERAL: `_fireUnlockTrace` (unlock/camera/malware-sniff paths) plays trace-fire + `showShardTrace` splash **and** whispers the GM a client-created ChatMessage — but **siphon and malware-deploy call `showShardTrace` bare** (no whisper, no record), and **nothing persists anywhere**. Design a real trace subsystem: (a) ONE `fireTrace({actor, source, action, dv?, host?})` every trace path routes through (kill the bare `showShardTrace` calls); (b) a **durable log** (world setting/flag — who traced what, where, when) surfaced in the Admin Panel (Messages or a new Security tab); (c) escalating **consequences** to discuss — GM alert (always), a per-terminal/network "heat" counter, lockout on repeat traces, an ICE response, a countdown timer (the `traceStarted/traceExpiresAt` fields already exist on messages but aren't wired to anything). The trace timer P3 hook in `DEFAULTS.TERMINAL_CONFIG` was scaffolded and never built — fold it in.

## Architecture: Upload / Forge / Trace / Operator Gate / Security tab (2026-07-14→15)

The player→machine arc (backlog #4/#5 above, now BUILT — design doc `dev/design/UPLOAD-FORGE-TRACE.md` is the source of truth). All new, all UNCOMMITTED, verified via scratchpad sims + faithful headless renders. **Not yet live-tested in Foundry** — the dev breaks here.

**Trace subsystem — `scripts/services/TraceService.js`.** ONE funnel, two entry points: **`fireTrace(o)`** (a FAILURE — plays trace-fire + `showShardTrace`, bumps heat, GM-alerts, logs) and **`logSuccess(o)`** (a SUCCESS — logs with `success:true`, **zero heat**, so the Activity Log is a *full* feed the GM can audit, "no heat gained" shown explicitly). Both build a `_payload(o)` carrying `actor/operator`, `action`, `severity`, `targetKind` (terminal/network/shard/message/contact/account), `targetUuid`/`targetMessageId`/`targetContactId` (deep-link anchors), `outcome`, `roll`. `recordTrace` appends to the `traceLog` world setting (cap `LOG_CAP=250`) and applies `heatDelta` (`HEAT_BY_SEVERITY` minor:10/major:20/hostile:35; success=0). Heat lives in the `traceHeat` world setting keyed by `operator::objectId`; `getHeat/bumpHeat/setHeat`, decay `HEAT_DECAY_PER_HR=5` (poll-based, through the time provider), tiers `TIERS` 25/50/80 (`tierOf` static → cold/warm/hot/burned). `_heatChat` posts a GM-reason card on manual adjust; `_gmAlert` respects the `securityAlertLevel` client setting. Countdowns: `startCountdown/cancelCountdown/reapCountdowns` (the "corp found you" escalation timer). **GM-owned world writes → players relay via `SOCKET_OPS.TRACE_REQUEST`.** Every prior bare `showShardTrace` call site (31 of them) was routed through `fireTrace`.

**Upload / PLANT — `scripts/ui/content/UploadFlow.js` + `UploadConsole.js` (+ `styles/components/upload-console.css`).** The inverse of exfil: carry a forged typed-entry (from a shard "go-bag" or composed) to a terminal and leave it behind. `runUpload` opens the FUI picker (OperatorSelect pattern — framework-free promise modal appended into `host`), runs a **FORGE check**, and installs the content as a resident (below). `forgeQualityFromMargin(margin)` → a 0–100% quality; `stealthDVFromQuality` → the scan DV a later ANALYZE must beat (a botched forgery = low quality = easy to detect = "leaves tells").

**THE FORGE CHECK IS A LADDER, NOT PASS/FAIL** (`forgeOutcome(total, dv)`, `FORGE_SLOPPY_BAND = 4`): `margin >= 0` → **clean** · `-1..-4` → **sloppy** (it lands, but ANALYZE will flag it FORGED) · **worse than -4 → rejected, NOTHING installs**. The original model installed on any committed roll with no floor, so failing by 12 planted the same file as failing by 1. `runSecurityConsole` gained an optional **`verdictFor({ok,total,dv}) → {label, tone}`** hook for exactly this (a binary ok/fail can't express three rungs) plus an **`is-warn`** amber stage tone (a sloppy forge is a compromised success, so no shake). The console's intro spells the ladder out before the player commits. **Don't bother hiding the DV** — the shared `templates/chat/hack-result.hbs` prints "vs DV {dc}" for every check in the module, so hiding it in one console is cosmetic-only; it would need a `hideDV` flag threaded through `SkillService.performCheck` + that card (which every hack shares).

**Re-uploading REPLACES, it does not stack (`residentSlotKey` + `installResident`).** A resident carries a `slotKey` (`effectType::accountId::name`); installing one **replaces an existing ARMED resident in the same slot**, and only an armed one (a *fired* plant already landed, so a re-upload is genuinely a new file; slotless/legacy residents never dedupe). Without this, a player who missed the forge and retried left N armed duplicates and the GM's next login prompt fired **every one of them** — the reported "it uploads all the failed tries as well". Sim: scratchpad `test-forge-bands.mjs` (21/21, drives the real `installResident` via `prototype.call`). The COMPILING beat streams procedural code via **`HackFX.streamHackCode(el,{durationMs,subject,family,reduced,onDone})`** — family-aware pools (plant vs execute, with warning lines), reduced-motion-gated. **Composer** gained an uploadable fieldset (author the payload up front).

**Residents (triggered effects) — in `TerminalService`.** A planted file can be a **resident**: `getResidents/installResident/updateResident/removeResident`, lifecycle `_checkLoginResidents` (fires on next login) / `_proposeResidentFire` / `fireResident` / `reapResidents` / `checkActionResidents`. Stored in `TERMINAL_STATE.residents`. `applyMalwareEffect` is the shared effect applier. This is the "fires on next login" armed-plant mechanic surfaced in the Security tab's Planted & Resident card. **Only `plant` is implemented** in `fireResident`; `keylog`/`forgeAccount`/`logicBomb`/`deployICE` fall through to "GM to adjudicate" until thread #6.

**A trigger match PROPOSES — and the world setting `residentFireMode` decides.** `propose` (default) → the GM gets the prompt · `auto` → fires on trigger, GM is just told · `manual` → never prompts, waits for Admin → Security's Fire. **The pause exists because a plant firing may be the moment the table runs the NET** (design §5) — but that intent was completely invisible, so the prompt now says it in as many words. The prompt is **`scripts/ui/content/ResidentConsole.js` + `styles/components/resident-console.css`** (`runResidentConsole` → `'fire'|'defuse'|'later'`): the FUI family modal (framework-free promise, appended into `TerminalService._openViewerElement(item)` so it inherits *that machine's* preset; body-fallback), replacing a raw `Dialog` with inline styles. It shows what fired, who planted it, the target mailbox, the **forgery tell** (clean/passable/sloppy from `forgeQuality`), names the **closest NET action** for the effect (`NET_VERB`), and points at the setting for tables that don't play it that way. ESC/backdrop = "later" (never destructive). **Its markup is a template literal — keep backticks out of it** (one inside an HTML comment terminated the string and the modal silently never rendered). Preview: `dev/mockups/resident-console-preview.html` (imports the REAL module against the real cascade).

**Operator capability gate — `canOperate(actor, override)` (helpers.js) + `DEFAULTS.OPERATOR_GATE` (constants.js).** Restricts WHO can operate a terminal/run content actions: `mode` (everyone / only-who-qualifies), `roleLogic`, `roles[]`, `skill`+min-rank, **`itemGroups`** (grouped **OR-of-ANDs** — each group is an AND of required items, groups OR together; items are `{name,img}` so the gate UI shows item images), `actors[]`, `allowGM`. `nameOf` handles both string and `{name,img}`. Gate config is the `operatorGate` world setting, edited in the Security tab.

**[Superseded 2026-10-27: the tab is now five sub-tabs — see "countermeasure visibility + Admin → Security". The capabilities below all still exist.]** **Admin → Security tab (tab key `7`) — `scripts/ui/AdminPanel/tabs/SecurityTab.js` + `templates/admin-panel/tab-security.hbs` + `styles/components/admin-panel/tab-security.css`.** Composed BaseTab (static delegators on `AdminPanelApp`, ~20 actions `security*`). Five cards: **summary strip** (events 24h / running hot / planted / recycle bin), **Activity Log** (the trace feed — filter chips All/Serious/Fumbles, alert-level select, search, pagination, "N new since last check" via the `securityLogSeen` client setting; color-coded target icons per `targetKind`; each row's ↗ **deep-links to the exact object** — terminal/shard/network via `game.nightcity` launchers, message via journal-page scan, contact via `GMContactManagerApp`), **Heat** (per operator·object bars with tier legend, GM **Adjust** [reason→chat], **Clear**/**Clear all**, **Escalate** [countdown + ICE `Dialog` → `ICEService.applyDamage`] gated on value≥50, **Defuse**), **Planted & Resident** (armed plants, Fire now / Defuse / Remove), **Recycle Bin** (GM Restore / Purge / Empty bin for wiped files), **Operator Access** (the gate editor — skill dropdown from `SKILL_MAP`, role chips, grouped gear chip-groups with item images, `+ add an OR option`). **The gear/role choosers are the shared name picker (`showNamePicker` from `onGateAddItem`/`onGateAddRole`), not `<select>`s** — the burn-picker/key-item precedent (search box + item art + click-to-pick; already-chipped rows render `.is-taken`, greyed and click-refused). A native dropdown had no search and no art, and it forced `_gateContext` to scan **every actor's items on every render** to fill the options; the pickers now read `_availableItems()`/`_availableRoles()` **on demand**, so the context no longer carries choice lists at all. Settings registered in `SettingsManager`: `operatorGate`, `recycleBin`, `traceLog`, `traceHeat`, `traceCountdowns`, `securityAlertLevel`(client), `securityLogSeen`(client). New `SOCKET_OPS`: `TRACE_REQUEST`, `TERMINAL_RESIDENT_REQUEST`. **CSS note:** the tab's prefix is **`ncm-sec-admin-*`, never `ncm-sec-*`** — `ncm-sec-*` belongs to security-overlays.css and colliding with it was the "cards squished to half width, everything centered" bug (see the class-collision gotcha; the `-admin-` infix mirrors `ncm-term-admin-*`). It uses ONLY `--ncm-*` admin tokens (follows all three presets), keeps `box-shadow/text-shadow:none !important` to kill CPR's neon glow, and needs `table { margin: 0 }` (core's `table{margin:1rem}` clipped the last column). Verify with `dev/mockups/admin-security-preview.html` — it renders the **REAL `.hbs`** through Handlebars against real foundry2.css + CPR main.css + admin-theme.css at the true 820px with the scrollbar gutter forced (`?t=clean-light|high-contrast` switches preset, `?diag=1` dumps overflow/wrap/text-align/inheritance measurements, `?empty=1` renders empty states). **Don't hand-copy the markup into the preview** — the original mock did, drifted from the shipped template, and hid all of the above. (Preview note: it `fetch`es the real `.hbs`, so render it with `--allow-file-access-from-files` or the fetch fails; `?h=<px>` grows the frame to reveal lower cards.) **Update round (2026-08-14):** every growable list (log/heat/plants/bin) now caps height + scrolls (`.ncm-sec-admin-scroll`) AND paginates (`_paginate` helper; `_heatPage`/`_plantPage`/`_binPage`; actions `security{Heat,Plant,Bin}Page`). A **Programs card** up top opens the Malware Workshop — Create (`openMalwareWorkshop()`) / Program library (`openMalwareWorkshop({openLibrary:true})`). **Operator Access was redesigned** to radio-CARDS for Anyone/gated (so `gateMode` is a radio group — read `[name=gateMode]:checked`, NOT the first match, in `onSaveGate` + the `.is-ungated` dimming listener), boxed ROLE/SKILL/GEAR blocks that dim when ungated, and **OR-groups with Option A/B headers (`letter` Handlebars helper) + a ✕ remove button** (`securityGateRemoveGroup`; `_renumberGateGroups` re-letters; `_gateGroupInner` keeps the JS-added group in sync with the hbs). Action count is now ~26.

**Shared FUI modals** built alongside: `OperatorSelect.js` (operator picker — see operator-identity section) and the pre-existing `SecurityConsole.js`/`SecurityAuthAdapters.js` seam are the pattern every new modal follows (framework-free promise, appended into `host`, inherits `--ncm-*`).

**Netrunning resolution modes (Thread 7, 2026-07-20) — `hackResolution` A/B/C + the `netAction` tag (design §5).** NCM never pretends to BE netrunning; the table picks HOW gated hack/upload actions resolve. World setting **`hackResolution`** (`auto` default | `flagged` | `gm`), edited in Admin → Security's **Netrunning Resolution** card (auto-saves on change, `[data-sec-hackres]`), with a **per-terminal override** (`terminalConfig.hackResolution`, `'inherit'` default — terminal config → Network tab); precedence via **`resolveHackResolution(item?)`** (helpers.js). The seam is deliberately ONE place: `runSecurityConsole.go()` (plus `BankHeist` phase 2, which owns the siphon roll) — a caller opts in by passing **`gate.net = { verb, action, hostItem?, hostName?, choices? }`**; no `net` = plain Tier A forever (auth/login checks deliberately don't participate — access ≠ action). All the gated content flows are tagged (siphon/crack/unlock/camera/deploy/execute/analyze/exfil/upload/claim).
- **Tier B (`flagged`)** — same auto-roll, then `postNetActionCard` (NetResolve.js) whispers the GM a `.ncm-netcard` naming the NET-action shape + roll + PASS/FAIL, so the table can fold it into a live run.
- **Tier C (`gm`)** — **no roll**: the console parks in an AWAITING GM stage (`is-await`, cancel stays live and **withdraws**) while `requestNetAdjudication` runs a socket round-trip (`SOCKET_OPS.NET_RESOLVE_REQUEST/RESULT/CANCEL`, the exfil userId-filter pattern; GM-as-operator adjudicates locally since emits don't echo; **no GM online → blocked with a warning**; multi-GM → only `game.users.activeGM` prompts). The GM's **NetAdjudicator** (`scripts/ui/content/NetResolve.js` + `styles/components/net-adjudicator.css`, FUI family, prefix `ncm-netadj-*`) shows who/what/verb/DV + the "run the NET at the table, then call it" copy; **DISMISS = withdrawn, not failed** (no trace, retryable; ESC dismisses but a backdrop click deliberately does NOT). The verdict **synthesizes `{ok, total}` so callers are unchanged** — the upload passes ladder `choices` (CLEAN dv+4 / SLOPPY dv−2 / REJECTED dv−BAND−2) whose in-band totals feed `forgeOutcome`/`forgeQualityFromMargin` correctly; synthesized totals are **never displayed as rolls** and never ride the wire.
- **The `netAction` verb is derived centrally**: `NET_ACTION_VERBS` (constants.js) maps the `action` strings already passed to `fireTrace`/`logSuccess` → Backdoor/Control/Virus/Eye-Dee/…; `TraceService._payload` stamps `netVerb` (explicit `o.netVerb` wins, unknown action = none) and the Activity Log renders it as a mono `.nverb` chip. Verified: `test-netresolve.mjs` 42/42 (precedence, protocol, withdraw/late-result/wrong-user, ladder band math) + headless renders of the adjudicator (both verdict sets), the awaiting stage, the Tier B card, and the tab card via `dev/mockups/net-adjudicator-preview.html` (`?ladder=1`, `?view=await|card`, `?snap=1` forces the entrance state for capture — **and the preview MUST mock `Handlebars`**: a missing global makes the modal's Promise-executor throw get swallowed as a silent rejection, i.e. "mounted but never appears").
- **Both sides of the Tier C wait DOCK, and the two are SYNCED (2026-07-22).** The point of `gm` mode is "run the NET at the table first", so neither screen can be held hostage. The GM prompt has a **RUN THE NET** button and the player console/heist a **STEP AWAY** button; both hide the overlay (`is-min`/`is-docked`, click-through) and drop a chip into the shared fixed corner dock **`#ncm-netadj-dock`** (`netadjDock()` exported from NetResolve.js). **When the GM minimizes, the player's console auto-docks in sync** — the dev's ask, "the player shouldn't have to guess the run started": the GM's minimize/restore emits **`SOCKET_OPS.NET_RESOLVE_STATE {requestId, userId, minimized}`** (only for a relayed request — a GM adjudicating their own action has no remote player), `handleNetResolveState` routes it to the `onRemoteState` callback the console passed into `requestNetAdjudication`, and the console docks to a distinct **green "NETRUN IN PROGRESS"** chip (`.ncm-netadj-chip--run`, animated scan bar) vs the cyan self-dock "AWAITING GM" chip. The verdict's arrival auto-undocks; ESC is suppressed while docked. Both SecurityConsole and BankHeist wire it identically (`dock(remote)`/`undock()`). Sim `test-netstate-sync.mjs` 9/9 (real NetResolve: request→state→onRemoteState, wrong-user/unknown/late-STATE dropped, self-adjudication emits no STATE).

**Escalation consequences (2026-07-20) — heat tiers now have teeth (design §6b).** The Admin → Security **Escalate** dialog (GM-gated, one dialog, three options; shown from **warm heat ≥25**, was ≥50) offers **A · Lock out** / **B · Black ICE** / **C · countdown**. The lockout tier's missing map (heat is keyed by bare `objectId`, SecurityService lockouts are keyed granularly) is resolved by **`SecurityTab._lockoutScope(objectId)`** — a terminal fans out to every account key (`termId::acctId`, incl. hidden), a shard → the shard id, a network → the network id (the Agent-connect gate; a terminal's own compound net-join key is deliberately not covered), a `label:` heat → nothing lockable. The primitive is **`SecurityService.forceLockout(actorId, targetId, {durationMs, reason})`** — imposes a lockout immediately without burning attempts (sets attempts→max so login screens read 0 tries left, emits `NETWORK_LOCKOUT{forced:true}`, expires on game time like every other lockout). GM-forced, so it bypasses the account's own `maxAttempts` config. Sim `test-forcelockout.mjs` 18/18. **Still deferred:** the per-terminal **`autoEscalate`** hands-off toggle (all escalation is a manual GM click today) and folding the scaffolded `traceStarted`/`traceExpiresAt` message fields into the countdown.

**Escalation live-test round (2026-07-22) — five fixes off the dev's first Tier-C/escalation playtest:**
- **Tier C must not lock the table out of Foundry — both sides of the wait now DOCK.** The whole point of `gm` mode is "run the NET on the tabletop first", but the adjudicator was a full-screen modal, so the GM couldn't switch scenes/run the netrun before calling it. The GM prompt has a **RUN THE NET** top-bar button and the player-side waits (SecurityConsole `is-await` + BankHeist's siphon wait) a **STEP AWAY** button — all three hide the overlay click-through (`is-min`/`is-docked`) and drop a chip into the shared fixed corner dock **`#ncm-netadj-dock`** (`netadjDock()` exported from NetResolve.js; chip = `.ncm-netadj-chip`, cyan `--wait` variant for the player side). Clicking the chip restores; the verdict's arrival auto-undocks the player console so the outcome animation is never missed; **ESC is suppressed while docked** (a stray keypress must not silently withdraw a run in progress) and the promise flow is untouched — withdraw/dismiss still clean up the chip.
- **Forced lockout must SYNC — SecurityService state is CLIENT-LOCAL.** The escalation `forceLockout` only wrote the GM's in-memory Map, so the locked player just logged back in, and the per-account event fan-out spammed the GM with N "Unknown — ? min" toasts. Now **`forceLockoutMany(actorId, targets, {durationMs, reason, label})`** is the escalation entry: applies locally, emits ONE forced `NETWORK_LOCKOUT`, broadcasts **`SOCKET_OPS.SECURITY_LOCKOUT`** → every client mirrors via `receiveForcedLockout` (state + timers, no re-broadcast), **kicks live sessions** (`_kickLockedViewers` scans ui.windows + foundry.applications.instances; a terminal viewer gets the new **`TerminalViewerApp.forceLogout(msg)`** → bounced to login with the error line, a shard viewer closes), and the owning non-GM player gets ONE labeled toast + `lockout` sound. `NotificationService`'s `NETWORK_LOCKOUT` listener **skips `forced` events** entirely, and `showNetworkLockout` now resolves the target label for ALL key shapes via `_lockTargetLabel` (network id / shard item / `termId::acctId` → "Item · Account") + minutes from `duration` — the "lock unknown … ? min" fix for earned lockouts too. Sim: scratchpad `test-lockout-sync.mjs` (23/23, real SecurityService, two simulated clients).
- **Countdowns are visible and have TEETH.** The reaper was registered but a started countdown was invisible ("I did countdown and nothing happened" — also note it runs on **game time**, so manual/world-time clocks stall it). The Security tab's Heat card now lists **active countdowns** (`_gatherCountdowns` — remaining time, a clock icon + tooltip when a non-real-time provider gates it, **Call off** button → `securityCountdownCancel`), and `reapCountdowns` expiry additionally writes a durable **`trace-complete`** Activity Log row at `hostile` severity (+35 heat → typically BURNED, which also fires the GM alert toast) on top of the existing whispers + sound.
- **Black ICE toast reaches the player.** `ICEService.applyDamage`'s toast rode the client-local EventBus, so a GM escalation strike showed only on the GM's screen — and the listener resolved the name from a nonexistent `data.itemId` ("'Unknown' fights back"). The listener now prefers `iceInfo.name`/`targetName`, and applyDamage relays **`SOCKET_OPS.SECURITY_NOTIFY`** `{userIds, kind:'blackIce', data}` to the actor's active non-GM owners (handler shows the toast + `black-ice` sound). SECURITY_NOTIFY is a generic targeted-toast op — reuse it for future cross-client security pings.
- **…but a TICKING clock is throttled (2026-10-29, dev: "it keeps redoing the open animation of the nav, sliding in every second"):** `AdminPanelApp._onGameClock(force)` re-renders only a tab in `CLOCK_TABS` (overview, messages, security, terminals, shards) and only once the game clock moved `CLOCK_RERENDER_MS` (60 s) since the last render — a real-time Simple Calendar / world-time ticker fires every second, and a full re-render every second replayed the entrance animations. A deliberate change (Set time, a provider switch = the time settings) passes `force`. Every render stamps `_clockRenderedAt`. Networks / Contacts / Access / Tools never re-render for the clock.
- **The panel follows the GAME CLOCK (2026-10-28, dev: "is anything that uses live time live?").** `AdminPanelApp._setupEventSubscriptions` re-renders the open tab (debounced) on `updateWorldTime`, on Simple Calendar's `simple-calendar-date-time-change`, and on the `updateSetting` hook for `manualTime` / `disguisedBaseTime` / `disguisedAnchor` / `timeProvider` — so Set time, a manual advance, a provider switch and a calendar change repaint every age, countdown and clock at once (hooks removed in `close()`). The Overview's NOW label + dock clock card tick every SECOND off `timeService` (`_tickClocks`), the line geometry every 15 s. A new tab that prints game time needs nothing extra. Sim `test-game-clock.mjs`.
- **The Security tab live-updates.** The log/heat/countdowns/bin are WORLD SETTINGS, so `AdminPanelApp._setupEventSubscriptions` registers an **`updateSetting` hook** (filtered to `traceLog`/`traceHeat`/`traceCountdowns`/`recycleBin`, debounced, security-tab-only; `Hooks.off` in `close()`) — it fires on every client including the writer. A 30s `_liveTick` in `SecurityTab.onRender` (cleared in `onClose`) repaints heat **decay** and countdown remaining-times, which change without any setting write. The panel was already `minimizable: true` — double-click the title bar.

**Second escalation-notes round (2026-07-22b) — legibility + copy + fire default:**
- **Fire default flipped to AUTO.** `residentFireMode` default is now `'auto'` (fires + tells the GM) instead of `'propose'`; `TerminalService._proposeResidentFire`'s local fallback matches. The prompt/pause is now opt-in.
- **Trace countdowns aren't always "the corp".** `startCountdown` gained a free-text **`source`** ("who's closing in" — Arasaka / a fixer / the Maelstrom, default "A trace") and **`playerWarning`** (custom player cue); the Escalate → countdown dialog surfaces both (Who / Warn fields), the Security-tab countdown list shows the source (`.cdsrc`), and all countdown whispers name it. The old hardcoded "the corp found you" is gone.
- **Player-facing trace warnings are legible cards now.** New shared helper **`whisperCardHtml({tone, icon, title, lines})`** (helpers.js) + **`.ncm-wcard`** (styles/chat/chat-cards.css §9, hard fallbacks — chat log has no `--ncm-*` ancestor; tones info/warn/danger/ok/purple). Every bare `<b>NCM trace:</b> …` whisper (heat adjust, trace start/complete, lockout, ICE) — in TraceService AND SecurityTab — routes through it at ~13.5px body vs Foundry's default. Reuse it for any future NCM security whisper.
- **Toast X + countdown made visible.** `.ncm-toast__dismiss` was a 7px near-black glyph hidden until hover → now 11px, always at 0.65 opacity. `.ncm-toast__progress` was a 2px/0.35-opacity bar → 3px, 0.85 opacity + glow over a dark track. (Applies to every toast, not just security.)
- **ICE retaliation card — legibility + context.** Chat-card `.ncm-cc__result-badge` (SUCCESS/FAILED) bumped 8→11px and `.ncm-cc__roll-label` 7→9px (the "success/fail can be more legible" + "roll labels" notes). The ICE card (`.ncm-ice-card__*`) got a size pass (badge 9→11, name 13→15, portrait 56→68px + a red **scan-sweep** over the pulled-in image, damage number 30→36 with a pulse, formula/HP/footer up ~1–2px). **Its title is context-aware**: `showShardBlackICE` reads `data.context` → "BLACK ICE — Shard/Network/Message/Terminal" (was hardcoded "— Shard"; a trace-escalation strike said the wrong thing). `context` now threads BLACK_ICE_DAMAGE event → NotificationService AND the SECURITY_NOTIFY relay. `.ncm-netcard` (Tier B whisper) fonts bumped too. Verified: headless `legibility-preview.html` render (result badge, roll grid, ICE-with-Network-context, four whisper cards — all legible).

**Third escalation-notes round (2026-07-22c) — lockout hardening + visibility + dialog polish:**
- **THE lockout re-login bug (critical): `requiresAuth` let a locked-out player straight back in TWO ways** — an OPEN account (no auth) and a session that still "remembered" the login both returned `requiresAuth=false` and dropped into `_loginSuccess` with NO lockout check. Fixed with **`TerminalViewerApp._lockGuard(accountId)`** — the authoritative gate: it reads `TerminalService.getAccountLockoutRemaining` (new, → `SecurityService.getLockoutRemaining`) and runs FIRST on every login entry point (`_onSelectAccount`/`_onFocusAccount`/`_onInlineAuth`/`_onSwitchAccount`) AND as the final gate inside `_loginSuccess` (so no path — open account, remembered session, keypad — reaches the mailbox past an active lockout). `_cardContext` now exposes `lockedOut`; the login card renders a red **LOCKED OUT** badge (`.is-lockedout`) and clicking it re-shows the takeover.
- **SYSTEM LOCKDOWN takeover (the "animation of what's happening").** `_syncLockdown()` (called in `_onRender`, GM-excluded) injects a full-viewer red alert overlay (`.ncm-term__lockdown` in terminal.css — hazard stripes, sweeping scan bar, pulsing lock, a **live MM:SS countdown** on a 1s interval, STAND DOWN dismiss) whenever `_lockdownAccountId` is set and still locked. `forceLogout` sets it (keyed on the kicked account); when the clock hits 0 it clears + re-renders so the cards go clickable. Timer cleared in `close()`. Reduced-motion drops the animations.
- **Player-facing countdown HUD (the "I don't see anything on the player side" fix).** New **`scripts/ui/content/TraceCountdownHud.js`** (registered ready/43 on EVERY client next to TraceService; `game.nightcity.traceCountdownHud`) — a body-mounted top-right panel (`#ncm-trace-hud` in net-adjudicator.css) that shows every active `traceCountdowns` entry targeting an actor THIS user owns: WHO is closing in, on WHAT, a live MM:SS ticking on the **game clock** (same basis as the GM reaper, so they never disagree). Rebuilds on the `traceCountdowns` `updateSetting` hook, entrance-flashes new ones, self-clears when empty. Reads the world-scope setting directly — no socket. A GM normally sees nothing (they don't own the targeted PC).
- **Escalate dialog stylized.** Was a raw grey Foundry `Dialog` of inline-styled rows; now three labeled **option cards** (A/B/C tag badges, WARM+/COUNTDOWN chips, per-option accent) + a red danger lede + a reason/warn-player footer, with FUI-vocabulary buttons (lockout amber, countdown/ICE red). Passed `{classes:['ncm-esc-dialog'], width:440}`; styles are **top-level** in tab-security.css (a Dialog is body-mounted, NOT inside `.ncm-admin-panel`, so they can't use the `-admin-` scope) with hard fallbacks. Verified headless (`escalate-preview.html`, `lockdown-preview.html`).

**Still deferred (next session):** Tier B/C **program-item hooks** (equipped Sword/Worm modifying DVs — §5's "later hook"), `autoEscalate` toggle, plus the standing polish list.

**Thread 6 — EXECUTE malware verbs + SYSTEM SWEEP + malware-uploadable (2026-07-24→25, BUILT, sim/headless-verified, NOT live-tested).** The offensive half of the player→machine arc. Decisions locked in `dev/design/THREAD6-MALWARE-MODEL.md` (GM-authored economy w/ store/Item-Piles selling; 24h backdoors; deployICE both-sources; timed-single logicBomb; worm out). Design: `dev/design/UPLOAD-FORGE-TRACE.md` §4e. **The five stubbed resident verbs are now native**, grown into the TWO existing appliers (no third added):
- **Triggered verbs** live in `TerminalService.fireResident`, which was split into a reusable **`_fireResidentEffect(item, effectType, params, ctx)`** switch (so `logicBomb` can run an INNER effect without re-marking the outer resident). **`keylog`** captures the login that fires it → whispers the account (a secret-login lead) to the runner+GM via `_deliverToRunner`. **`forgeAccount`** → **`forgeBackdoorAccount`** (shared with the instant path): writes a HIDDEN, password-protected account with a `forged` provenance block + a 24h game-time expiry (`reapForgedAccounts` on the resident-reaper cadence closes it, `ttlHours:0`=permanent); creds whisper to the runner. **`deployICE`** bites the intruder whose login fired it — runner-brought `params.iceActorId` wins, else `_resolveDeployIce` reads the box's own account `security.ice`, else honest degrade. **`logicBomb`** runs ONE inner effect through the same switch; an inner `logicBomb` is refused (the recursion fence).
- **Instant verbs** live in SiphonFlow's **`_applyNativeEffect`** (now EXPORTED for sims, where `wipe` lives): **`corrupt`** redacts document/dossier paragraphs/sections IN PLACE + arms a DECLASSIFY (DV) to restore — the reversible sabotage; non-redactable types are honestly skipped (`_corruptContentData`). **`forgeAccount`** (instant variant) delegates to `forgeBackdoorAccount`.
- **SYSTEM SWEEP** (`scripts/ui/content/SystemSweep.js` + `styles/components/system-sweep.css`) — the counterplay: a cyan FUI-family promise modal (the defensive sibling of UploadConsole; radar-sweep beat) wired into `TerminalViewerApp`'s OPERATOR-TOOLS dropdown (`terminalSystemSweep`→`_onSystemSweep`). ONE sweep roll (`performCheck`, Interface, card DV 15) → reveals every ARMED resident whose `stealthDV ≤ total` (GM-without-actor force-reveals); the verdict **never says how many you missed**. **Information split:** GM sees type+`provenance`+`stealthDV`+forgery-tell (forgeQuality<45=SLOPPY else WELL-MADE); a player sees only "UNKNOWN PROCESS"+a vague per-type hint. Per-resident **DEFUSE** → `updateResident(state:'defused')` (GM direct / player relays via **`TERMINAL_RESIDENT_REQUEST {op:'defuse', residentId}`** — `handleResidentRequest` branches on `data.op`). No trace/heat (recon). Mock `dev/mockups/system-sweep-mock.html` (`?view=player|gm`, `?freeze=scan`); real CSS verified through the full import chain (cyan from content-types.css `[data-ct-color]`).
- **Malware-uploadable authoring** — the composer's uploadable fieldset (`data-shard-composer.hbs` + `DataShardComposer` context/collect) gains a **"When pushed, it…" verb select** (`uploadEffect`: keylog/backdoor/deployICE) that renders ONLY on malware/payload (context `isExecuteProgram`); labels flip Forge→Run. `TerminalViewerApp._gatherCarriedPrograms` branches: malware/payload+`uploadEffect` → an EXECUTE program (`effectType`=verb, `family:'execute'`, `params.iceActorId` for deployICE from the malware form's linked ICE) — the old `disabled:'execute'` SOON gate is GONE; anything else uploads as a PLANT (incl. a verb-less malware file). `runUpload` reuses the SAME three-band forge LADDER + generic installer — only console/notify COPY is family-aware (`isExecute`: FORGE→RUN, "install/glitchy/rejected"). So an uploaded EXECUTE program installs a triggered resident (fires onLogin), caught by SWEEP.
- **Deliberately NOT built** (deferred, not forgotten): `runProgram` unification (a pure refactor of two *working* flows — `runPayloadExecute`/`runMalwareDeploy` — that the upload path never routes through; no user-facing benefit, regression risk); composer authoring of `logicBomb`'s inner effect; `corrupt`/`wipe` as *uploadable* verbs (they need push-time target-file selection).
- **Test macro:** `dev/tools/test-thread6-setup.macro.js` seeds a Black-ICE actor + an operator-owned go-bag shard (3 EXECUTE uploadables: keylog/backdoor/deployICE + 1 PLANT for contrast) + **pre-armed residents at four stealth DVs** on the first terminal, so SYSTEM SWEEP has targets on the first run. (The `plant`-only `dev/tools/test-upload-setup.macro.js` predates this — its "EXECUTE is SOON" note is now stale.)

## Architecture: the Shard Encoder (player-facing authoring) + the legibility layer

The player's answer to the GM's Malware Workshop: a blank chip → something on it → one roll. Design is signed off (the seven decisions are in the `shard-encoder-design` memory note); the approved mock is `dev/mockups/shard-encoder-fui-mock.html`. **Built 2026-09-21: BLANK → BUILD (content **or** program) → LOCK → ENCODE, plus the GM card, the accessibility layer and the Market.** Nothing of the flow is left stubbed; what remains is retiring the old wizard and a live test.

**`STAGES` is the ONE list** the spine, the footer count and the navigation read, and its **middle row is a BRANCH**: the same slot is CONTENT (forge a file) or PROGRAM (build a tool), chosen by `S.mode`, which is why its `title`/`sub` are `{content, program}` maps read through `stageText()`. A new stage slots in by adding a row plus a `_rail<Stage>()` / `_patch<Stage>()` pair. Entry defaults to CONTENT; the **Payload / Malware tiles switch the branch** (the mock's "② CONTENT / ② PROGRAM"), and the Program Deck's door asks for `program` by name.

**The rules are a pure module — `scripts/ui/content/EncoderModel.js` — and it is the ONLY place a number lives.** No Foundry globals, so `dev/tools/sims/test-encoder-model.mjs` (49 checks) drives the real functions. `ENCODER_DEFAULTS` is the shape; the world setting **`encoderConfig` stores ONLY what the GM changed** and `resolveEncoderConfig(stored)` layers it over the defaults (objects merge, **arrays replace**, so a deleted tier stays deleted) — which is why a default added in a later version reaches existing worlds **with no migration**. It also sanitises: an unknown botch rule / perk / pricing mode falls back, a negative price clamps, a nameless item entry is dropped, and there is **always exactly one fallback tier**.

- **The ladder is the upload arc's, not a second one.** `SLOPPY_BAND` mirrors `FORGE_SLOPPY_BAND` in `UploadFlow.js` and **both the sim and Phase 6 assert they match** — meet the DV = clean · miss by up to the band = sloppy (it writes, with a tell) · worse = rejected (nothing compiles). `encodeOutcome` / `ladderOdds` / `likelyRung` / `oddsClass`. Quality uses the same `50 + margin × 8` scale, so a player-made file reads to ANALYZE/SWEEP exactly like an uploaded forgery.
- **A chip can carry SEVERAL files, and a crowded chip is a harder chip.** `S.entries[]` holds them; `contentStackDV()` prices the lot: the **hardest** file sets the base (you are only as good as the fake that takes the most work), the craft-skill penalty is paid **once**, and every file past the first adds `cfg.content.extraEntryDV` (default 2, 0 turns it off). One encode roll covers the chip, so **one quality lands on every file**. How many fit at all is the tier's `capacity`. Corollaries the code enforces: `_needsRoll()` reads EVERY file (a note beside a forged dossier does not make it easier), `_eddiesLoad()` sums every cred drop on the chip, `_choices()` offers the trades for the HARDEST type, and the GM relay accepts a LIST — a player sending three files must not lose two.
- **ADDING a file and EDITING one are different doors, and the add door needs BOTH halves.** "Write another file" only sets the intent (`S.adding`) and opens the type row; **picking the type is what opens the editor** (`case 'type'` consumes the flag). The first cut stopped at the rail, where the button re-read as "Write another file" again — so the editor was unreachable for a second file and the only way in was the per-file pencil, which EDITS: every "new" file replaced the last one. Any unrelated click clears `adding`, so a later type pick can never surprise-open the editor. The slot a composer writes back to is **captured when it opens**, never read from the live `editIndex` at save time (a second composer would otherwise land its file on top of another one), and a second composer is refused outright — `PlayerEntryComposer` owns ONE window id, so two would fight over the same slot.
- **The startup wears the chip the player designed.** `_bootConfig` sets `iconMode:'image'` + `imageUrl` from the chosen art (a baked data URI works — the boot renders a plain `<img>`), falling back to a glyph only when there is no art. Without it the splash animated around an empty space.

- **A tier says how much the chip HOLDS.** `capacity` (entries; **0 = no limit**) is the fourth thing a tier buys, alongside price, botch rule and perk — defaults Scrap 1 / Standard 3 / Premium 8, so a better blank is worth buying for more than surviving a botch. `capacityOf()` / `capacityLeft()` are the readers. The encode stamps the tier's limit onto the shard as `config.capacity`, and **`DataShardService.addMessage` enforces it for every writer** — the Encoder, the GM composer, the Workshop, and a terminal burn — returning `{success:false, full:true}` with a sentence rather than failing silently. **An absent capacity resolves to 0/unlimited**, so every shard authored before this behaves exactly as it did. A burn onto a full chip now carries the reason out of `exfilToShard` (it counted successes and returned a bare `success:false`, which read as a broken button).

- **Blank tiers are GM-defined.** `tierForItem(cfg, {name, tierId})` takes a PLAIN DESCRIPTOR, never a Foundry Item (the model stays pure — the app's `_desc(item)` builds it). An explicit `tierId` stamped on a bought blank wins; else the GM's per-tier item lists by **space/punctuation-insensitive name** (the SkillService rule); else the fallback tier. `botchFor(tier, {nat1})` says what a rejection costs — a natural 1 is one step harsher. A tier perk (`craft+1`) is a **real modifier**: `SkillService.performCheck` gained an `options.modifier` that lands in the total and the result.
- **Program branch:** `FAMILY_ABILITIES` maps utility/execute → `PROGRAM_EFFECTS` keys. **Only verbs with a working applier are offered** — `wipe`/`corrupt` need target files picked at PUSH time and `logicBomb` has no inner-effect authoring, so all three are absent (the standing "a dead option is worse than no option" rule; the sim asserts the negatives). Rank in the gating skill both **unlocks** abilities and **buys charges**; the player then PICKS the craft skill (`craftChoices` — the recommended one free, another trade at +DV, some alts limited to certain verbs). `programDV` = ability DV + craft penalty + trojan cover. `programEfficiency(total, dv, rank)` is what the margin buys: clean = full charges / stealth DV 12+margin (capped 20) / run DV −2; sloppy = 1 charge / stealth DV 8 / run DV +2 / GLITCHY.

**The Content branch does NOT reimplement authoring — it opens the REAL composer.** `PlayerEntryComposer` (scripts/ui/ShardEncoder/) subclasses `DataShardComposer` for exactly two reasons a per-instance option cannot cover: `ADMIN_THEMED` is read as `this.constructor.ADMIN_THEMED` and this window is player-facing, and it needs its own window id so it can't collide with a composer the GM has open. The parent gained a **`playerMode`**: it **COLLECTS instead of persisting** (`onCollect(entryData)` — the Encoder keeps the entry, and only the encode roll decides whether it is ever written), its `_prepareContext` / `_onSaveEntry` isGM gates relax, the type picker is filtered to the GM's `allowedTypes`, and the template hides the GM-only apparatus (③ SECURITY & ACCESS, the template library) behind `{{#unless playerMode}}`. The inherited static actions still work because they are bound to the PARENT's methods by name and branch on the flag.
- **The roll is the forgery's quality, not a gate.** `contentDV` (the type's DV + the penalty for using the other trade) → `contentEfficiency` → `quality` on the same `50 + margin × 8` scale, and a **`scanDV` that mirrors `UploadFlow.stealthDVFromQuality` exactly** (the sim asserts the formula still matches), written to the entry as **`contentData.sniffDV`** — which is what ANALYZE rolls against. So a shard a player forged reads to ANALYZE and SWEEP like one they uploaded, and a sloppy fake really is easier to catch.
- **A type with no craft skill needs no roll at all** (`_needsRoll()`): a message, a note and a cred drop are not fakes, so the die never appears, the ladder is replaced with "nothing to fake, nothing to roll", and the blank is never at risk. Do not "fix" this by giving them a DV.
- The centre panel previews the finished file through `enrichContent` + the shared `content-body` partial, with the `--sp-*` tokens mapped onto the preview so a typed file looks the way it will on the chip.

**The LOOK is the player's, the SECURITY is the roll's.** The Blank stage's **Look** row sets the chip art and a cosmetic preset. Art defaults to the Cyberpunk RED compendium chip (`systems/cyberpunk-red-core/icons/compendium/gear/memory_chip.svg`, `DEFAULT_SHARD_IMG`) with five one-click alternatives from the same set and a FilePicker for anything else; it is written to the **item**, never into the shard config (`_lookConfig` stamps `__img` and `_shardConfig` strips it). A preset contributes **`theme` + `boot` ONLY** — several shipped `SHARD_PRESETS` carry Black ICE at DV 20, and a player must not inherit that by liking the artwork, so the Encoder passes explicit overrides and never hands `convertToDataShard` a preset KEY (which would apply `security` too). Security comes from the Lock stage, where it is rolled for.

**The roll follows what was WRITTEN, not what was picked.** The composer carries its own "Change type", so a player can start a message and finish a dossier. `_adoptEntryType(entry)` runs on collect AND again before the encode: an allowed type is adopted (with the craft skill re-synced, since Forgery is not Accounting), and anything the GM disallows is reverted with a reason rather than rolled at a DV that does not belong to it. Without it a rewritten message was forged at the message's nonexistent DV.

**The chip designer bakes an icon — `scripts/ui/ShardEncoder/ShardIconBuilder.js`.** A framework-free promise modal (the SecurityConsole / OperatorSelect family, mounted into `host` so it wears that window's palette) composing a shape, a colour, and a mark that is either a Font Awesome symbol or any image. The **chip shape is the Encoder core's own silhouette** — the square with the top-left and bottom-right corners cut in, mirroring `.ncm-fw-chip-core`'s `clip-path` on canvas — so the icon on the item is recognisably the thing the player watched compile. Colour is a `CT_COLORS` swatch **or any hex** from a native picker; `hexFor()` takes either, and dragging the picker repaints live without rebuilding the swatch row. The icon is baked onto **`#1b1f21`**, the flat near-black every Foundry item tile sits on — a transparent PNG shows the sheet through the chip and reads as a hole in the row. It resolves a **PNG data URI, not a path**: writing a real file needs upload permission a player usually lacks, while a data URI is a string on an item they already own. **The Font Awesome trick:** a class name carries no character — the glyph lives in the stylesheet's `::before { content }` — so a hidden probe element is mounted, `getComputedStyle(el, '::before').content` is read back, and THAT character is drawn in the FA family. `paintIcon` is shared by the preview, the two small previews and the bake, so what is approved is the pixels that land. A cross-origin image taints the canvas and `toDataURL` throws; that is caught and explained rather than handed back broken.

**A cred drop is money MOVED, not made — `_eddiesLoad()`.** An `eddies` entry needs no roll, so nothing stands between a player and minting eb from nothing; the old `ShardConversionFlow` debited the author's wealth and the Encoder must too. The amount is checked against wealth **before the roll** (together with a bought blank's price, so a player is never told halfway), debited **only once something is really written** (a rejected encode returns before the money moves — the blank is spent, the money is not), re-checked at the moment of writing in case wealth moved, and **refunded with the blank price if the write then fails**. It is stated in three places: the readout sentence, the artifact card, and the encode stage's locked-in list.

**The startup is the player's to write.** The Look row also sets what the shard SAYS when it opens: title, the line under it, one of the nine boot choreographies `ItemInboxApp._bootAnimations` actually implements (offering a tenth would be a dead option — the sim cross-checks the two lists), a speed, and a switch to skip the startup entirely. A preset seeds the animation and the placeholders; typing over them is an override, and `_bootConfig` writes ONLY fields the player filled, so an untouched shard keeps the preset's own words instead of being flattened to the generic defaults.

**Copying shards into the Items directory — Admin → Shards → "Copy Shards to Items".** The recurring question is where a player-made shard shows up for the GM, and the honest answer has two halves: an item **embedded in an actor cannot be foldered at all**, and moving it would take the shard off the player's sheet. So `DataShardService.archiveShardsToItems()` makes **reference copies** as world items, filed under **Player-Made Shards → the character**. Each copy keeps the source's `journalId`, so it is a **live window** on the player's real shard rather than a snapshot that drifts. `surveyShardArchive()` is the read-only census the dialog shows first (what would be copied, what already is, which copies have lost their shard). Re-running is idempotent — a source that already has a copy is skipped, and a copy is never itself copied.
- **The guard that makes this safe:** the admin Unconvert calls `removeDataShard(item, true)`, which deletes the linked journal. An archive shares that journal, so deleting a copy would gut the player's shard — `removeDataShard` now refuses to delete a journal when the item carries `shardArchive`. Never remove that check.

**Player-made shards are filed under their maker.** `MessageRepository.getSubfolderPath([...])` creates a nested journal-folder path, and `convertToDataShard` uses it when `configOverrides.playerMade.actorName` is set: the linked journal lands in **Data Shards / Player-Made / <character>** instead of the flat folder. Folder creation is a world write, so the helper is **GM-only and degrades** — a player gets the deepest folder that already exists and the journal simply stays put, rather than throwing. The **item** cannot be foldered at all (it is embedded in an actor), so the Admin → Shards row carries a **"Made by <character>" badge** instead (gold when the forgery was sloppy), which is also how a GM learns a dossier is a fake before the NPC it accuses does.

**The Agent encodes as the character it is OPEN as.** `MessageViewerApp` has `actorId`, NOT `actor` — the first cut passed `this.actor` (undefined), so the Encoder fell back to `resolveOperator()` and quietly encoded as the remembered character rather than the one whose Agent was on screen. The door now passes `game.actors.get(this.actorId)`, and the Encoder's Operator card shows on EVERY stage (naming the character even where nothing rolls) so the question can never be silent again.

**PERMISSIONS — three of the Encoder's writes are the player's own, and three are not.** Theirs: creating a bought blank on their actor, paying for it out of `system.wealth`, and renaming/restyling the item. Not theirs: the shard journal, the world settings, and the price — all relayed. `_permissionProblem()` checks the player-side ones **up front (on open) and again before the roll**, in one sentence each: no operator · an operator you do not OWN (both the buy and the payment write to that actor) · authoring switched off mid-session. `_createBlank` refuses loudly rather than returning a silent null, the cosmetic item writes are wrapped so a failure cannot cost a shard that was already rolled for, and `EncoderConfigApp.render` refuses for a non-GM at the door instead of failing at Save.

**The LOCK stage is a CEILING, never a check — and it is optional.** A lock roll cannot be failed: the total picks a rung in `cfg.lock.ceilings` (the table the old `ShardConversionFlow` used, now a GM setting), and the rung says the highest encryption DV you may set and which affordances open. A bad roll still locks the chip, it just cannot lock it hard. Two independent, optional rolls: **Cryptography** buys the encryption ceiling and Black ICE (which ALSO needs the GM's `lock.blackIce`), **Electronics/Security Tech** buys the bite-back failure mode (10+) and a key-item reader (20+). A password needs no skill and no roll at all — it is a secret you set.
- **`lock.allowed: false` removes the stage**, it does not grey it out: `_stages()` filters `STAGES`, and the spine, the footer count and the navigation all read that filtered list. Never index `STAGES` directly.
- **`_lockConfig()` writes the shard config's OWN long-standing field names** (`encrypted`/`encryptionType`/`encryptionDC`/`failureMode`, `requiresLogin`/`loginPassword`, `requiresKeyItem`/`keyItemName`…), so a player-locked shard is breached by exactly the same code as a GM-locked one. An untouched lock writes **nothing** — an open shard must not carry half a security block.

**MARKET — the player proposes, the GM disposes (`scripts/ui/ShardEncoder/ShardMarket.js`).** It renders only at the seal, because there is nothing to price until the thing exists. `cfg.pricing`: **off** (never rendered) · **propose** (the default and the dev's locked decision — the figure does NOT touch the item until the GM approves) · **free** · **formula**. The write is always the GM's: `system.price.market` is CPR's field on an item a player may not own, so `propose` relays through **`SHARD_PRICE_REQUEST`** and the GM's own client prompts and writes. **`applyShardPrice` refuses a non-GM caller**, so the mode is enforced where the write happens rather than in the UI. Three failure paths are handled out loud rather than silently: no GM online, a prompt closed without answering (= **deny**, never a silent yes), and no answer inside two minutes.

**`ShardEncoderApp` is hosted in the Malware Workshop FRAME.** Its root classes are `['ncm-app', 'ncm-malware-workshop', 'ncm-shard-encoder']`, so every `.ncm-fw-*` rule IS the real chrome and only `.ncm-enc-*` is new (`styles/components/shard-encoder.css`, imported **after** malware-workshop.css — the sim asserts the order). Build-once-and-patch like the Workshop: the PART is a bare root, `_mount()` builds it and every interaction patches the DOM, never `render()` — which is also what lets the stage transition (out-fold → handoff sweep → core morph → cascade in) animate on live nodes. Two Workshop rules needed a local override: `.ncm-fw-icecard__ic` is **red** there because it means Black ICE (a blank chip is not a threat), and `.ncm-fw-chip` is styled as a SPAN, so a `<button>` wearing it still gets Foundry's white default.

**It is NOT `ADMIN_THEMED`** (it is a player window; the GM config app IS) and the sim asserts both directions.

**Writes:** the player owns the blank item, but a shard journal is GM-owned, so conversion + the first entry ride the existing `SHARD_CONVERT_REQUEST` relay. **`_canPersist()` runs BEFORE the roll** (the sim asserts the ordering) — never after something irreversible. A bought blank debits wealth and **refunds on any failure**. A **bricked** blank is FLAGGED (`flags.<MODULE>.blankBricked` + a renamed item), not deleted — non-destructive, auditable, and `_candidates()` filters bricked chips out so it can never be re-used. The entry is built through **`programToEntry`** (the Workshop's own shape, so the Deck / upload / SWEEP need nothing) and stamped with `provenance.playerMade` + the forge quality; the shard config carries a `playerMade` block for the GM's audit, and `TraceService.logSuccess` writes the Security-tab row when `cfg.audit` is on.

**Three doors, one launcher — `game.nightcity.openEncoder({item?, stage?, family?, actor?})`** (registerReadyServices ready/115, refuses when the GM has not switched authoring on): the Agent's Shards tab **"Encode new"** action pill (`open-encoder`, gated on the context flag `canEncode`), the item right-click (`ShardSheetOverride._launchConversionFlow`, whose door-side gate is `ShardSheetOverride.canAuthor(actor)` → `canEncode`), and the **Program Deck's first empty cartridge** (`.ncm-pd-cart--new`). Every one of them, and the launcher itself, judges through `canEncode` — see the encoder-gate section.

**GM card: Admin → Shards → Player Authoring → `EncoderConfigApp`** (`ADMIN_THEMED`, admin vocabulary: display chrome, mono values, 2px radius, `.ncm-ct-row__check`). Five tabs — Basics (the master switch, pricing, lock, re-encode/copy, audit) · Blanks (tier cards with price / botch rules / perk / an item picker in the **burn-picker pattern**) · What they can make (the 18-type allow-list with craft skill + DV + alt) · Programs (families, gating skill, alts, per-ability DV + rank, charges, trojan cover) · Optional depth. **Nothing is written until Save** (`this.draft` is the working copy), and it builds-once-and-patches because a settings sheet full of live inputs must not lose focus to a re-render.

**ACCESSIBILITY — `scripts/utils/legibility.js` is the ONE place, and any FUI surface can adopt it.** Per-player (client setting `legibilityPrefs`), opened from the Encoder's footer **DISPLAY** button, applied live with no re-render. `applyLegibility(root, prefs)` stamps `--ncm-lg-text` plus `data-lg-motion|density|outlines|font` and writes the contrast palette inline via the shared `applyContrastToElement`. Seven knobs: **text size** (80–160), **contrast** (70–140), **motion** (full/calm/off), **density** (compact/roomy), **stronger box edges**, **plain font**, **always show the numbers** — plus five one-click presets (Default / Large text / High contrast / Calm / Maximum).
- **It sits ON TOP of the theme, it does not replace it.** Text size MULTIPLIES the player's Agent Text Size — the Encoder redefines `--ncm-text-scale: calc(var(--ncm-text-scale-base,1) * var(--ncm-lg-text,1))`, which is the shard-viewer composition trick and means the **borrowed Workshop classes scale too** (they are all `calc(Npx * var(--ncm-text-scale,1))`). Contrast is the same OKLCH tone-map as the Theme Customizer, and **100% removes the inline overrides** so an untouched window keeps following the theme live.
- **Motion can only ever be LOWERED.** `effectiveMotion` forces `off` when the OS asks for reduced motion or the module's animation pref is off; the player's choice can never raise it. Every beat of the encode sequence reads it (`off` = instant, `calm` = shorter with no looping decoration).
- **Plain font works by redefining the font slots** (`--ncm-font-display/title/mono/ui` → the body face) plus one `text-transform:none` sweep, so it reaches the borrowed chrome without touching a single component rule.

**First live run (2026-09-21) — four fixes worth keeping in mind.** (1) The blank list has a **search + filter chips**: the search filters the LIVE DOM (re-rendering the rail per keystroke steals focus), and the chips are **one per item TYPE the character actually carries** (Gear, Cyberware, Weapons…, labelled by `_typeLabel`) plus All and Blanks. They render whenever the character carries anything — the first cut only showed them when the GM had registered blank items, so a fresh world had no filter at all. (2) **The player composer must not demand an item** — a BOUGHT blank has none until the encode succeeds, and requiring one showed the GM-only "No shard selected" panel, so player mode renders and saves without one. (3) **Every type tile names its skill**, not just the selected one — otherwise you have to click all eleven to learn which trade each wants. (4) **A finished shard has to be findable**: the item directory, the actor sheet and the Agent SHARDS tab all cache their lists, so `_refreshShardViews()` repaints them, the seal's button becomes **Open it**, and the hint names whose gear it is in.

**Test setup:** `dev/tools/test-encoder-setup.macro.js` — paste into a Script macro as GM: switches player authoring on, puts one blank of each TIER on a carrier actor and registers them by name in their tiers, then prints a what-to-try list (the three doors, a forgery, a rejected/bricked blank, the program branch, the accessibility panel, the two-client relay test).

**Verification:** `node dev/tools/sims/test-encoder-model.mjs` (165 checks — config layering, tiers, the ladder, program gating/efficiency, the content branch (no-roll types, the scan-DV formula, the collect-not-persist contract), the lock ceilings (monotonic, never empty, the GM veto), the market (GM-only writes, deny-on-close, no-GM), the legibility clamps, and the wiring a window can get wrong: stylesheet order, template registration, class-prefix collisions, admin-theme flags, relay-before-roll). Phase 6 gained **6.14** (model + settings + ladder agreement + every offered verb has an applier) and **6.15** (legibility clamps and stamps). Visual: **`dev/mockups/shard-encoder-probe.html`** mounts the REAL app against the real cascade (`?stage=blank|content|program|lock|encode`, `?type=`, `?wrote=1`, `?rolled=`, `?sealed=clean|sloppy`, `?preset=large|contrast|calm|max`, `?display=1`, `?still=1`, `?h=`) and reports contrast / clipping / the stamped prefs in `<title>`. **`?still=1` matters**: headless advances JS timers but NOT CSS animations, so an entrance cascade captures frozen at opacity 0.

## Architecture: shards players don't own — the state relay, the editing rule, re-encode, Permissions (2026-10-21)

Tester report: "to modify a shard a player needs Owner rights, and Owner puts the shard in the Items sidebar before its reveal" + "we haven't done anything to update existing shards". Both were real; they are two different problems.

- **A shard that arrives as a message attachment is a WORLD item the player has no permission on, and every state write a player's hack caused went straight to `item.update`** — `_handleHackSuccess` (decrypted), `_updateActorSession` (attempts / lockouts / login / key item), `_decryptMessage` (a page on the GM-owned journal), `_destroyShard`. The roll succeeded, the write threw, the shard looked like it never unlocked — hence "needs Owner". **Now every one of them goes through `_canWriteItem(item)` (GM or `item.isOwner`) → direct, else `_relayState(op, item, payload)` → `SOCKET_OPS.SHARD_STATE_REQUEST`** (ops `decrypt` | `session {actorId, updates}` | `decryptPage {messageId}` | `destroy`; GM handler `handleStateRequest`, one GM lands it; no GM online → warns + returns false). A shard on the player's own sheet is still a direct write (they own the actor). **So a world shard needs NO ownership at all** — leave it at None, it stays out of the sidebar, and the player hacks it through the Agent. Don't reintroduce a bare `item.update` on a player-reachable path; the sim greps for it.
- **Per-shard editing rule — `config.playerEditing`: `locked` | `maker` | `qualified`** (Configure Shard → Security → "Player Editing"; `SHARD_EDIT_MODES` + the pure `resolveShardEditing(config)` in EncoderModel.js). **A missing value resolves by provenance: player-made (`playerMade.actorId`) → maker only, everything else → locked** — so every GM-made shard, and every pre-existing one, is read-only for players with no migration and nothing to remember. It governs WRITING only (add a file / re-encode); hacking, claiming, cracking and reading are play, not modification, and never consult it. **`canEditShard(item, actor)` (helpers.js) is the ONE door-side answer**: GM → ok; the shard's rule; then `canEncode`; then `encoderConfig.reencode.allowed`. Doors: the shard viewer's + button (`canPlayerEdit` → a player's `addEntry` opens the Encoder, never the GM composer), the item right-click "Encode More Onto It" (directory + inventory row), `_launchConversionFlow` on an already-converted item, and the Encoder itself (`_permissionProblem` re-judges before the roll). Admin → Shards rows wear an **Editable · maker/qualified** badge when the rule is open.
- **Re-encode is REAL now** — `encoderConfig.reencode` was a dead knob (the GM card ticked "Rewrite a sloppy shard" and nothing read it). The Encoder has a third source, **`srcMode: 'edit'`** ("Re-encode a shard": carried shards passing `canEditShard`; a shard handed to `openEncoder({item})` opens there).
