Rules: the LOCK stage is absent (`_stages()` drops it — the lock was rolled when the shard was made), the Look row is hidden, DV = stack DV + `reencode.dv`, room = the chip's own stamped `config.capacity` minus what is on it (`_onChip()`), a rejection **never bricks** (`_botch()` → "the shard is unchanged"; every botch read goes through it), and the write is **`DataShardService.reencodeShard(item, {…, entries})`** (GM direct; player rides `SHARD_CONVERT_REQUEST` with a `reencode` block — the handler branches BEFORE converting): the new quality + `sniffDV` are re-stamped on every **player-made** entry only (a GM-authored entry on a shard the GM opened up is never touched), `config.playerMade` keeps the original maker/date and gains `reencodedAt/reencodes`, new files go through `addMessage` so capacity is enforced and a refusal is reported.
- **Permissions button** (Admin → Shards row + expanded actions, world items only — an embedded item has no ownership of its own): `shardOwnership` → `new DocumentOwnershipConfig(item)` (bare lexical reference with a typeof guard + the v13 namespace fallback, the ImagePopout lesson). It is for the GM who wants to hand a shard over outright; a player hacking one does not need it any more.
- Also: the Agent's SHARDS tab reads a received world shard's lock state LIVE (`config.encrypted && !state.decrypted`) instead of the attachment's as-sent stamp. Sim: `dev/tools/sims/test-shard-edit-relay.mjs` (38 — the writers as GM / owning player / non-owner, the handler, the rule matrix, `reencodeShard`, the Encoder door, every surface). Phase 6 gained **6.17**. **Not live-tested.** Known limit: the composer's Attach Shard picker is still a raw inline-styled list (not the shared name picker).

## Architecture: shard attachments resolve by UUID + the sender address follows the directory (2026-10-24)

Two tester reports, both real. **Not live-tested.** Sims: `test-shard-edit-relay.mjs` (43), `test-sender-name.mjs` (42).
- **"Attach a shard, open it from the message" has ALWAYS existed** (composer microchip button → DATA SHARD card → `open-shard`), but the guide never said so and the tester assumed ownership was the route. The guide + `dev/guide-export/` (HTML and the regenerated PDF) now say it in one paragraph. **The export pages are fixed-height A4 with `overflow:hidden`** — a paragraph added to one silently clips off the bottom; measure (`.page` scrollHeight vs clientHeight in headless Chrome) before re-printing. PDF = `chrome --headless=new --print-to-pdf --no-pdf-header-footer` over the HTML.
- **A shard sent from a CHARACTER's inventory arrived as "shard not found."** An embedded item's id only means something inside that actor, and the viewer looked only in `game.items` + the RECIPIENT's own gear. The composer now stamps **`itemUuid`** beside `itemId` on the attachment, and **`MessageViewerApp._resolveAttachedShard(att)`** is the ONE lookup (uuid → world → viewing actor → every actor, the legacy fallback for pre-uuid attachments) used by `_openShard`, `_openAttachment` and the SHARDS tab's `_buildShardItems`; both click surfaces carry `data-item-uuid`. A gone shard reports `SHARD_GONE_MSG`, never "not in your inventory". The state relay already keyed on `item.uuid`, so an embedded shard hacks fine once found. Known limit: the legacy all-actors scan picks the first actor holding that id (ambiguous only when two actors carry an item with the same embedded id).
- **Sender address rewrote to `name@nightcity.net`.** The Send-as list picks a master contact that HAS a linked actor **by actor id** (`fromContact` cleared), so the service saw only `fromActorId` and `_stampOutgoing` stamped flag-or-synthesized, never the directory address. **`MessageService._senderEmailFor`** is the twin of `_recipientEmailFor`: registered flag → directory entry (by `fromContactId`, else `getByActorId`) → '' (synthesize). The composer header + signature read the same order through **`_fromEmail()`**. Mail already stored keeps its synthesized `from`; for it the viewer's **`_findSenderContact(msg)`** (address → directory address → actor/master id) decides "in contacts", and `_addContactFromMessage` stores the DIRECTORY address when one exists. Groups/fan-out are unaffected (the base is stamped once through the same path).
- **Found on the way: the encrypted-attachment "Breach" / GM "Force decrypt" buttons called APIs that were never built** (`attemptBreachAttachment`, `forceBreachAttachment`), and the panel rendered a second LOCKED copy of every attachment of an encrypted message AFTER the message was decrypted (the composer stamps `encrypted: true` on each attachment of an encrypted message; the normal branch renders only post-decrypt). `encryptedAttachments` is now `classified.encrypted.filter(a => !!a.ice)` — dormant until a writer gives an attachment its own ICE block. Don't resurrect the per-attachment breach without building the service side.

## Architecture: shard naming, blank rule, terminal mail import (tester round 2026-10-26)

Tester (Aegaeon): players could encode over any carried item (pistol, cyberarm); shards "couldn't be renamed"; a terminal account linked to an NPC showed none of that NPC's existing mail. Sim: `dev/tools/sims/test-shard-naming-and-import.mjs` (64). **Not live-tested.**
- **Conversion is reversible.** `DataShardService.convertToDataShard` (the ONE door every convert path uses — GM, Encoder relay, terminal burn, macro) records `flags.<id>.original = {name, img}`; `removeDataShard` restores both (`restorePatch` in the pure `scripts/utils/shardNaming.js`; a pre-2026-10-26 shard has no record and just loses the prefix; an archive copy keeps its own name).
- **Name prefix** — world setting `shardNamePrefix` (default '', read via `SettingsManager.getShardNamePrefix()`): `withShardPrefix` on convert, on the Encoder's rename and on Rename…; stripped on unconvert. Case-insensitive, never doubled.
- **Rename** — `Rename…` on the directory + inventory right-click menus (`ShardSheetOverride._renameShard`, `canRename` = GM, or an owner the shard's `canEditShard` rule allows). It writes `config.shardName` AND the (prefixed) item name together — the viewer title prefers `shardName`, so renaming only the item looked like it hadn't stuck. "View Original Item" is now "Edit Item Sheet".
- **The blank rule** — `encoderConfig.blanks.source`: `gear` (default — Gear-type items + anything listed in a tier + bought blanks) | `registered` | `any` (the old behaviour). Pure `isEligibleBlank(cfg, {name, type, tierId})` + `blankRuleRefusal(cfg)` in EncoderModel. Read by the Encoder's blank list + seed, the player convert button / right-click / launch flow, and the terminal Burn-to-Shard picker for players (GM unrestricted). GM card: Player Authoring → Blanks → "What can be written on". The Encoder confirms before renaming/re-arting an item the player already owned.
- **Terminal accounts** — `account.mirrorAgentMail` (absent = on): on = received mail mirrors into the Inbox (`mirrorDeliveryToAccounts`) AND the identity's outgoing mail into the Sent folder (`mirrorSentToAccounts`, called from `MessageService._createSentCopy`, the one sent-copy writer; matched FROM-side by contact id / actor id / address); off stops both (a "work machine"). `TerminalService.gatherIdentityMail(item, accountId)` (read-only: the linked actor/contact inboxes found by `identifyInbox`, received + sent, minus deleted/spam) + `copyMessagesToAccount(item, accountId, messages)` (mirror rules: id `copyIdFor(id, acct)` = `<id>-t-<acct>` so re-imports are no-ops, From/timestamp kept, eddies 0; SENT mail lands in a "Sent" custom folder, reused by name or created once by `_ensureSentFolder`). Doors: the account's Mail tab **Import from inbox** (linked accounts only; multi-select picker, already-copied rows greyed) and the GM **Terminal** button on a message in the Agent viewer (`copy-to-terminal` → one picker over every account on every terminal).

## Architecture: SEND AS A TERMINAL ACCOUNT (2026-10-26)

Dev: "if I hack into a computer I can log into so-and-so's account and email people on their contacts." Sim `dev/tools/sims/test-terminal-send-as.mjs` (43); probe `terminal-files-probe.html?write=1`. **Not live-tested.**
- **GM switch** `config.accountMail = {compose:false, log:true}` — Configure Terminal → Accounts → **Sending as an account** (`acctMailCompose` / `acctMailLog`, saved only when on screen). Off by default.
- **Who it sends as**: `TerminalService.accountSendIdentity(item, accountId)` = the account's linked directory contact (→ the main identity of its linked character → the directory entry for its address) + the character id. `canAccountCompose` also requires a DIRECTORY contact or a character this client can see (a player can't see a GM's NPC sheet, so an actor-only link can't send — the account editor's email link-or-create normally gives every account one).
- **Address book**: `accountAddressBook(item, accountId)` = everyone in the account's own mail (senders of received mail, recipients of its Sent folder, comma-split), plus the linked character's `contacts` flag when readable; directory names where known; the owner's own addresses (every identity) and files/other accounts/the shared volume excluded; most-used first.
- **UI**: a **WRITE** chip in the HUD (every layout) + **Reply as <user>** on open mail (`terminalComposeAs` / `terminalReplyAs`; TTY `write` / `reply`). `_composeAsContext` reads the BOX (an admin viewing with full rights writes as the viewed account; `adminReadOnlyBlocks('send')` stops read-only). The launcher's `terminalSend` branch skips owner resolution + Email Setup; the composer locks the sender (`canSwitchFrom`, a VIA <machine> tag), its To search is the address book, eddies are off, `messageData.terminalSend = {itemUuid, accountId}`, and success writes a `send-as` Activity row (`_logTerminalSend`, unless `log` is off).
- **Guard**: `MessageService.handleMessageRelay` runs `TerminalService.verifyAccountSend(data)` for any `terminalSend` — the machine allows it, the account exists, and the sender IS that account (its contact or one of its identities, its character) — else it acks `Refused: …` and delivers nothing. Sent copies land in the owner's mailbox and (mirror on) the account's Sent folder.
- **Your OWN account vs an impersonation** (dev: "as GM I may get lost on who is sending what"): the viewer stamps `terminalSend.own` when the logged-in PLAYER owns the account's character (John on John's workstation) — then eddies work and no `send-as` Activity row is written; an impersonation keeps both blocks + the row. **Every** terminal send carries `sentVia = {machine, account, operatorId, operatorName, impersonated}`, persisted by `MessageRepository.createMessage` **only when `status.sent`** — i.e. on the SENDER's sent copy, never the recipient's page (the recipient must not be able to read who really typed). Admin → Messages shows it on the row: "via WS-7F-12" (own) or a red "via WS-7F-12 · typed by Rache" (impersonated) — on the sent row AND, lent by base message id (display only), on the matching received row — and in the NPC Inbox panel (`_sentViaIndex`), where an impersonation counts as PLAYER mail even though its sender looks like the NPC.

## Architecture: IDENTITIES — one character, several contacts (2026-10-26)

Tester: "can one actor have multiple email addresses / aliases?" Not a second address system: an **identity is a GM master contact LINKED to the character** (`contact.actorId`). Mail to any of them already lands in the character's inbox (`_resolveRecipient` → linked actor). The contact field `alias` is the unrelated display subtitle — in code these are IDENTITIES; the UI says "main" / "alias of …". Sim `dev/tools/sims/test-identities.mjs` (73). **Not live-tested, no mock was made (dev: "do all those things").**
- **Rules = pure `scripts/utils/identities.js`**: `identitiesOf(contacts, actorId)` (main first), `primaryOf`, `identityRole(contacts, c)` → `{isPrimary, isAlias, primary, count}` (null for an unlinked or lone contact), `aliasLabel`, `identityUsage(messages, identities)` (a message counts for the identity it names — `toContactId` received / `fromContactId` sent — else the main), `replyIdentity(contacts, original, replierActorId)`. **Main = `contact.primaryIdentity`** (whitelisted in add/update), else the first linked. **`MasterContactService.getByActorId` now returns the MAIN identity** (unchanged when none is flagged); `identitiesFor`, `identityRoleOf`, `setPrimaryIdentity` (one per character), `addIdentity(actorId, {name, email})` (GM).
- **The exact identity now survives a send** — before, the composer collapsed a character-linked contact to the character and the mail recorded the MAIN address: `_addRecipient(actorId, locked, ident)` keeps `{contactId, email, name}`; autocomplete passes it; GM/player search dedupe by contact/address (not by character) so every identity is its own row; a sole recipient sends its `toContactId`. **Send-as** keeps BOTH `fromActorId` and the picked identity (`fromContact`, list type ALIAS); `_fromEmail` and `MessageService._senderEmailFor` let an explicitly picked identity OF THAT CHARACTER win over the registered address. **Replies**: `buildReply` answers the identity the mail came FROM (`toContactId` + `to`) and sends AS the identity it reached (`fromContactId`/`from` via `replyIdentity`); the composer adopts both.
- **GM tracking**: GM Contact Manager **Identities card** (`_identitiesContext` — every linked contact, ★ main [`identSetPrimary`], open [`identOpen`], counts + last use from the character's inbox, the registered address when no identity carries it, **+ Add alias** [`identAdd` → `addIdentity`, address + duplicate checks]); Admin → Contacts (alias rows nested + indented under their main with "alias of X", main row "main · N identities", an **Aliases** filter pill, a search hit brings the whole group); Admin → Messages (feed rows + NPC Inbox show "`alias` · alias of X" via `_aliasNote`; filtering by a character includes all its identities). CSS `.ncm-gm-ident*`, `.ncm-ct-row--alias`/`.ncm-ct-badge--alias|--main`, `.ncm-msg-alias`. Export/import carry `actorId` + `primaryIdentity` as part of the contact.
- **NPCs with NO actor have identities too** (dev, same day). Groups are keyed by `groupKeyOf(contacts, c)`: `a:<actorId>` for a character's linked contacts, `c:<rootContactId>` for a contact-only NPC whose aliases carry **`identityOf: <the NPC contact id>`** (whitelisted; an alias of an alias stores the ROOT; an `identityOf` pointing at a character's contact joins the character). Main = flagged `primaryIdentity`, else the root / first linked. **Routing**: `_resolveRecipient` sends an actor-less alias's mail into its group's inbox (the character, or the root NPC's contact inbox — `identityInboxId`) while `toContactId` stays the alias; `_createSentCopy` files mail sent AS such an alias in the same inbox. `MasterContactService.addContactIdentity(contactId, data)` / `identitiesForContact`; `setPrimaryIdentity` works per group. `buildReply(…, {fromContactId})` + `replyIdentity(contacts, orig, {actorId, contactId})` so the NPC answers AS the alias the mail reached. The GM card shows for EVERY contact (a lone one gets just itself + **+ Add alias**). Sim now 73.
- **Portraits: an alias NEVER borrows its character's face** (a burner showing Lucky Lin's portrait unmasks it). `resolveDisplayPortrait` and `PortraitService.resolvePortrait` (incl. a player's copy via `masterContactId`) return initials for an alias with no picture of its own; its own `portrait` still wins; the main identity falls back to the character as before. **+ Add alias** takes a picture (text + FilePicker via the prompt's `render`).

## Architecture: roll timing + the ROLL REVEAL (2026-10-27)

Dev: "a lot of roll checks… an animation to make it more climactic, then post to chat… make sure the other animations wait for Dice So Nice and for the animation, then post — the timing is off on some." Mock `dev/mockups/roll-reveal-mock.html` (frozen frames, `?o=&ctx=&t=&at=`), real-module probe `dev/mockups/roll-reveal-probe.html` (`?still=1` = the reduced-motion final frame), sim `dev/tools/sims/test-roll-reveal.mjs` (46). **Not live-tested; Dice So Nice untested with the real module.**
- **`SkillService.performCheck` owns the timing.** (1) **Dice So Nice** — `_showDice3d(rolls)` plays EVERY die (crit bonus / fumble penalty too; `_rollD10WithCritFumble` now returns `rolls[]`), synchronized, awaited before the method returns, capped at 12 s per die, errors swallowed; `dice3d:false` opts out. Before this the module never called DSN at all (the chat card carries no Roll). (2) **`reveal: {host, title, ctx, ok, no, verdictSound}`** plays the shared Roll Reveal in that window and posts the chat card at its verdict. (3) **`deferChat: true`** posts nothing and hands back **`result.postChat()`** (idempotent) for a caller with its own animation to fire at ITS climax. Neither → posts at once (macro/API paths).
- **`scripts/ui/content/RollReveal.js` + `styles/components/roll-reveal.css` (`ncm-rr-*`)** — framework-free, mounted into the asking window (wears its palette; body-mounted fallback is `--fixed`). One deterministic `paint(p)` fed by rAF + a wall-clock cap (the BootSplash pattern): tiles light (stat hidden for a rank-only role ability, Luck and bonus get tiles), the d10 spins and lands, a 10/1 flashes and rolls the second die, the total counts into a bar against the DV line, verdict, then `onChat`. ~2.6 s / ~3.5 s with a second die, hold 0.9 s, click skips / dismisses. Motion is VETOED by OS reduced motion or the animation pref (final frame, no spin sound). Sounds: hack-progress on the spin, hack-success/fail at the verdict unless `verdictSound:false` (callers that play their own outcome — the network dialog suppresses the adapter's via `checkSkill(…, {silent})`; terminal logins, message bypass, trace and entry decrypt keep their own).
- **Who reveals:** network/account login dialog, terminal quick logins (skill bypass, net spoof, net join), message network bypass, anonymous trace, message decrypt without the cipher grid, shard per-entry decrypt. **Who defers to its own climax:** shard breach (`attemptHack({silent})` → `deferChat`, the viewer posts after `_playHackSequence`), shard layer hacks, message decrypt grid + signal reconstruct (at the result card; attemptDecrypt with `deferChat` is also QUIET — no early sound/toast), contact ICE breach, the action console (at its verdict), the bank heist, System Sweep (its "rolled N" also waits). The Shard Encoder already reveals at the moment it posts; its lock roll has DV 0 and stays as is. **A new roll = `reveal` if it has no animation, `deferChat` + `postChat()` at the climax if it does** — never an immediate card under a running animation.

## Tester round 2026-10-27 — Send As identity, per-skill bypass DVs, gate skills

Sim `dev/tools/sims/test-tester-2026-10-27.mjs` (54). **Not live-tested.**
- **Send As sent from the OLD address after an NPC moved to a new main identity.** Admin → Contacts **Send As** passed only `fromActorId`, so the composer fell back to the actor's registered `email` flag, which nothing ever moved. Now: **every Send-As door passes `fromContactId`** (Admin → Contacts, the GM Contact Manager's Send As, Admin → Messages → Send as NPC — the GM Contact Manager one was missed in the first pass and was what the tester clicked; the sim greps all three for a bare `fromActorId: contact.actorId`); `MasterContactService._syncActorEmail(actorId)` rewrites an **NPC's** flag to its main identity's address whenever the main is starred (`setPrimaryIdentity`) or its email/actor/primary changes (`updateContact`); and `_senderEmailFor` / the composer's `_fromEmail` let a STARRED (`primaryIdentity`) main beat a stale flag — **NPCs only**: a player character's address is the player's Email Setup choice and is never rewritten or overridden.
- **Sender portraits follow the identity** (same round): the composer From avatar + reply quote card (name/address/portrait from `orig.fromContactId`), the Agent viewer sender avatar (an alias never falls back to `fromActor.img`, nor to a player contact that saved the main face), the terminal viewer (`_senderPortrait(msg)`: `fromContactId` → directory by address → actor) and the Messages → Send as NPC chips (one chip per IDENTITY now, own portrait via `resolveDisplayPortrait`) all go through the alias-safe resolvers. The composer's Send-as list lights only the picked identity.
- **Per-skill bypass DVs — `scripts/utils/bypassSkills.js`.** The shared SECURITY_BLOCK (networks + terminal accounts) gains an optional `bypassSkillDCs: {skill: dv}`; **`bypassDCFor(sec, skill)` is the ONE read** (space/punct-insensitive name match, falls back to `bypassDC` — no migration). `bypassFieldsFrom(rows)` writes `bypassSkills` + the map + `bypassDC` = the first row's DV (so a missed single-DV reader still sees a sane number). Editor = `bypassRowsHtml` / `wireBypassRows` / `readBypassRows` (unnamed inputs, `[data-bypass-rows]`, CSS `styles/components/bypass-skills.css`, `ncm-bypass__*`) in the Network Manager editor and the terminal account editor. The auth dialog shows each row's own DV and a range in the header (`bypassDCLabel`). Shards are separate (`config.skillDCs`, long-standing). The terminal quick-entry console still rolls the FIRST skill only.
- **Operator Access role picker did nothing** — `onGateAddRole` looked for `[data-role-chips]` inside a gear OR-group (`.ncm-sec-admin-gate__grp`); the Role block is a `__req`. Fixed, and `_appendChip` clears the "no role required" placeholder.
- **Gate skills are a LIST** — `gate.skills: [{name, min}]`, ANY one qualifies, each at its own rank; the legacy single `skill` is read as a list of one (`gateSkillsOf` in helpers.js for `canOperate`; `sanitizeGate` folds it into `skills` and clears it). Security tab = skill rows (`_gateSkillRowHtml`, actions `securityGateAddSkill`/`securityGateRemoveSkill`); the Player Authoring card edits `gate.skills.N.*` (`gateSkillAdd`/`gateSkillDel`).
- **World setting `roleAbilityRoll`** (Module Settings, default `stat` = unchanged): `rank` rolls a role ability (Interface) as rank + d10 — `SkillService._readSkillData` zeroes the stat (`statName: none`) for role abilities only, via `SkillService.roleAbilityRankOnly()`.

## Architecture: terminal ADMIN ACCESS — view another account (2026-10-26)

Tester: "Admin should be able to browse the accounts and have rights to their files/messages — like View As User." Dev-approved mock `dev/mockups/terminal-root-access-mock.html` (9 frames, every theme). Sim `dev/tools/sims/test-terminal-admin.mjs` (68); Phase 6 **6.20**; probe `terminal-files-probe.html?av=1|ro&sw=1`. **Not live-tested.**
- **Rules = the pure `scripts/utils/terminalAdmin.js`**: `resolveAdminAccess(cfg)` (`config.adminAccess = {browse, seeHidden, rights:'full'|'read'}`, all default on/full — absent keys resolve, no migration), `adminViewVerdict(cfg, viewer, target, {visible})` → `{ok, listed, reason}` (viewer must be `isAdmin`; never self; an unreachable net-gated account is not listed; hidden only with seeHidden; **`account.privateFromAdmin` is LISTED DEAD** with its reason), `adminViewTargets`, `adminLabelFor(cfg, TERMINAL_PRESETS)`, `adminReadOnlyBlocks(action)` (read-only blocks `wipe`; reading and copying out stay). TerminalService wraps them (`adminViewVerdict/Targets(item, viewerId, …)`, network check = `isContentVisible`).
- **The label is part of the THEME**: `config.theme.adminLabel` (≤24, upper-cased) → the preset's top-level `adminLabel` (all 12 carry one: ADMIN OVERRIDE / NETWATCH AUTHORITY / SYSOP / COMMAND ACCESS / HOUSE ACCESS …) → `ADMIN OVERRIDE`. **Edited on the Theme tab** (`[data-ttc-adminlabel]`, draft key, no re-render; blank or equal to the suggestion = no override; a preset card clears it), so saved themes, the preset library and theme export carry it for free. **Named on Accounts → Admin access** (read-only chip + a `switchTab` link with `data-focus` that lands on the field), which also holds the three settings (`adminBrowse`/`adminSeeHidden`/`adminRights`, saved ONLY when the section is on screen — it is absent while an account is being edited) and a "Right now…" line. Account editor: **Private from admin** (`acctPrivateAdmin`), row badge.
- **Viewer — `_viewAs` + ONE accessor**: `_boxId()` = the viewed account, else the logged-in one; `_boxMessages()` reads the box with `getAccountMessages(…, {gateAccountId: <admin>})` (the box's entries, folder gates judged with the ADMIN's rights). Every content read / exfil / GM authoring scope goes through them; login, session, footprint and the HUD ADMIN chip keep `_accountId` (the only direct read left is the own-login "new since" count — the sim asserts it). `_startAdminView` re-checks the verdict, logs `traceService.logSuccess({action:'admin-view', severity:'minor'})` (Activity Log, no heat), no session write, no read-state change (terminals never mark read). A view that stops being allowed is dropped on the next render; logout / login / force-logout clear it. Partials `terminal/partials/admin-bar.hbs` (banner under the HUD, every layout; TTY = a session line) + `admin-view-list.hbs` (VIEW AS ADMIN in all THREE switcher copies). FILES/desktop list `fs.otherHomes` (YOU = back; click = view that account in FILES). TTY: `su <name>` views, `exit` returns while viewing (else logs out as before). Actions `terminalAdminView/Back/Pick`. CSS `ncm-term__adminbar*` / `acdrop-*--admin` / `fs-home` / `ncm-cfg-adminlabel` in terminal.css, label colour = the theme's ACCENT.

**Players pick between aliases (2026-10-31, tester: "players can't figure out how to send with their alias"; the GM still creates them).** `senderChoices` / `pickSenderChoice` (identities.js) via `MasterContactService.senderChoicesFor(actor)`: the registered address is the main row (even with no directory entry), then the other linked identities, never a burned address. The Identity drawer's **Sending as** (player only, 2+ addresses) writes the actor flag `SEND_AS_FLAG` (absent = main), and a new or forwarded message starts there (`_applyDefaultSender`); a reply keeps the address the mail reached, a draft keeps its own. The composer's From caret overrides for one message, using the GM's dropdown with a player-only list. Sim `test-player-identities.mjs`; probe `player-sendas-probe.html`, `composer-netpicker-probe.html?as=player&alias=1&sendas=1`. **`EmailService.hasEmail` means "has an address"** (setup flag, actor address, or linked directory contact, minus burned), and the Character Select intercept matches `openInbox`: never a GM, only when `emailSetupRequired`.

## Architecture: what players call a shard — `shardTerm` (2026-10-26)

"Shard" is a 2077 word; a 2045 table says "memory chip". World setting **`shardTerm`** (Data Shard default / Memory Chip / Data Chip / Custom) + **`shardTermCustom`** ("Name" or "Name / Plural"; plural + short form derived), both `requiresReload`. **`scripts/utils/shardTerm.js` is the ONE reader**: JS `shardTerm(form, case)` (imported as `T`; forms `one` "Memory Chip" · `many` · `short` "Chip" · `shortMany`; case `upper`/`lower`), `withArticle()` for a/an, and the Handlebars helper **`{{ncm-term "short" "upper"}}`** (hash `pre`/`post`/`a=true`; works as a subexpression, e.g. a partial's `prompt=`). **Scope: player-facing surfaces + the item right-click menus** (shared by GM and players, so they follow the word for both); GM windows (Admin → Shards, Configure Shard, the entry composer, Workshop, Player Authoring card) keep saying "shard" — the plain-language rule. Labels only: flags, settings keys, classes and data-actions never change, so no migration. **Anything evaluated at module load must be a getter** (`MessageViewerApp.SHARD_GONE_MSG`, the terminal `EXFIL_META.shard` label/title/intro) or it freezes the default before settings exist. Sim `dev/tools/sims/test-shard-term.mjs` (47: resolution, the helper through Foundry's Handlebars, the player templates compile, and a guard that fails on a hardcoded "shard" in a player template's visible text or a player-facing JS toast/title/label). Preset NAMES (e.g. "Street Shard") are left as themed names. **Not live-tested.**

## Architecture: address directory export / import (Admin → Tools → Email Domains & Addresses, 2026-10-24)

Tester ask: "mass import a csv or json of emails." Master contacts already had JSON import/export; **actor addresses (the `email` flag) had neither**. The Tools-tab domain dialog (`ToolsTab.onManageDomains`) now carries two tool rows: **Export / Import domains** (JSON envelope `{ncmEmailDomains:1, default, domains, allowCustom}`, or one domain per line; the import fills the OPEN dialog's rows and Save persists) and **Addresses: Export CSV / Export JSON / Import…**.
- **The rules are the pure module `scripts/utils/emailDirectory.js`** (no Foundry globals; sim `dev/tools/sims/test-email-import.mjs`, 46 checks). `buildDirectoryRows(world)` = every person-actor with an address + every master contact with one (`kind: actor|contact`, both ids; a linked contact exports as its own row because the two addresses CAN differ). `parseDirectoryImport(text)` reads CSV/TSV (header by alias in any order — `Character`/`Address`/`Contact ID`… — or positional `name,email[,kind]`; a header is only a header when NO cell is an address and it is mostly alias words, because `Judy Alvarez,judy@mox.nc,contact` has one alias hit and is data) and JSON (the envelope, a bare array, a `{name: address}` map, or a master-contacts export). **`planDirectoryImport(rows, world)` writes nothing**: per row → `update-actor` | `update-contact` | `create-contact` | `skip` with a reason. Order: explicit actorId → explicit contactId → character by name (space/punct-insensitive) → contact by name → (a name, no `kind`) new contact. `kind:'actor'` never touches/creates a contact; `kind:'contact'` never touches a character. **A blank address never clears one. An unchanged row is a skip even when its linked twin differs** (re-importing an export plans ZERO writes — the sim asserts it). When the primary changes, the character↔master-contact **twin is kept in step (`also`)** and is never reported as a stranger holding the address; a true stranger is a WARNING, as is a duplicate address in the file and a player character (the GM is overriding what the player chose). Same target twice → the second row is skipped naming the first. Ambiguous names (two actors) → skip asking for the id column. ICE/demon/container actors are never targets.
- **The world adapter + writes live in ToolsTab** (`_directoryWorld`, `_exportDirectory`, `_importDirectory` → the plan PREVIEW dialog (`.ncm-dirimp-*`, admin-themed, table `margin:0`) → Apply → `_applyDirectoryPlan`): characters through **`EmailService.forceSetEmail`** (the one writer; it warns on a taken address), contacts through `MasterContactService.updateContact({email})` / `addContact` (new contacts are `type:'npc'`, not send-only, with a provenance note). Failures are collected and toasted, never thrown mid-list. **`scripts/utils/csv.js` is now THE CSV parser/writer** — `DataShardComposer._parseCsv` delegates to it (the sim asserts the inline copy is gone). Mail already sent keeps the `from` it was stamped with (standing rule). **Not live-tested.**

## Architecture: ACCESS POINTS — every way into a terminal / shard / inbox (2026-10-29, step 1)

Tester ask: "rethink terminal placement… macro options… options for placements?" Dev-approved mocks `dev/mockups/access-points-mock.html` + `access-points-v2-mock.html`; real-markup probe `dev/mockups/access-points-probe.html`; sim `dev/tools/sims/test-access-points.mjs` (39). **Not live-tested.**
- **A ref is `{kind, id}`** — `terminal` / `shard` (item UUID), `inbox` (owner id), `message` (`<ownerId>|<messageId>`). **ONE door: `game.nightcity.openAccess(ref)`** (→ openTerminal / openDataShard / openInbox; a player can't open an inbox they don't own). Every trigger carries a ref: **hotbar macro** (`createAccessMacro(ref, {slot})` — reuses this user's macro for the ref, flag `access`, GM macros land in folder "NCM — Access points"; the body is `accessMacroCommand`, guarded, double-quoted), **drag to the hotbar** (`hotbarDrop` hook: `{type:'NCMAccess', ref}` from an admin rack unit / shard cartridge via `wireAccessDrag`, OR any converted Item dragged from the directory — it becomes a login macro instead of an item-sheet macro), **chat card** (`postAccessCard` → a dialog: who sees it [Everyone / Only GMs / Only <player>; a whisper ALWAYS includes the GMs — `whisper: []` is public] + the colours with a live preview), **journal link** (`@NCM[kind:id]{label}`, `copyAccessLink` → clipboard; `registerAccessEnricher` at init renders a chip; a gone target is a dashed "not found" chip), and the existing map pin. One capture-phase document click listener opens any `[data-ncm-access]`.
- **Handout colours** — world setting `accessHandoutStyle` `{mode: match|default|custom, custom:{primary,accent,bg,text}}` (saved from the Post dialog). Pure `handoutPalette(style, sourceColours)`: match = the terminal's `buildTerminalThemePrefs` / the shard's `buildShardThemePrefs` colours, default = brand, custom = the GM's four (muted text derived). Handouts are body-mounted, so their colours live ONLY in inline `--h-*` properties (`paletteStyle`); fonts fall back hard. CSS `styles/components/access-points.css` (`ncm-ap-card*`, `ncm-ap-chip`, `ncm-ap-dlg*`); every icon-only box has `i { margin:0 }` so glyphs centre.
- **Right-click = "Open · Configure · Terminal options ▸ / Shard options ▸"** (dev-picked layout; first labelled "Night City", renamed the same day as too vague). Foundry v12's directory menu has no submenus, so the ▸ row opens NCM's own `.ncm-ctx-menu` beside it; the inventory row menu does the same (`more: true` entries open a second menu at the row). Both build from ONE `ShardSheetOverride._nightCityEntries(item, actor)` = **ACCESS** (`accessMenuEntries`: Place on this scene [terminal, GM] · Add to hotbar · Post to chat… · Copy journal link [GM]) · **ITEM** (Program Deck, Build a program / Encode more onto it, Rename…, Edit item sheet) · Remove. `_showContextMenu(ev, entries, {title, icon})` gained `{head}` rows, `bad` rows, a title line and on-screen clamping; its colours are tokens now. Admin → Terminals / Shards details have an **Access** button (`tabAct access` → `showAccessMenu(item, button)`, wears the opener's admin preset).
- **The Post dialog is the admin vocabulary** (dev: "dialogs could look nicer like our admin panel"): pure `postDialogHtml` (the thing as a header row in its own colours, banded sections, a segmented Who sees it [Everyone / Only GMs / One player + select], three colour cards each with a swatch strip, the custom pickers, a framed preview). **Every GM dialog got the panel chrome** (admin-theme.css "Admin dialogs, round 2"): canvas header with an act underline, display-face uppercase title, pane-alt body, 30px controls with an act focus ring, a ruled button strip, ONE filled primary (Foundry's `.default` button), a destructive default (data-button delete/remove/purge/wipe/reset/burn) filled red. Dialogs with bespoke chrome are excluded by class (escalate, breach, burn-confirm, link-actor, the picker, time config, role manager). Handout text colours carry `!important` — the admin dialog's `color:inherit` rule otherwise turned the preview card dark on clean-light. Probe `dev/mockups/admin-dialog-probe.html?t=&mode=custom&who=one`.
- **Map pins right-click too** — Open terminal · Configure · Terminal options ▸ (minus "Place on this scene") · THIS PIN: Edit pin… / Remove this pin (GM). **It rides the board's DOM `contextmenu` event, never a wrap of `Note#_onClickRight`**: `TerminalScenePins._onBoardContextMenu` (capture listener on document, `#board` targets only) fires AFTER the button is released, skips a release that travelled >6 px from the right-`pointerdown` (a pan), and hit-tests the terminal pins under the cursor (`_pinAt`: `canvas.canvasCoordinatesFromClient`, radius iconSize/2+6). Two earlier builds wrapped `_onClickRight` and failed live: skipping Foundry's handler left the note's right-drag unfinished, so the map AND the menu followed the mouse; and Foundry binds a note's handlers when it is drawn, so after a reload the wrap never reached existing pins. **Never wrap a placeable's click handlers for a menu — read the DOM event after release.** Sub-menu positions are measured on `pointerdown` (`ShardSheetOverride._subMenuAt`, cursor fallback) — by `click` the menu is detached and measures 0,0. Players get it too (DOM event), double-click still opens.
- **THEMED DIALOGS — `.ncm-themed-dialog` (round 2, same day; dev: "the dialogs look super funky… post to chat is hugged up against the top… Remove chip data should match").** Two real causes, neither visible in the probe: (1) **passing `options.classes` to a Dialog REPLACES Foundry's `['dialog']`** (the admin wrapper only appends its own class), so the Post dialog lost Foundry's dialog layout — the probe frames always carried `dialog`, which hid it. **Every themed dialog opens with `THEMED_DIALOG_CLASSES` = ['dialog', 'ncm-themed-dialog']** (scripts/utils/dialogBody.js); grep for a `classes: [` without `'dialog'` in a Dialog call. (2) The preview was injected in `render` AFTER Foundry had placed the window, so it grew down from too high — the first paint now includes it, and `refit()` re-centres (`setPosition`) whenever the content grows. One look for the small dialogs: `styles/components/ncm-dialog.css` (tokens with an admin→player→hex fallback chain, so a GM gets the admin preset and a player THEIR theme; content padding 0, a header band naming the thing, a full-width button strip, ONE filled default, `.ncm-dlg-bad` = filled red; selectors doubled `.ncm-themed-dialog.ncm-themed-dialog` because admin-theme.css's `.default { color: secondary }` made the filled button's label vanish) + pure `dialogBodyHtml({tone, icon|img, title, sub, kind, lead (**bold**), goes[], stays[], field, hint})` + `themedConfirm` / `themedPrompt` in adminTheme.js (admin or player wrapper by isGM). Used by Post to chat, Rename shard, Remove shard data, Remove terminal data, Remove pin. The generic admin "round 2" rules exclude `.ncm-themed-dialog`. Probe `dev/mockups/admin-dialog-probe.html?t=&as=player`.
- **Step 2 — Admin → WORLD → Access points tab (built same day)** — `tabs/AccessTab.js` (gathers) + pure `tabs/accessModel.js` (rules) + `tab-access.hbs` (`TEMPLATES.ADMIN_TAB_ACCESS`) + `admin-panel/tab-access.css` (`ncm-acc2-*`); context `acc2`, built only while the tab is open; keys 9 = Access, **0 = Tools** now. Gathered: **things** = every terminal + shard (world AND actor-owned; archive copies skipped; `playerHeld` when a player-owned actor carries it), **points** = NCM pins on every scene (position as % of the scene rectangle with padding removed; reach ring from `scenePin.range`), NCM hotbar macros (flag `access`, which users' slots hold them), `@NCM` links in every text journal page, posted chat cards (who they went to + when); any ref a point names that is not a listed thing (an inbox, a message, something deleted) becomes a thing, `found:false` when gone. **Map** = scene-folder shelves (only scenes that have points, plus the viewed scene) with mini-maps on the scene's own thumbnail, an "Off the map" shelf, a desk with the big map + numbered points (Go to it / Open / Edit pin / Remove) and **Place…** (switches to the scene, picks a terminal, `placeAtViewCenter`). **By thing** = groups by kind or holder, chips per way in, "in X's gear", "not reachable anywhere"; the desk lists every way in + **Make access** (`showAccessMenu`). **One toolbar shape for both views** (dev: switching views "looks a bit off and wonky"): the Map | By thing switch + Pop out sit in the page head (`.ncm-tabhd__end`); the toolbar is search · Show · **Group (always present — Map has only "Scene folder")** · Sort · **New**, never wraps at 820, selects have fixed widths. Stats strip = filters with short labels + tooltips: All / On maps / Off the map / Hidden / Broken / **Not reachable** (jumps to By thing). Standing fold rule; hovering a row lights its dot. Live: re-renders on note / macro / user (hotbar) / chat / journal-page hooks. Removing: a pin → the themed Remove pin; a macro / chat card → themed delete; a journal link → opens the page (links live in the page text). Sim `dev/tools/sims/test-access-tab.mjs` (94: rules, the REAL tab over a mocked world, ops ↔ template, styled classes, tokens only, real Handlebars render; `NCM_DUMP` writes acc-map/acc-thing.html). **Not live-tested.** Networks join as a kind later (dev 2026-10-29: a node switches its network off on ITS scene only; one network may have several nodes).
- **Step 3 — placing on a scene (built same day).** **Place on a scene…** (the ACCESS menu entry, now for terminals AND shards, GM; Admin → Access points → Place… presets the scene) opens `openPlaceDialog(item, {sceneId})` (AccessPoints.js; themed, `ncm-plc-*` in ncm-dialog.css; remembers the last choices for the session): Scene (+ Change → the scene picker) · Where (middle of my view / click on the map) · Way in (a pin / a step-on area / both) · Pin reach (squares, 0 = anywhere) or Area size (1–4 squares) · Players see it (right away / hidden until I reveal it), with a live hint. Pure rules in utils/accessPoints.js: `sanitizePlacement`, `placementDialogHtml`, `placementHint`, `areaRect`, `accessRegionScript`. `TerminalScenePins.placeAccess(item, place)` views the scene, gets the point (`_pickPoint`: ONE capture-phase left click on #board → `canvasCoordinatesFromClient`, Esc / right-click cancels, crosshair cursor) and creates:
  - **a pin** (`_createPin`): flags `accessKind` / `accessUuid` / `accessHidden` / optional `accessRange` (+ the old `terminalItemId/Uuid` for terminals, so every older reader still works); `isAccessPin(doc)` is the test everywhere (activation, the right-click hit test, the Access tab). A shard pin opens the shard. The range gate reads the pin's own `accessRange` first, else the terminal's Network-tab setting.
  - **a step-on area** (`_createArea`): a Region (rectangle, `visibility: LAYER`, flag `access`) with ONE `executeScript` behaviour on `tokenEnter`. **Region events are broadcast to every client**, so the script runs everywhere and acts only when `event.user.isSelf`, never for the GM (moving NPCs must not pop terminals), and only for a token that user owns.
  - **Hidden**: a hidden PIN points its `entryId` at a GM-only journal "NCM — Hidden access points" (flag `hiddenAccessPoints`, created once, ownership NONE) — Foundry does not draw a note whose journal a player can't see — and is tinted grey; revealing re-points it at the item's own journal (`journalId` flag). A hidden AREA has its behaviour `disabled`. `setHidden(doc, hidden)` does both; the pin right-click menu has Reveal / Hide, the Access tab an eye per pin / area, a **Hidden** stat, hatched area rectangles on the mini + big maps, dashed hidden points, and Region hooks for live updates. A hidden way in does NOT count as reachable. Unconverting / deleting the item removes its pins AND areas (`removePinsFor`, now also on `-=isDataShard`). Sim `dev/tools/sims/test-access-place.mjs` (33: rules, the script parses + its guards, the REAL placeAccess / setHidden over a mocked scene). Probe `dev/mockups/admin-dialog-probe.html?how=both|pin|area&hid=1`. **Not live-tested** — in Foundry check: the Region behaviour fires for a player's token (and not the GM's), a hidden pin is really invisible to a player, click-on-the-map placement.
- **Step 4 = the macro catch-all, NOT a Monk's Active Tiles integration** (dev's call, same day: "generate macro and you can copy and paste that — anything that has macro access can open it"). The ACCESS menu (GM) gained **Copy macro code** (`copyAccessMacro` → clipboard; an http world without clipboard access gets a dialog with the code selected) and **Make a macro** (`createAccessMacro(ref, {assign:false})` — a world macro in "NCM — Access points", not put on the hotbar, for tools that pick a macro by name). Both carry `accessMacroSnippet(ref, name)`: a header naming what it opens + the warning that it opens for whoever RUNS it, so a trigger module must run it as the triggering player (Monk's Active Tiles, Item Piles, Regions, Stream Deck…), then the guarded `openAccess` call. Made hotbar macros carry the same header.
- **Still to build:** network nodes as a kind (a node switches its network off on ITS scene; one network may have several nodes).

## Architecture: NETWORK NODES — networks as access points (2026-10-29, all five steps built)

Design + decisions: `dev/design/NETWORK-NODES.md` (rounds 1–2, the console-ticker note, counterplay). Mocks: `dev/mockups/network-nodes-mock.html` (round 1, record), `network-nodes-v2-mock.html` (console / session / rules / scale / topology / timeline), **`network-nodes-jackin-mock.html`** (the APPROVED jack-in: a three-ring lock, one ring per layer clicks into a slot, the last ring is the roll with a stall + heartbeat, IN splits the lock and blooms the network, MISS grinds red + ICE; cyberpunk pass = hex rain, radar sweep, ring text, yellow hazard accents, chromatic split + glitch on every click; **tailor it to the content**: rings = the node's real layers, text from the node/network, the bloom = the network's real nodes). Build order: ① nodes + the scene rule (BUILT) → ② the node console (BUILT) → ③ Jacking in rules + the breach (BUILT) → ④ session actions (BUILT) → ⑤ infection (BUILT).
- **A node is a physical thing on a map that carries a network into a place** (router / relay / mast / junction / antenna). Stored in the hidden world setting **`networkNodes`** `{ [nodeId]: node }` (`{id, networkId, name, kind, sceneId, place, state: on|off|jammed|damaged, jammedUntil (game ms), hidden, discoverable, alert 0..3, showTraffic, flavour[], security, override, log[]}`). Pure rules **`scripts/utils/networkNodes.js`**: `sanitizeNode`, `nodeStateAt` (a jam whose time passed reads as on at once), `sceneVerdict` / `cutNetworks` / `carriedNetworks`, `statePatch`, `appendLog` (cap 40), `nodeHex` / `nodeFirmware` (display flavour off the id), `expiredJams`.
- **The scene rule (v1, scene-level):** a scene with nodes for a network gets that network only while ONE of them is up; an up node also CARRIES its network onto a scene it was never wired to; a scene with no nodes behaves exactly as before (nodes are optional — a world without any sees no change). Enforced in ONE place, `NetworkService.getAvailableNetworks` (cut → removed, carried → added), so every selector / composer / auth path follows.
- **NetworkService node API:** `getNodes({networkId, sceneId})`, `getNode`, `saveNode` / `deleteNode` (GM), `setNodeState(id, state, {minutes, who})` (GM writes + a log line; a player relays via **`SOCKET_OPS.NETWORK_NODE_REQUEST`** → `handleNodeRequest`, one GM; no GM online → refused with a reason), `reapNodeJams` (GM, every 30 s). **Every client re-applies the rule to its own connection** (`_reevaluateNodes`, on the setting's `updateSetting` hook, `updateWorldTime` and scene change): on the cut network → moved to another that still reaches with a "signal lost" toast, or into the dead-zone path (`_nodeDead`) when nothing reaches so queued mail waits; when it comes back → returned to it ("is back"). The hook also emits `NETWORK_CHANGED {type:'nodes'}` so open windows refresh.
- **Admin:** Networks → All networks → a network's detail has a **Nodes** section: per-scene verdict pills (red = every node there down, green = n/m up), a row per node (kind icon, name, Hidden tag, hex · kind · scene link · place, the state in its meaning colour, Switch off / Jam (themed prompt, minutes) / Switch on / Edit / Remove), **Add a node** → `scripts/ui/NetworkNodes/NodeDialogs.js` (`editNodeDialog` — the placement dialog's look: name, kind, scene via the picker, where on it, Hidden / Discoverable / Show traffic, flavour lines for the console ticker; pure `nodeEditHtml` / `readNodeEdit`). Ops `nodeAdd/nodeEdit/nodeState/nodeDel/nodeScene` via `tabAct`; rows carry `netId`/`netName` (no `../` in that block). CSS `ncm-net2-node*` (tab-networks.css), `ncm-nde*` (ncm-dialog.css). The preview `admin-networks-preview.html?view=all&sel=NCPD` carries five sample nodes (`?nodes=0` hides them).
- **Live-test round (same day):** the state pill IS the on/off switch (`nodeToggle`); the dialog's kind is five equal icon tiles (`ncm-nde__kind`, the wrapped segmented row was jumbled) and the three switches are cards with a toggle (Hidden in the secret colour). `_reevaluateNodes` acts only when a signature of (scene, cut set, carried set, current network, dead state) changes — it had been re-running on every `updateWorldTime` tick of a live clock; the world-time hook now only runs while some node is jammed; AdminPanel treats `NETWORK_CHANGED {type:'nodes'}` as a refresh, never a "switched to Unknown" activity row. Kind is flavour today (icon, firmware name, later the jack-in rings) — no rules difference. Probe `admin-dialog-probe.html?nde=1`.
- **The GM is moved too, and machines follow (2026-10-29, dev: "I jammed it and I'm still on it in the messenger"):** the GM exemption added during the flicker fix is gone — the GM's own connection follows the scene like a player's (the signature guard is what stops the repeat; the GM still sends from anywhere via the composer's VIA override, and dead-zone queueing is player-only). **A terminal's uplink over a cut network is LOCAL ONLY on the viewed scene** — `TerminalService._nodeCutsUplink(networkId, wired)` inside `getUplink` (returns `{networkId:null, network:null, cutNetworkId}`); a WIRED interface rides out a jam (radio) but not a node switched off or damaged; an open terminal re-renders on the `networkNodes` setting hook (`_onNodesChange`, removed in close()). Everything else that keys on the player's connection (network-gated shards, messages, the composer) follows by itself because the player is moved off.
- **Jammed networks in the selector + Unjam (same day, dev):** a network a node is cutting on the viewed scene is its OWN row in the inbox network selector (state `jammed`, striped amber, a chip "Jammed · 18m" / "Node down" / "Node damaged" with its icon, sorted last, left out of the "N available" count; clicking it says why instead of connecting) — `sceneBlocks(all, sceneId, now)` (utils/networkNodes.js: jammed wins, then damaged, then off; until = the last jam to end) via `NetworkService.getSceneBlocks()`; `isNodeDead` lets a node-made no-signal still list the networks (the partial keys its dead-zone panel on the new context flag `selectorDead`, never `isDeadZone`). The console's switch reads `switchLabel`: GM **Unjam** on a jammed node, GM **Repair** on a damaged one; a player cannot unjam (it comes back by itself).
- **Step 2 — the node console (`scripts/ui/NetworkNodes/NodeConsoleApp.js`, `templates/network-nodes/node-console.hbs` = `TEMPLATES.NODE_CONSOLE`, `styles/components/node-console.css`, prefix `ncm-ndc-*` — `ncm-nc-` is taken by viewer keyframes).** Player-facing (player theme + the network's colour as `--nc`), one window per node (`ncm-node-<id>`), `game.nightcity.openNode(nodeId)` (registerNetworkSystem ready/112); a hidden node does not open for a player. Shows the REAL node: name, `nodeHex`, `nodeFirmware`, state pill, a live traffic trace (busy on, spikes jammed, flat off; reduced motion = still), signal bars from the network's strength (0 when down), players viewing its scene, alert pips, box Open/Locked (`node.security.requiresAuth`). Ticker = `tickerLines` (utils/networkNodes.js): the node's log newest first (never a wiped line), the GM's flavour lines, packet chatter in the network's naming when Show traffic is on, "no carrier" off, noise jammed. Actions: **Connect** (only on the node's scene, while it is up and reaches — through `game.nightcity.connectToNetwork`, login dialog included; shows "Connected" when you are on it), **Switch off / on** (a player only when the box is NOT locked — a locked box says "jack in to get at it", step 3; damaged / jammed say why), GM **Edit** / **Jam**. A state change replays a short glitch. Re-renders on NETWORK_CHANGED / CONNECTED / DISCONNECTED.
- **Nodes are an access-point kind** (`node`, ref id = the node id; journal links `@NCM[node:…]`): `openAccess` → `openNode`; `describeAccess` (name, network · scene, the kind's pin icon `assets/icons/nodes/<kind>.svg`, the network colour as the handout palette). **Pins / step-on areas:** `TerminalScenePins.placeNode(nodeId, place)` (the placement dialog via `openNodePlaceDialog`, the node's scene preselected — picking another scene MOVES the node, and the Hidden choice becomes the node's), pin flags `accessKind:'node', accessNode, accessHidden, accessRange`, a visible pin points at the OBSERVER journal "NCM — Network nodes" (flag `networkNodesJournal`), a hidden one at the GM-only hidden journal; activation opens the console (reach = the pin's own range); the pin right-click menu: Open the node, GM Switch on/off + Open in Admin → Networks + the This-pin rows. `setNodeHidden` (the node's Hidden switch hides/reveals all its pins and areas), `removePinsForNode` (Remove node takes them with it). Admin node rows gained Open console / Place on the map / Ways in (`nodeAccessEntries` → `showEntriesMenu`, the generic menu `showAccessMenu` now uses). The Access points tab lists nodes as things (kind `node`, "Network nodes" in Show) and their pins.
- **Step 3 — JACKING IN.** Rules = the pure `scripts/utils/jackIn.js` + the hidden world setting **`jackInRules`** (only what the GM changed; `resolveJackIn` layers it over `JACK_DEFAULTS`, sanitizes, never throws): skill (Interface) + jack-in DV (15; a node may override it — `node.override.dv`, `jackInDV`), who decides (inherit Netrunning resolution / roll / roll + flag / GM decides), the gate (operator-gate shape, `canOperate(…, {standalone:true, label:'Jacking in requires'})`), the three skill DIALS each switchable — **Unlocks** (min rank per action), **Edge** (−1 DV per rank above an action's min, capped), **Moves** (rank table + 1 for beating the DV by a margin; `movesFor`) — the action table `JACK_ACTIONS` (switch · jam · tap · backdoor · insert · sweep · wipe: DV / min rank / minutes / costs a move / on; `actionView`), failure (trace + heat severity, raise the node's alert, tries → lockout via SecurityService key `jackKey(nodeId)` = `node::<id>`), and a Discovery placeholder. **Only `JACK_BUILT` (switch, jam, wipe) appear in a session**; tap / backdoor / insert / sweep can be configured now and arrive with steps 4–5 (tagged "later" on the card). GM card: **Admin → Security → Hacking rules → Jacking in** (`SecurityTab._jackContext` / `_saveJack` / `jackReset`, fold key `jack`, CSS `ncm-jkr-*`). Node dialog gained **Box locked** (`node.security.requiresAuth` — a player can then only switch it from inside a session) and **Jack-in DV**.
- **The lock = `scripts/ui/NetworkNodes/JackInLock.js` (`runJackIn(host, o)` → `{ setVerdict, finished, destroy }`) + `styles/components/jack-in.css` (`ncm-jk-*`).** The approved three-ring lock, TAILORED: one ring per REAL layer (`lockLayers`: box only when locked · firmware · the network; `ringPlan(2|3)`), ring text from the node / its firmware / the network, the network colour, the real roll parts (stat + rank) and d10, the bloom = the network's node count (`bloomPlan`), moves pips. One `paint(t)` on rAF + a wall-clock cap, click to skip, reduced motion = the end frame, `freezeAt` for probes. **The d10 cell is `.is-d10` — CPR styles a bare `.d10` class** (it lifted the box out of line, twice). Flow (`NodeConsoleApp._jackIn`): gate → lockout check → initTracking → (auto/flagged) `performCheck({deferChat:true})` then the lock, the chat card + success/fail sound at the climax (4.1 s), a flagged NET card; (gm) the lock idles on 'wait' while `requestNetAdjudication` asks, then `setVerdict`; a GM with no character goes straight in. `_afterJack`: IN → the client-side `_session = {actorId, rank, moves, used, margin}`, `recordSuccess`, a node log line, `logSuccess`; MISS → `recordFailedAttempt`, alert +1, "intrusion attempt" in the log, `fireTrace` (`action:'jack-in'`, `targetKind:'network'`, `objectId:'node:<id>'`).
- **OPERATOR chip on the console (2026-10-29, dev: "use the exact same thing as the terminal")** — under the header: portrait + name + ▾ when the user can pick among several (`getOperatorActors`), and the rank it reads ("Interface 5"); click → the shared `openOperatorPicker(this.element)`; refused mid-session. `_operatorContext` and the jack-in both read `_runner` = `resolveOperator()` (remembered pick → assigned character → the sole owned actor), the terminal's exact rule. A GM with no character shows "GM · no roll"; nobody shows a red NO OPERATOR. Also: `SkillService._readSkillData` no longer lets an "Interface" SKILL item at level 0 hide the role's real rank (the higher wins, the stat follows the role).
- **Plain words + honest switching (2026-10-29, dev: "edge isn't clear", "switch off doesn't switch it off").** No "Edge" anywhere players or GMs read it: the session note is "−2 for your rank", the rules card dials are "Rank unlocks actions" / "Rank makes it easier" / "Rank gives more moves" (internal keys unchanged). A node switched off while ANOTHER node of its network is up on the same scene leaves the network up — the console's switch hint says "Only this node — N other nodes keep X up here" (`_othersHere`) and both switch paths toast what actually happened (`_switchToast`). The test macro had put the worm-hop nodes on the test scene when the world had one scene, which is what made Switch off look broken; they now stand on another scene or none, and a rerun moves them off.
- **System sweep = the terminal's sweep modal (2026-10-29, dev).** `runSystemSweep` (scripts/ui/content/SystemSweep.js) now takes a `source` — `{ name, sub, list() → [{id, stealthDV, icon, gmTitle, gmType, quality, by, hint}], defuse(id, actor) }` — and the terminal's residents are just the default source (`terminalSource`; the old `term._sweepItem` stash is gone). The node console's `_sweep` passes a node source: infections behind their stealth DV, taps / backdoors someone else left always found, never your own; Defuse = `cleanInfection` / `removeNodeEffect`. The console window scrolls when it is taller than the screen (`.window-content` max-height + overflow); the lock and the sweep take `_lockHost` (min-height 470, scrolled to the top, `is-locking` stops the scroll).
- **The console shows what is happening (2026-10-29, dev: "needs feedback itself when jammed, tapped").** Not up → a cover (`ncm-ndc__cover is-<state>`: jammed = rolling static, off = dimmed, damaged = a red fault band) and a banner under the header ("JAMMED · back in 18 min" / "NO CARRIER" / "HARDWARE FAULT"); **status TAGS along the top** under the title bar (`_statusTags`, `ncm-ndc__tag is-<tone>`, the reason in each tooltip): Jammed · 18m / Off / Damaged, Alert: <level>, Box locked, Hidden (GM), Jacked in · N moves, Locked out · Nm; the GM also sees Tapped / Backdoor / Infected ×N with who and what in the tooltip (this replaced the separate GM flag strip); a player sees only THEIR tap / backdoor / programs, and someone else's infection as "Unstable" when symptoms are on; a player sees an infection only as symptoms (`has-symptoms`: an occasional twitch of the header + trace, when the rules' symptoms are on). Every action that lands plays a 1.6 s **flash** (Tap = a packet stream node → Agent, the terminal download's look; Jam = a spectrum saturating into noise as the carrier collapses — both 2.4 s; a jam also toasts how long it lasts) (`_flash(kind, label)` — POWER DOWN / POWER UP / JAMMED / LINK ESTABLISHED / BACKDOOR OPEN / LOG SCRUBBED / INSTALLED / REJECTED / … FAILED), mounted on the WINDOW element so the part re-render does not cut it; reduced motion skips it. **Insert a shard = the terminal's upload**: `runUploadConsole` (new options `tag`, `sub`, `goLabel`, `delivery:false`, `outcomeFor`, `deliveryHint` — terminal defaults unchanged) for the pick + compile code-stream, then `runSecurityConsole` with the clean / glitchy / rejected ladder (`verdictFor`, GM-decides choices) — the old name-picker + bare roll are gone.
- **The session** replaces the console's actions: moves pips, each action with its DV after edge (base struck through), the rank it needs when locked, "costs a move" / free; **Switch off/on** (no roll — works on a locked box), **Jam** (roll vs the edged DV through the Roll Reveal; minutes from the rules), **Wipe your log line** (roll; `NetworkService.wipeNodeLog` marks that runner's lines wiped — the ticker never shows a wiped line), **Jack out** (also on close). A failed session roll costs the move, raises the alert, traces (minor). New service writes `nodeLog` / `bumpNodeAlert` (0..3) / `wipeNodeLog`, each relayed (`NETWORK_NODE_REQUEST` ops `log` / `alert` / `wipe`).
- **Admin node row condensed + node activity (2026-10-29, dev: "a lot of buttons")** — a node row is now the state pill, **Inspect** and ONE ⋯ menu (`nodeMenu` → `NetworksTab._nodeMenu` → `showEntriesMenu`: jam / switch on, console, place, edit, the ways-in entries, remove); the infection / tap / backdoor / runner pills are folded into summary chips (`summary` getter, `.ncm-net2-node__sum`) that open the inspector, which holds the per-item controls. **Every line a write adds to a node's log is also an Activity-log row** (`NetworkService._writeNode` → `_logNodeActivity`, type `node`, the node's network + scene, at most 5 per write) and **a player changing scene is logged** (`updateUser` hook in `NetworkAccessLogService`, type `scene_move`, with the network from `playerNetworks`); the log has **Nodes** and **Scene moves** chips. **Scene cards fall back to the scene's background image** when Foundry has no thumbnail (`thumb || background.src`, pack index fields too).
- **GM node inspector (2026-10-29, dev-approved mock `dev/mockups/node-inspector-mock.html`)** — `scripts/ui/NetworkNodes/NodeInspectorApp.js` + `templates/network-nodes/node-inspector.hbs` (`TEMPLATES.NODE_INSPECTOR`) + `styles/components/node-inspector.css` (`ncm-nin-*`, tokens only). A separate ADMIN_THEMED, minimizable, resizable window per node (`ncm-node-inspect-<id>`), opened by `game.nightcity.inspectNode(id)` from Admin → Networks → a node row (magnifier, op `nodeInspect`), the console's GM **Inspect** and the pin right-click (Inspect (GM)). Controls: State (On / Off / Jammed → the jam form / Damaged), Alert −/+, **Jack-ins decided by** (per-node `node.override.resolution`, read by `jackResolution`), and the node's switches as ONE joined bar (Box locked / Hidden (also hides its pins) / Show traffic). What is affecting it, one band per kind, every row with its control: the jam (Unjam, +30 min), infections (Edit name/planter/sweep DV/note/quest, Clean, Remove everywhere for a worm, a sniffer's catch), taps/backdoors (remove), runners (Give a move back = `setRunUsed`, **Kick** = `kickRun` + an optional hit through `ICEService.applyDamage` with a custom formula (default the network's ICE) and an optional network lockout via `forceLockoutMany`, Reset), tries & lockouts on the network (`SecurityService.knownForTarget`, Clear = `clearFor`). **Add** = Infection (any program, NPC planter name, a character to receive a tap/sniffer's catch, sweep DV, worm/bomb/sniffer settings, **Quest — tell me when a player cleans it**: `_questCleaned` whispers every GM when it comes off by Clean or a Patch), Tap / Backdoor for any character, Jam (10 min … until lifted), Log line (a clue in the ticker), Damage. While an Add form is open a node update does not re-render it. The log shows wiped lines struck through. **Kicks**: the run's `kicks` counter goes up; the player's console drops a session whose run has more kicks than it joined with (flash DISCONNECTED). **The jack-in lockout IS the network's login lockout**: `jackKey(node)` = the networkId (shared with `NetworkAuthDialog`), `jackLock(rules, net)` = the network's `security.maxAttempts` / `lockoutDuration` when set, else the Jacking in rules. Sim `test-node-inspector.mjs` (75).
- **Who can jack in (2026-10-29)** — `jackInRules.gateFrom`: `anyone` (default) · `operator` (the Terminal operator access rule, the `operatorGate` setting) · `own` (`rules.gate`: CPR role chips + up to 3 skill rows at a minimum rank, or/and; nothing ticked = anyone). Pure `jackGate(rules, actor, canOperate)` in jackIn.js; a rule saved before the switch with a gated `gate` reads as `own`. Card: Security → Hacking rules → Jacking in → **Who can jack in** (`jk-gatefrom` / `jk-grole` / `jk-gs-N` + `jk-gm-N` / `jk-glogic`). The console checks it on the click AND shows the reason on a dead Jack in button. GM always can. Sims in test-jack-in.
- **Node writes are QUEUED (2026-10-29, dev: "my moves got reset")** — every NetworkService node writer (`NODE_WRITERS`, wrapped on the prototype at the end of NetworkService.js) runs one at a time. Each reads the whole `networkNodes` map and writes it back, so two overlapping writes (a session action's un-awaited move save + its log line; two relayed player requests the GM handles at once) started from the same map and the later silently undid the earlier — spent moves came back. No writer may call another (it would wait on itself). Sim §6 of `test-node-inspector.mjs`.
- **Saved sessions (2026-10-29, dev: "jack out and back in = more moves?")** — a runner's session is saved ON THE NODE (`node.runs` = `[{actorId, actorName, rank, moves, used, since, until, open}]`, one per runner, `sanitizeRuns` / `liveRun` / `withRun` / `expiredRuns`). Rule `jackInRules.session = {minutes: 60, returnAlert: true}` (Jacking in card → Sessions; 0 = every jack-in fresh). The window runs from the FIRST jack-in; inside it a new jack-in (still a roll) resumes the same moves and, by rule, raises the node's alert (`jacked back in`); every spent move saves (`_saveRun(true)`), Jack out / close save it as not open; a refresh / reopened console resumes an OPEN run with no roll (`_jackContext`; `_jackedOut` stops a deliberate jack-out resuming). `NetworkService.saveRun` (keeps since/until) / `clearRun` (relayed ops `run` / `unRun`), the reaper drops ended runs. Another character gets their own moves. Admin node rows list saved sessions (moves left, in now, time left) with a reset (`nodeRunDel`). The Jacked-in tag's tooltip says when the session ends. A GM with no character is never saved.
- **Step 4 — Tap / Backdoor / System sweep** (`JACK_BUILT` now = everything but Insert a shard). Effects live ON THE NODE: `node.taps` / `node.backdoors` = `[{id, actorId, actorName, at, until}]` (until 0 = until removed; `sanitizeEffects`, `activeEffects`, `withEffect` — one per runner per node, `expiredEffects` for the reaper; `NODE_EFFECTS`). `NetworkService.addNodeEffect` / `removeNodeEffect` (relayed ops `effect` / `unEffect`; removal logs who). **Tap:** `MessageService._deliverMessage` (GM) calls `networkService.tapDelivery(recipientData)` after the terminal mirror: every runner with a live tap on an UP node of the network the mail went over (`data.network` = the SENDER's network) **that stands on the scene the sender was on** (`data.sentFromScene`, stamped in `_stampOutgoing` from the sender's viewed scene, delivery-time only — a node hears what passes through it, not the whole city; mail with no scene is never heard; the same rule holds for tap and sniffer infections) gets a copy in their inbox's EXFIL folder (`metadata.recovered`, recoveredFrom "TAP · <node>"), own id, eddies 0 / eddies content claimed, never the sender or recipient themselves, a fan-out copied once (`_tapSeen` key = subject|from|timestamp), anonymous mail stays masked (the recipient-facing clone). **Backdoor:** `hasBackdoor(networkId)` — a live backdoor on any node of the network for a character this user OWNS — and **every auth check now goes through `_authOk`** (`isAuthenticated`, `canAccessNetwork`, `switchNetwork`, the scene-change auto-switch), so the login is simply skipped. **System sweep:** lists what SOMEONE ELSE left on the node (a player sees "unknown process", the GM the name) into `_session.found`; each has a free **Remove**. The session marks "Your tap is listening" / "Your backdoor is open". The reaper (`reapNodeJams`) also drops expired taps / backdoors. Admin node rows show live taps / backdoors as violet pills with time left and ✕ (`nodeFxDel`; each effect carries its `nodeId` — no `../` in that block).
- **Step 5 — INSERT A SHARD + INFECTION.** Five network programs in `PROGRAM_EFFECTS` with **mode `node`** (`nodeWorm` · `nodeTap` · `nodeBomb` · `nodeSniffer` · `nodePatch` — the mode keeps them out of every terminal / payload picker; `nodeProgramKind('nodeWorm') → 'worm'`). **Authored in the shard composer's Uploadable section** on a malware / payload entry: "When pushed, it…" lists them as "Network node · …"; their settings (`nodeEvery` / `nodeMax` / `nodeCarry` for a Worm, `nodeDelay` for a Bomb, `nodeCap` for a Sniffer) show only for the picked verb by CSS `:has()` (no re-render) and save as `contentData.uploadParams`; Run DV / skill = the insert roll. **Insert a shard** (session, `_insertProgram`): the character's carried shards → uploadable entries with a node verb (`_carriedNodePrograms`; picker when several) → `performCheck` vs the PROGRAM's DV through the Roll Reveal → the upload ladder (`forgeOutcome`: clean / sloppy / rejected; rejected = nothing installs + alert + trace) → `forgeQualityFromMargin` → `stealthDVFromQuality` → `NetworkService.insertProgram`. **Infections live on the node** (`node.infections`, `sanitizeInfections`, `makeInfection`): `{id, family, kind, name, actorId, quality, sloppy, stealthDV, every, nextAt, maxNodes, carry, fireAt, cap, loot}` — `family` ties a worm's copies. Worm: the GM tick (`tickInfections`, chained after `reapNodeJams` every 30 s) hops it to another node of the same network (other scenes first — `wormTarget`), carrying its tap / bomb / sniffer, up to `maxNodes`; a sloppy worm spreads half as fast. Bomb: at `fireAt` every node of its family is switched off ("BLACKOUT") and the bombs are spent. Tap infection: `tapDelivery` copies mail to the planter (sloppy = subject only, body garbled). Sniffer: `tapDelivery` records into `loot` (cap, newest kept, each mail once); **Collect** sends it to the planter's EXFIL folder ("SNIFFER · <node>") and empties it. Patch: cleans every infection on the node. **Counterplay:** System sweep finds infections whose `stealthDV` ≤ the sweep roll (`sweepFinds`; never your own; a GM with no character sees all; a player sees "unknown author"), **Remove** = `cleanInfection` on THAT node (a worm takes what it carried there with it); the planter's session lists "Your programs here" with **Recall** (`recallFamily`, every node) and **Collect**; symptoms (the ticker's checksum / resent lines, never the name — `symptomLines`) when the rules' `infection.symptoms` is on (Jacking in card → Infections; the GM always sees them). Admin node rows: infections as pulsing violet pills (detail tooltip: nodes reached, next hop, fuse, catch) with 🧹 clean this node (`nodeInfClean`) and ✕ remove everywhere (`nodeInfRecall`). Relayed ops `infect` / `clean` / `recall` / `collect`. Sim `dev/tools/sims/test-infections.mjs` (58). **Test seed:** `dev/tools/test-network-nodes-setup.macro.js` (GM Script macro — the "NCM Test Mesh" network, three nodes (one pinned on the current scene with a locked box), and a "Network Toolkit (TEST)" shard with one of every network program on a picked character; whispers a what-to-try list). **Not built:** Ransom, Spoofed credentials, chaining beyond a worm's carry, the shard's fate (stays plugged / wipes itself / bricks), the Encoder's program branch for network programs (author them in the shard composer), Security → Machines listing infections, the infection timeline + topology views.
- Sims: `dev/tools/sims/test-jack-in.mjs` (110: the rules, the lock's plans, the service writes + relay, the REAL console session view + a real Handlebars render, the card wiring incl. every field `_saveJack` reads). Probe `dev/mockups/jack-in-probe.html?kind=in|miss|wait&at=<s>&box=0` runs the REAL lock module.
- Probe `dev/mockups/node-console-probe.html?ctx=<dumps>&still=1` renders the real template + stylesheet over the real context (`NCM_DUMP=<dir> node dev/tools/sims/test-network-nodes.mjs` writes node-console-on/locked/off/gm.json).
- Sim `dev/tools/sims/test-network-nodes.mjs` (107: the rules, the REAL service over a mocked world incl. move-off / come-back / jam + reaper / the player relay, the dialog round-trip, wiring). **Not live-tested.** Not yet: gear bonuses on the jack-in roll, ICE on a miss, the Nodes view at scale + topology, the dial / atlas showing nodes, real message traffic in the ticker (comes with Tap).

## Architecture: the program-effect registry (what a program can DO)

**`PROGRAM_EFFECTS` in `ContentRenderers.js` is the ONE source of truth for every effect a payload executes, a malware deploys, or a resident fires.** Accessors: **`programEffectInfo(key)`** (unknown/legacy → `adjudicate`) and **`programEffectOptions({mode, host, exclude})`** for pickers.

Before it (fixed 2026-07-26) the catalog was duplicated across **five** places that disagreed — the payload metadata map, a *second* hardcoded option list in `DataShardComposer._plContext`, the upload verb list, `_EXEC_DESC` in `TerminalViewerApp`, and the two appliers — and **7 of the 11 verbs had no label anywhere** (`wipe`/`corrupt`/`forgeAccount`/`plant`/`keylog`/`deployICE`/`logicBomb`), which is exactly why they never reached a picker and why **malware could not be authored with an ability at all**. `PAYLOAD_EFFECTS`, `payloadEffectInfo` and `_EXEC_DESC` are gone; don't reintroduce a local list.

Per entry: `label`/`icon`/`blurb` · **`modes`** — `'instant'` (applied by `SiphonFlow._applyNativeEffect`) and/or `'triggered'` (applied by `TerminalService._fireResidentEffect`) · `host` (`'terminal'` when it only means something on a machine) · `group` (picker grouping) · **`params`** (the fields the applier reads out of `effect.params`, so an authoring UI can render them — previously `effect.params` had NO authoring path anywhere).

- **The two appliers are the authority; the registry mirrors them.** A verb must never claim a `mode` whose applier has no `case` for it. The payload manifest's **`isNative` keys on `modes.includes('instant')`, NOT mere presence in the catalog** — with the catalog now holding triggered-only verbs, a presence check would let a payload claim native support it falls through to `adjudicate` on.
- **A verb whose params can't be authored yet stays OUT of its picker.** `logicBomb` is excluded from the upload verb list (`exclude: ['plant','adjudicate','logicBomb']`) because with no `inner` it installs and then no-ops (`'logic bomb had no payload'`) — a dead option is worse than no option.
- Sim: scratchpad `test-registry.mjs` (45/45) asserts **both** directions against the appliers' real case lists, including the negatives.

**Resident triggers ride the trace funnel.** `onLogin`/`onDate`/`onAction` were implemented with live reapers but only `onLogin` could ever be *produced*, and `onAction` had exactly ONE call site (exfil). Now **`TraceService._fireResidentTriggers`** (called from `recordTrace`) reaches every gated action, since they all already report there with an `action` tag + host — no per-site calls. Added **`onHeat`** (`TerminalService.checkHeatResidents`, threshold `trigger.atLeast`) off the heat that funnel already tracks. Rules: **`onAction` fires on SUCCESS only** (a failed attempt is the trace's business, not the bomb's); **`'exfil'` is deliberately EXCLUDED from the funnel path** because its own `EVENTS.TERMINAL_EXFIL` listener owns it *and* catches ungated exfils that never reach the funnel — running both would propose the same resident twice. `SecurityTab`'s resident rows only label `onDate`/`onLogin`; the two new kinds fall through to the raw kind string (cosmetic, unfixed).

## Architecture: embedded media (audio/video)

`resolveMediaEmbed(rawUrl, mediaType)` in `helpers.js` is the **single** source resolver, shared by shards and normal messages. It returns `{ kind: 'none'|'native'|'embed', src, isVideo }`: YouTube/Vimeo links → `embed` (iframe, `src` rebuilt from the parsed video ID — never raw user HTML); direct file/URL → `native` (`<video>`/`<audio>`, audio-vs-video inferred from extension, else `mediaType`); non-`http(s)` schemes (`javascript:`/`data:`) → `none`. Two surfaces:
- **Shard AV Log** content type — `mediaUrl` field added to the composer (`DataShardComposer`, with a `browseMedia` FilePicker) and the player wizard. `ItemInboxApp._enrichEntry` resolves it; `item-inbox.hbs` renders player-or-waveform.
- **Normal messages** — a new media **attachment** (`{ isMedia: true, mediaUrl, mediaType }`) via the composer's "Embed Audio/Video" toolbar action (`_onAttachMedia`). Reuses the existing `attachments[]` persistence (no repo/service change). `MessageViewerApp` splits `isMedia` attachments out of the cards/encrypted-classifier and `_buildMediaEmbeds` renders inline players in `message-detail.hbs` (normal/unlocked branch only — locked/encrypted messages don't leak the player).

## Architecture: sound feedback

All audio goes through `SoundService.play(id, {volume,loop})` (IDs in `SOUND_PATHS`), which gates on the `soundEnabled` pref and scales by `soundVolume` — that one NCM slider governs **everything** below.

**UI interaction sounds are centralized in `BaseApplication`** (every NCM window extends it) — do **not** sprinkle `play('click')` in handlers (that drifted inconsistent; the manual calls were removed). Wired once via `_wireUiSounds()`: a document-level **capture** listener with a `this.element.contains()` containment guard (element-level listeners are unreliable in Foundry), torn down in `close()`. So only the window containing the event plays, and capture means a handler's `stopPropagation` can't swallow it. Coverage: `click` on actionable controls (`button, [data-action], a[href], [role="button"], .ncm-clickable`), `open` (first render), `close`. `EmailSetupFlow` extends raw `ApplicationV2` and is **not** covered.

**The GM can replace any sound — Admin → Tools → Sounds (2026-10-29).** `scripts/utils/soundCatalog.js` (pure) is the catalogue: `SOUND_INFO` gives every `SOUND_PATHS` id a name, a group and plain words for where it plays (keystroke / queue-flush / terminal-hum are honestly "not used yet"; the sim checks with git grep), and `soundSource(id, overrides)` is the ONE answer to what plays. World setting `soundOverrides` = `{ [id]: { src, volume 0.1–2, off } }` (sanitized: audio files only, no script/data urls). **Every play path reads it** — `SoundService.source(id)` feeds `play`, the varied Web Audio player (clicks, terminal sound sets, which remap onto ids and so pick up replacements too) and the preload; an `updateSetting` hook drops the cache on every client. `SoundBoardApp` (`scripts/ui/Sounds/`, `templates/sounds/sound-board.hbs` = `TEMPLATES.SOUND_BOARD`, `styles/components/sound-board.css` `ncm-sb-*`; ADMIN_THEMED, minimizable; `game.nightcity.openSoundBoard()`): grouped rows with ▶ (`preview`, plays even with sounds off), Browse (Foundry's audio FilePicker, which uploads), a per-sound volume %, Off, Reset; search + Changed filter, Export / Import JSON (`{ncmSounds:1, sounds}`, the files themselves are not included), Reset all.
